Pointwise criteria of p p -adic local systems
Haoyang Guo, Ziquan Yang

Abstract
. Given a Z p \mathbb{Z}_{p} -linear local system over a smooth rigid space, we show that it is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to any smooth (resp. semi-stable) integral model if and only if its restrictions at many classical points are crystalline (resp. semi-stable) representations. To this end, we introduce a crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor, and give several applications, including a semi-stable comparison theorem in the relative setting.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Pointwise criteria

Let
K
be a complete discretely valued
p
-adic field of characteristic
0
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and perfect residue field
k
, and let
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
be the absolute Galois group of
K
.
Given a continuous representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
on a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space
V
, a fundamental question in arithmetic geometry is to characterize those representations which “arise from geometry”; namely those which are subquotients of the
p
-adic étale cohomology of algebraic varieties
Y
over the field
K
.
Celebrated results in
p
-adic Hodge theory, which used to be conjectures of Fontaine and Fontaine–Jannsen, state that
V
is a
de Rham
representation if it arises from geometry, and is moreover
crystalline
(resp.
semi-stable
) if the variety
Y
admits
good reduction
(resp.
semi-stable reduction
), i.e.,
Y
extends to a smooth (resp. semi-stable) scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

It is then natural to consider these notions in the relative setting, in which a
p
-adic
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
-representation is generalized by a
p
-adic local system
over a smooth rigid space
X_{\eta}
over
K
.
Moreover, the notions of being de Rham, crystalline, or semi-stable make sense for
p
-adic local systems as well. There are two fundamental results which tell us when a
p
-adic local system is de Rham:

(I)

In his foundational work
[
Sch13a
]
, Scholze established a relative de Rham comparison isomorphism, which implies that given a family of smooth proper rigid varieties
f_{\eta}:Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, the local system
R^{i}f_{\eta,*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
over
X_{\eta}
is de Rham.

(II)

Liu–Zhu’s celebrated rigidity theorem
[
LZ17
]
tells us that a
p
-adic local system on a connected rigid space
X_{\eta}
is de Rham if and only if its restriction at any single classical point
1
1

1

By a
classical point
of a rigid space, we mean a point that corresponds to a map
{\mathrm{Spa}}(K^{\prime})\to X_{\eta}
, where
K^{\prime}/K
is a finite extension of
K
and in particular has perfect residue field.
When
X_{\eta}
is an algebraic variety over
K
, a classical point is the same as a closed point.

x_{\eta}
is de Rham—this is a pointwise criterion for de Rham local systems.

In this article, we aim to investigate how to extend Scholze and Liu–Zhu’s results to crystalline and semistable local systems.
It is well known that the naive analogue of Liu–Zhu’s rigidity theorem is false for either of the two notions (
[
LZ17
, Rmk 1.4]
).
In fact, for a local system
T
over a rigid space
X_{\eta}
with good reduction, even if it
is crystalline at a set of points whose reductions are Zariski
dense on the special fiber,
T
may still not be crystalline or semistable (cf.
Example

7.8
).
However, the situation becomes different if we consider more points.
In particular, we have the following.

Theorem 1.1

(Pointwise Criteria, cf.
Theorem

7.2
)
.

Let
X_{\eta}
be a rigid space over
K
that has a smooth (resp. semi-stable)
p
-adic integral model
X
.
Let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
local system on
X_{\eta}
.

Then
T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
X
if and only if the Galois representation associated to the restriction of
T
at each classical point is crystalline (resp. semi-stable).

Along the way, we also gave an analogous pointwise criterion for unramified
\ell
-adic local systems, see
Theorem

6.31
.

Remark 1.2

(Effective version)
.

The above results are stated in their cleanest forms.
In fact, it suffices to test crystallinity or semi-stability at any
effective
subset
\mathcal{C}
of classical points in
X_{\eta}
, rather than at all classical points.
Roughly speaking, a subset
\mathcal{C}
is effective if the inertia groups of these points topologically generate the global inertia group
\ker(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta})\to\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X_{s}))
, and the same property is preserved after replacing
X_{\eta}
by certain types of étale morphisms.
For example, given a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and any Zariski dense subset of closed points in the special fiber
X_{s}
, the set of classical points
\mathrm{red}^{-1}(\mathcal{C}_{s})
is effective in
X_{\eta}
, where
\mathrm{red}:X_{\eta}\to X_{s}
is the reduction map of the rigid space.
We refer the reader to
Definition

6.27
for the precise definition.
We would like to mention that it is a natural question if the set of special points form an effective subset in a Shimura variety with good reduction, which we do not have an answer.

Remark 1.3
.

One may naturally compare
Theorem

1.1
with a result of Shimizu
[
Shi22
, Thm 1.6]
, where the latter implies that if the restriction of the
p
-adic local system
T
at a classical point
x_{\eta}
is potentially crystalline or semistable, then the same holds in a small analytic neighborhood of
x_{\eta}
.
As far as the authors are aware of, our result cannot be deduced from Shimizu’s.
One major reason is that the union of such open neighborhoods at all classical points is in general far from being a cover of the entire
X_{\eta}
in an appropriate sense of rigid analytic geometry.

Here we also remark on a pleasant consequence of
Theorem

1.1
.
Unlike that of de Rham local systems, the definitions of crystalline and semistable local systems a priori make references to the choice of a smooth or semi-stable integral model of
X_{\eta}
, despite the fact the local systems themselves are objects defined on
X_{\eta}
.
However, the property of being crystalline or semi-stable at every classical point clearly only depends on the generic fiber.
As a consequence, we see the crystallinity and the semi-stability are independent of the choice of models (see also
[
DLMS24
, Cor. 5.5]
). In fact, along the same lines one deduces a stronger consequence. Namely, both the crystallinity and the semi-stability are preserved under pullback along a map of rigid spaces, even if the map does not extend integrally.

Corollary 1.4

(Pullback preservation)
.

Let
f_{\eta}:X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
be a map of two rigid spaces, and assume both
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
and
X_{\eta}
admit good (resp. semi-stable) reductions.
Let
T
be a crystalline (resp. semi-stable) local system over
X_{\eta}
.
Then
f_{\eta}^{-1}T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable).

Another immediate application is that Fontaine’s conjecture in
[
Fon97
]
, originally about crystalline and semi-stable representations and was proved by Liu
[
Liu07
]
, extends naturally to the relative setting.
Without much additional effort, the latter in particular implies that the locus of crystalline (resp. semi-stable) local systems with fixed Hodge–Tate weights defines a closed subscheme in the universal deformation scheme, generalizing the fundamental work of Kisin (
[
Kis08
]
) and Liu (
[
Liu07
]
) to the relative setting.

Corollary 1.5

(Fontaine’s conjecture in the relative setting)
.
2
2

2

Here we also kindly mention that for crystalline local systems, this was showed recently by Moon in
[
Moo24
, Thm. 1.2]
, by extending Liu’s arguments to the setting of
p
-adic valuation rings with imperfect residue field, and then using the purity result of crystalline local systems.

Let
X_{\eta}
be a rigid space over
K
that has a smooth (resp. semi-stable)
p
-adic integral model
X
.
Let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
local system on
X_{\eta}
, and let
h\in\mathbb{N}
.

Assume that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the torsion local system
T/p^{n}T
is the reduction of a crystalline (resp. semi-stable) local system
T_{n}
with Hodge–Tate weights in
[0,h]
.
Then
T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with Hodge–Tate weights in
[0,h]
.

1.2.
Relative semi-stable comparison

One of the main conceptual contributions of
Theorem

1.1
is the idea that Fontaine and Fontaine–Jannsen’s crystalline and semi-stable comparison conjectures in the relative setting should be consequences of their absolute versions, namely over a
p
-adic field with
perfect
residue field.
The crystalline version in the relative setting has recently been established by the first named author and Reinecke in
[
GR24
]
via prismatic methods.
However, despite been widely expected, the semi-stable comparison is much more technical and its relative version in full generality so far has not been justified.
In fact, there has been little detailed exposition of the notion of semi-stable local systems until the very recent work
[
DLMS24
]
, though the idea for such a notion dates back to
[
Fal89
]
and
[
AI12
]
.
Here we also mention that unlike smooth morphisms, “semi-stable morphisms” lack a standard definition.

In this paper, we use
Theorem

1.1
to provide a working prototype of semi-stable comparison theorems in the relative setting, shed light on the optimal generality for such a theorem, and provide a new strategy which simplifies the entire program to the absolute setting.

Theorem 1.6

(Semi-stability of étale cohomology sheaf, cf.
Theorem

7.9
)
.

Let
X
be a semi-stable
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with the standard log structure
M_{X}
, let
(Y,M_{Y})
be a fine and saturated
p
-adic formal log scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
f:(Y,M_{Y})\to(X,M_{X})
be a proper and log smooth morphism with Cartier type mod
\pi
reduction, and let
f_{\eta}
be the induced morphism between generic fibers.

Assume one of the following conditions is true.

(a)

The map
f
is algebrizable.

(b)

The underlying map between formal schemes of
f
is pointwise weakly semi-stable.

Then the higher direct image
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a semi-stable local system over
X_{\eta}
.

The subscript “
{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
” stands for “Kummer-étale”. The condition in
(b)
is defined below. Note that in case
(b)

M_{Y}
is in fact trivial on
Y_{\eta}
, and the underlying morphism between rigid spaces of
f_{\eta}
is smooth and proper, so we have
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Definition 1.7
.

We say that a morphism
f:Y\to X
between
p
-adic formal schemes which are flat and topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is
pointwise weakly semi-stable
if for every classical point
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
, the fiber
Y_{x}
at the integral extension
x\in X(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})
of
x_{\eta}
(
Proposition

6.1
) is weakly semi-stable (
Definition

2.5
).

Continuing with the theme in
Theorem

1.6
, one naturally asks if the
F
-isocrystal on the special fiber of the base
(X_{k},M_{X_{k}})
associated to
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is precisely the relative log crystalline cohomology of the special fiber
f_{k}
of
f
. Let us call it the “association statement”.
We give a positive answer when the base admits good reduction, by combining Scholze’s de Rham comparison theorem in
[
Sch13a
]
, the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor, and a relative Hyodo–Kato comparison theorem, where the latter two will be introduced soon.

Theorem 1.8

(Association statement)
.

In the setting of
Theorem

1.6
.
(b)
,
assume
X
is in addition smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Then the local system
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is naturally associated to the (log)
F
-isocrystal
R^{i}f_{k,{\mathrm{crys}},*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{k},M_{Y_{k}})/W}[1/p])
on
(X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, where
\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{k},M_{Y_{k}})/W}
is the structure sheaf on the log-crystalline site
((Y_{k},M_{Y_{k}})/W)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.

Here we mention that the two conditions required in
Theorem

1.6
.
(a)
and
(b)
together with the smoothness assumption on
X
in
Theorem

1.8
are only artifacts of our proof, and we expect them to be unnecessary.
We refer the reader to
Remark

7.13
for a detailed discussion.

Semi-stable families considered in
Theorem

1.6
appear naturally in practice.
For example, when we compactify moduli spaces of varieties of a fixed type (e.g., curves, abelian varieties, K3 surfaces, etc.), the boundary of the compactification is often given by semi-stable degenerations of the smooth fibers.
Then, semi-stable families naturally arise when we deform the boundary from characteristic
p
into the interior in characteristic
0
.
To illustrate the phenomenon, let us consider the moduli space of pointed stable curves.
Namely, we denote by
\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
) the moduli stack over
\mathbb{Z}
of
n
-marked smooth (resp. stable) curves of genus
g
, i.e.,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
is the Deligne-Mumford compactification
\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
. Let
\mathcal{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}\smallsetminus\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
be the boundary (equipped with a reduced substack structure).

Proposition 1.9
.

Let
S
be a semi-stable
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Suppose that
f:C\to S
is a stable curve given by a morphism
\gamma:S\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
such that the special fiber
\gamma_{k}
factors through
\mathcal{D}_{k}
but the generic fiber
\gamma_{\eta}
factors through the analytification of
\mathcal{M}_{g,n,K}
. Let
\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}:S\to C
be the marked points and let
C^{\circ}:=C\smallsetminus\cup_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}(S)
be the open complement.

Then
R^{1}f^{\circ}_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a semi-stable
p
-adic local system on
S_{\eta}
, where
f^{\circ}
is the restriction of
f
to the open curve
C^{\circ}
.

Note that since
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
is smooth over
\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
is a relative normal crossing divisor (
[
DM69
, Thm. 5.2]
), it is very easy to construct a family
\gamma
as above such that
\gamma_{k}
is a smooth cover of
\mathcal{D}_{k}
(see
Example

7.15
).

1.3.
Crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functors

To prove the pointwise criteria, we introduce crystalline analogues of the Riemann–Hilbert functor, which are compatible with the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert functor
D_{\mathrm{dR}}
studied in Liu–Zhu, and extend the functors
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
from the case of a
p
-adic field to the relative setting.
The construction of these functors is of independent interest and is, in itself, a major contribution of the article.
We assume that
X
is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
in this subsection, and let
T
be a
p
-adic local system on the rigid generic fiber
X_{\eta}
.

We first recall the simplest setting when
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is a point.
Under the assumption, a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
of rank
d
is equivalent to a continuous representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
on a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of rank
d
.
In addition, the local system
T
is crystalline (resp. the semi-stable) when the associated
\varphi
-module
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)
(resp.
(\varphi,N)
-module
D_{\mathrm{st}}(T)
) is of dimension
d
.
Here the
\varphi
-module
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)
can be recovered by
D_{\mathrm{st}}(T)
by taking the kernel of the monodromy operator
N
on the latter.

Now let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
, such that the restriction of
T
at every classical point is crystalline.
When
X
is of positive relative dimension over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, Faltings’s definition of the crystallinity (
[
Fal89
]
; cf.
Definition

4.4
) only requires the existence of some
F
-isocrystal that is
associated
to the given local system
T
.
Although this definition agrees with the preceeding one when
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, the associated
F
-isocrystals are not functorially assigned and a priori may not be unique.

In order to resolve this lack of functoriality, we introduce a higher dimensional extension of
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors.
To explain the construction, we setup the notations first: Let
K_{0}=W(k)[1/p]
be the maximal unramified subextension in
K
, and let
X_{s}
be the reduced special fiber of
X
, regarded as a smooth variety over the residue field
k
.
For a map of
p
-adic formal schemes
f:X^{\prime}\to X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we let
f_{s}:X^{\prime}_{s}\to X_{s}
be the induced map of the reduced special fibers.
We use
{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
to denote the category of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
local systems over
X_{\eta}
, and let
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
be the category of
F
-isocrystals over the crystalline site
(X_{s}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
.
In addition, we let
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
be the category of data
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}},N_{\mathcal{E}})
, where
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is an
F
-isocrystal over
(X_{s}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
, and
N_{\mathcal{E}}
is a nilpotent endomorphism of the isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
, satisfying the formula
N_{\varphi^{*}\mathcal{E}}=p\varphi^{*}N_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then there are natural functors of categories

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})\xrightarrow{\ker(N)}\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}),

where the first functor is defined by equipping an
F
-isocrystal with the zero endomorphism, and the second functor is defined by taking the kernel of the
N_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Note that the first functor is fully faithful, and the composition is the identity.

Next, we may sheafify the above notions with respect to the Zariski topology of
X_{s}
, and define a
weak isocrystal
to be a sheaf of isocrystals over
(X_{s})_{\mathrm{Zar}}
with
injective
transition morphisms (cf.
Definition

5.10
). A usual isocrystal is a weak isocrystal whose transition morphisms are all isomorphisms.
We let
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
and
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi,N}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
be the categories of weak
F
-isocrystals with additional structures that are analogous to the above.
Now we can state the main construction of the article.

Theorem 1.10

(Crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functors (
Theorem

5.20
))
.

There are natural functors
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{st}}
fitting into the following commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi,N}(X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker(N)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{st}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{crys}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}},N_{\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}})=\ker(N_{\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}}),}

satisfying the following properties:

(i)

(Rank inequalities)
The weak isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
) is of rank
\leq\mathrm{rank}(T)
, and the local system
T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
X
if and only if
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T})
is constant and is equal to
\mathrm{rank}(T)
(resp.
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T})=\mathrm{rank}(T)
).

(ii)

(Injectivity of base change morphism)
Assume
f:Y\to X
is a map of
p
-adic formal schemes such that
Y
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
for a finite extension
K^{\prime}/K
.
Then there are natural injections of weak
F
-isocrystals with nilpotent endomorphisms

f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T},\quad f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T},

which are functorial with respect to
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
and satisfy the cocycle condition with respect to compositions of maps between
p
-adic formal schemes.

(iii)

(Smooth base change)
In the setting of
(ii)
, assume the map
f
is
p
-completely smooth.
Then the base change morphisms in
(ii)
are all isomorphisms.

(iv)

(Compatibility with
D_{\mathrm{dR}}
)
By evaluating at the divided power thickening
(X,X_{p=0})

3
3

3

Here the thickening is an object in the crystalline site
(X_{p=0}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
, and the evaluation makes sense thanks to Dwork’s trick; cf.
Proposition

2.12
.
,
there are functorial injections of flat connections over the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
that are compatible with pullbacks:

(1)

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}(X,X_{p=0})\otimes_{K_{0}}K\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}(X,X_{p=0})\otimes_{K_{0}}K\hookrightarrow D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T),

where
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(-)
is the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert functor of
T
defined in
[
LZ17
, Thm 3.9(v)]
.

(v)

(Point case)
When
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is a point, there are isomorphisms
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}(X,X_{p=0})\simeq D_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)
and
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}(X,X_{p=0})\simeq D_{\mathrm{st}}(T)
respecting Frobenius and nilpotent endomorphisms, such that (
1
) become the canonical injections
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K\hookrightarrow D_{\mathrm{st}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K\hookrightarrow D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
.

Here we name our functors as the
crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functors
, in light of the compatibility with
[
LZ17
]
as in
Theorem

1.10
.
(iv)
.

Remark 1.11

(Relation to Tan–Tong
[
TT19
]
)
.

When the base ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is absolutely unramified, the evaluation of our functor
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{crys}}
on
X
is identical to
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)
in
[
TT19
, Def. 3.12]
.
One of the main observations, which enables us to extend their construction to allow ramifications, is precisely the crystal interpretation of the construction.
Namely, by deformation theory, one can always find a Zariski cover
\{U_{\alpha}\}
of
X
such that each
U_{\alpha}
admits an unramified model and apply
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(T)
to the unramified models.
Although the construction depends on the choice of the unramified models, we can glue the outputs, not in the category of vector bundles (with additional structures) on rigid spaces as in
[
TT19
]
, but in the category of
F
-isocrystals on the special fiber
X_{s}
.
As a consequence, although Tan-Tong’s crystalline period sheaf
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
, which is the crystalline analogue of Scholze’s de Rham period sheaf
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
, cannot be globalized, the resulting functor
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{crys}}
can.

Remark 1.12

(Breuil’s equivalence and relative Hyodo–Kato isomorphism)
.

Although the functor
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
behaves like the classical
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functor, the cautious reader might notice that the semi-stability is usually defined via
F
-isocrystals over the log-crystalline site (cf.
[
AI12
]
,
[
DLMS24
]
).
In fact, for a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, we show in
Corollary

2.26
that there is a natural equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(X_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})\simeq\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}),

where the latter is the category of
F
-isocrystals over the log crystalline site of the special fiber
(X_{s},(0{N})^{a})
over
W
.
One of the technical ingredients is a re-interpretation of the category of
\varphi
-modules over certain non-noetherian divided power polynomial (cf.
Theorem

2.21
), which in the case of a point was shown by Breuil in
[
Bre97
, §6]
.
As an application of the latter result, we in particular extend the Hyodo–Kato isomorphism of
[
HK94
]
to the relative setting (cf.
Theorem

2.30
).

Remark 1.13

(Purity for arbitrary
p
-adic local systems)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a weak
F
-isocrystal over a smooth connected variety
Z
in characteristic
p
.
There is a largest open subvariety
U_{\mathcal{E}}
of
Z
, which we define as the
pure locus
of
\mathcal{E}
, such that the restriction of
\mathcal{E}
on the open subvariety
U_{\mathcal{E}}\subset Z
is an actual
F
-isocrystal (cf.
Definition

5.11
).
For example, when
\mathcal{E}
is an
F
-isocrystal over
Z
, its pure locus is
Z
itself; and if
\mathcal{F}
is an isocrystal on an open subscheme
V
and if
i:V\to Z
is the open immersion, the pure locus of
i_{!}\mathcal{F}
is equal to
V
.
In light of the purity of crystalline and semi-stable local systems, we make the following conjecture on arbitrary
p
-adic local systems.

Conjecture 1.14
.

Let
X
be a smooth connected
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
be the Shilov point of
X_{\eta}
, and let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
.
Then we have the following equalities of integers

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}|_{U_{\mathcal{E},_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}}})=\mathrm{rank}(D_{{\mathrm{crys}},L}(T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)})),

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}|_{U_{\mathcal{E},_{{\mathrm{st}},T}}})=\mathrm{rank}(D_{{\mathrm{st}},L}(T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)})),

where
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},L}(T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)})
(resp.
D_{{\mathrm{st}},L}(T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)})
) is the
\varphi
-module (resp.
(\varphi,N)
-module) associated to the
{\mathrm{Gal}}(L)
-representation
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
.
4
4

4

The reader may look at
[
Mor14
, §2.2]
for an exposition of these functors in the imperfect residue field case.

1.4.
Descending the crystallinity

Now we explain the strategy of the proof for the pointwise criteria in
Theorem

1.1
. We let
T
be a local system of rank
d
on the Raynaud generic fiber
X_{\eta}
of a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
.
We will consider the good reduction case in the introduction; the case when
X
has semi-stable reduction follows quickly from the former, together with the recent advance of Du–Liu–Moon–Shimizu on the purity of semi-stable local systems
[
DLMS24
]
.

We start by recalling the strategy in the simplest situation, when
X_{\eta}=\mathrm{Spa}(K)
is a single point, and identify the local system
T
with its corresponding
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
-representation.
In this case, the crystallinity of
T
(with respect to
K
) can usually be checked in the following two steps:

(1)

First show that
T
is a de Rham representation, and hence semi-stable when restricted to a finite totally ramified Galois extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
.

(2)

Then show that the action of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)
on the associated
(\varphi,N)
-module
D_{{\mathrm{st}},K^{\prime}}(T)
is trivial, and the monodromy operator
N
is zero.

Here we emphasize that the second step makes an essential use of the functoriality of the construction
D_{{\mathrm{st}},K^{\prime}}(T)
with respect to
T
, so that there is a canonical
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)
-action on
D_{{\mathrm{st}},K^{\prime}}(T)
, identifying
D_{{\mathrm{st}},K}(T)
as the invariant submodule:

D_{{\mathrm{st}},K}(T)\simeq D_{{\mathrm{st}},K^{\prime}}(T)^{{\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)}.

We then return to the general setting for an arbitrary smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.
In this case, thanks to the rigidity of the de Rham local system by Liu–Zhu (
[
LZ17
]
), our pointwise assumption implies that the local system
T
is de Rham.
So by taking the restriction of
T
at the Shilov point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
, we know (the corresponding representation of)
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
is de Rham.
Moreover, thanks to the work of Morita (
[
Mor14
]
) and Ohkubo (
[
Ohk13
]
),
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
is in fact a potentially semi-stable representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L}
.
This means that there is a finite Galois cover
L^{\prime}/L
such that the restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L^{\prime}/L)}
is semi-stable.

To proceed, we want to relate the representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L^{\prime}}
with the local system over the original
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.
We first notice that by approximation arguments in
Proposition

6.16
and
Theorem

6.17
, the finite extension
L^{\prime}/L
can be refined so that it comes from the map of Shilov points with respect to the following tower of
p
-adic formal schemes:

X^{\prime\prime}=X_{m}\xrightarrow{g_{m}}\cdots\xrightarrow{g_{1}}X_{1}=X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{g_{0}}X_{0}=X^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}X,

where

•

the map
f
is
p
-completely étale;

•

the field
K^{\prime}
is a finite Galois extension of
K
that is totally ramified, and
g_{0}
is the extension map;

•

for each
1\leq i\leq m
, the map
g_{i}:X_{i}\to X_{i-1}
is a primitive inseparable cover between two smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
(cf.
Definition

6.12
).

Here the
primitive inseparable cover
is introduced in
Definition

6.12
, and roughly means a degree
p
Galois cover of a rigid space (with good reduction) such that the induced map on the reduced special fiber is purely inseparable.
We can then spread out the semi-stability of the restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L}}
to the entire
p
-adic formal scheme
X^{\prime\prime}
, thanks to the purity result in
[
DLMS24
]
mentioned before.

Next, to show the original local system
T
is crystalline, we need to descend the semi-stability from
X_{i}
to
X_{i-1}
using the pointwise assumption.
We let
x_{\eta}\in X_{i,\eta}
be a classical point such that
y_{\eta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{i,\eta}^{-1}(x_{\eta})
is a single ramified point.
Then the relative Galois group
G_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Gal}}(X_{i,\eta}/X_{i-1,\eta})
is naturally isomorphic to the Galois group of the field extension, namely
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta}))
.
By taking the rings of integers, we in particular obtain a
G_{i}
-equivariant commutative diagram of
p
-adic formal schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(y_{\eta})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i-1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{i}\text{-equivariant}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

Now by the functoriality of the construction
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T|_{X_{i}}}
, there is a natural action of
G_{i}
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T|_{X_{i}}}
in the categories of
F
-isocrystals.
Moreover, by the base change theorem of the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor in
Theorem

1.10
.
(ii)
and by
Theorem

1.10
.
(v)
, the
G_{i}
action on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T|_{X_{i}}}
restricts to the
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta}))
action on
D_{{\mathrm{st}},K(y_{\eta})}(T|_{y_{\eta}})
.
However, by the assumption that
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is crystalline, the latter Galois action is trivial.
As a consequence, thanks to the faithfulness of the restriction functor of
F
-isocrystals along the map of special fibers
y_{s}\to X_{i,s}
(
[
Ogu84
, Thm. 4.1]
), we see the
G_{i}
action on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T|_{X_{i}}}
is also trivial.
Finally, using the triviality of
G_{i}
action, we can show that the semi-stability of
T|_{X_{i,\eta}}
implies that of
T_{X_{i-1,\eta}}
,

5
5

5

Here a technical caveat is the fact that the map of reduced special fibers
X_{i,s}\to X_{i-1,s}
is only purely inseparable in general (instead of being an isomorphism, as happened in the case of a point), so additional descent arguments for vector bundles over period sheaves are needed in order to obtain the aforementioned implication of semi-stability.

and hence the semi-stability of
T
.
Similar argument can also be used to show that the pointwise crystalline assumption will enforce the semi-stable local system
T
to be crystalline.
We refer the reader to
Theorem

7.6
and
Theorem

7.7
for details.

Remark 1.15
.

The curious reader might wonder if the pointwise criteria follows quickly from the conjectural equivalence between the de Rhamness and the potential semi-stability for
p
-adic local systems.
In fact, even assuming the conjecture, we are not aware of an argument without shrinking and descending as described above.
This is essentially due to the complexity of semi-stable reductions of rigid spaces: given a finite étale cover
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, it is not known if
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
admits a global semi-stable reduction without further refinement by open and (non-proper) étale morphisms.

1.5.
Outline

We begin with the basics of log-crystalline sites in
Section

2
, where we provide various structural results for (log)
F
-isocrystal over a smooth finite type
k
-scheme with the hollow log structure, and prove the Breuil’s equivalence theorem and the Hyodo–Kato isomorphism in the relative setting.
Then we collect the constructions and the properties for the horizontal period sheaves and the period sheaves with connections in
Section

3
.
In particular, we introduce the semi-stable period sheaves for a rigid space with good reduction.

After the above preparations, we proceed in
Section

4
to introduce the crystalline and the semi-stable local systems.
Our first definition in
Section

4.1
is in the spirit of Faltings and has no regularity assumption on the reduction of the rigid space.
In the special case when the rigid space admits a smooth affine reduction with a prescribed unramified model, we construct the
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors from
p
-adic local systems to
F
-isocrystals in
Section

4.2
, and thus obtain a local formula of the associated
F
-isocrystals.

We then study the pullback behaviors of the aforementioned constructions in
Section

5
.
We show in
Section

5.1
that the general notions of the crystallinity and the semi-stability are preserved by the pullback functor, for the generic fiber of a map of
p
-adic formal schemes
f:X\to Y
.
When the map
f
is between affine smooth
p
-adic formal schemes with unramified models, we show in
Section

5.2
that the above
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors admit natural pullback injections.
As an interlude, we introduce and study the notion of weak
F
-isocrystals in
Section

5.3
.
In
Section

5.4
, we prove that when interpreted as weak
F
-isocrystals, the
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors can be globalized at face value, yielding the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functors for general smooth
p
-adic formal schemes.

Next in
Section

6
we establish the geometric preparations for the pointwise criteria.
We show in
Section

6.5
that any finite étale cover of the Shilov point of a rigid space can spreads out to a finite tower of standard covers of rigid spaces, up to shinking to a smaller neighborhood.
We also introduce the notion of the effective set of classical points, and in particular show the
\ell
-adic pointwise criterion in
Section

6.6
.

Finally, assembling all the previous ingredients, we prove the pointwise criteria in
Section

7
, and give various applications.
We start with the primitive reductions in
Section

7.1
.
The main technical arguments are established in
Section

7.2
, where we show the descent of the crystallinity and the semi-stability.
Finally we provide several applications of the pointwise criteria in
Section

7.3
, including the semi-stability of the higher direct image along a semi-stable proper smooth map.

1.6.
