1.7.
Conventions

Given a ring
R
and an ideal
I
, we use
R^{\wedge}_{I}
to denote the
I
-adic completion of
R
.
When
A
and
B
are
R
-algebras, we use
A\widehat{\otimes}_{R}B
to denote the
I
-complete tensor product
(A\otimes_{R}B)^{\wedge}_{I}
whenever the topology is clear.
We use
R\langle x_{i}\rangle
(resp.
R\{x_{i}\}
) to denote the convergent power series ring (resp.
I
-complete divided power polynomial ring) over
R
,
namely the
I
-adic completion of the polynomial ring of variables
x_{i}
over
R
(resp. the divide power polynomial ring of variables
x_{i}
over
R
).

6
6

6

Here we warn the reader that our usage of the notation is different from the common convention in prismatic cohomology, where
R\{x_{i}\}
means the free
\delta
-algebra over a given
\delta
-ring
R
, as introduced by Bhatt–Scholze
[
BS22
]
.

To lighten the notation, we also abbreviate the notion “divided power” using the term “PD” for many places in the article.

Given a pre-log structure
(R,N_{R})
, we use
N_{R}^{a}
to denote the associated log structure on
R
.
Moreover, for a (pre-)log structure
M
, we often abuse the same symbol to denote the associated monoid when the meaning is clear.

For a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, where
K
is a
p
-adic field with perfect residue field
k
, we use
X_{p=0}
to denote the mod
p
fiber of
X
, use
X_{k}
to denote the base change
X\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
, and use
X_{s}
to denote the reduced closed scheme
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

For a scheme
Z
over a perfect field
k
in characteristic
p
, we let
Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
be its absolute crystalline site, namely the crystalline site of
Z
over
W(k)
, where the latter has the canonical divided power structure on the ideal
p
.
If
Z
admits a log structure
M_{Z}
, we let
(Z,M_{Z})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
be its absolute log-crystalline site, namely the log-crystalline of
(Z,M_{Z})
over
W(k)
, where the latter is equipped with the trivial log structure.

For a given
p
-adic discretely valued field
K
with perfect residue field, we let
C
be its complete algebraic closure.
We fix a compatible system
\zeta_{p^{n}}
of
p^{n}
-th roots of unity in
\mathcal{O}_{C}
and let
\epsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\ldots)
be the corresponding element of
\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat}=\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathcal{O}_{C}/p
.
Let
\mathrm{A}_{\inf}=\mathrm{A}_{\inf}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W(\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat})
and
q\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[\epsilon]\in\mathrm{A}_{\inf}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
be the Teichmüller lift.
We set
\mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}q-1
and
\xi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[p]_{q^{1/p}}=\frac{q-1}{q^{1/p}-1}
.
They satisfy the relation
\varphi(\mu)=\mu\cdot\varphi(\xi)
.
We call the quotient map
\mathrm{A}_{\inf}\to\mathrm{A}_{\inf}/\xi\simeq\mathcal{O}_{C}
the
de Rham specialization map
and denote it by
\theta
as usual.

For a
p
-adic topological
K
-algebra
A
, to simplify the notation, we let
{\mathrm{Spa}}(A)
be the adic spectrum
{\mathrm{Spa}}(A,A^{\circ})
, where
A^{\circ}
is the subring of power-bounded elements.

2.
Log structure, and logarithmic
F
-isocrystals

Let
K
be a complete discretely valued
p
-adic field with perfect residue field
k
, and let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be its ring of integers. In this section, we recall the basics of the log structures used in the article, and prove various structural results on
F
-isocrystals and their cohomology over (log-)crystalline sites.

2.1.
Log structures, log-crystalline site, and semi-stable reduction

We recall some basic definitions for readers’ convenience and set up some notations and terminology which we shall use later, following
[
Kat89
]
and
[
HK94
]
. Let
X
be a scheme.
A
pre-log structure
on
X
is a sheaf of monoids
M
on the étale site
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
endowed with a homomorphism
\alpha:M\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
with respect to the multiplication on
X
, where
\alpha
is often assumed.
We call it a
log structure
if
\alpha
restricts to an isomorphism
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{*})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}^{*}
.
Given any pre-log structure
(M,\alpha)
on
X
, we can always form the
associated
log structure
M^{a}
by taking the pushout of
M
along
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})\to\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}
.
We shall often refer to a “scheme with log structure” simply as a “log scheme”. By the
trivial log structure
on
X
, we mean
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
. For convenience we impose the following convention:
When we view a scheme
X
as a log scheme without specifying a log structure, the trivial log structure is assumed.

For a monoid
P
, let us write
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
for the associated commutative group
\{ab^{-1}\mid a,b\in P\}
, and we say that
P
is
integral
if the natural map
P\to P^{\mathrm{gp}}
is injective. Given
P
, denote by
P_{X}
the constant sheaf of monoid over the scheme
X
defined by
P
.
A log structure
M
on
X
is said to be
quasi-coherent
(resp.
coherent
) if étale-locally on
X
, there exists a monoid (resp. finitely generated monoid)
P
and a homomorphism
P_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
such that
M\simeq(P_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X})^{a}
.
We say that
M
is
fine
if it is integral (i.e., given by a sheaf of integral monoids) and coherent. When
(X,M)
is a fine log scheme, such homomorphism
P_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
will be called a
chart for
M
.

For a morphism of schemes
f:X\to Y
and a log structure
N
on
X
, the
inverse image

f^{*}N
is defined to be
(f^{-1}(N))^{a}
.
A morphism between log schemes
f:(X,M)\to(Y,N)
is defined to be a morphism
f:X\to Y
between the underlying schemes together with a morphism
f^{*}N\to M
, such that the latter is compatible with the map of structure sheaves.
Now let
f:(X,M)\to(Y,N)
be a morphism between log schemes.
We say the morphism is
strict
(resp.
exact
) if the map of monoids
f^{*}N\to M
is an isomorphism (resp.
f^{-1}N=f^{-1}(N)^{\mathrm{gp}}\times_{M^{\mathrm{gp}}}M
).
We also say that
f
is a
closed immersion
(resp.
exact closed immersion
) if the underlying morphism
f:X\to Y
is a closed immersion and the associated morphism
f^{*}N\to M
is surjective (resp. bijective).
In particular, an exact closed immersion is strict.
One can also define the notions of (formally)
étale
or
smooth
morphisms between log schemes, and we refer the reader to the standard references (e.g.,
[
Kat89
, §3.3]
).
When
(X,M)
and
(Y,N)
are both fine log schemes, then étale locally on
X
we can always find a
chart for
f
, i.e., a triple
(P_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X},Q_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y},Q\to P)
where
P_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
Q_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y}
are charts for
M
and
N
respectively, and
Q\to P
is a morphism between monoids for which the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{-1}(N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M.}

For our purposes, charts are mainly used to find
exact localizations
for closed immersions, by which we mean diagrams like (
2
) below.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\iota:(X,M)\to(Y,N)
be a closed immersion between fine log schemes. Then étale locally on
X
,
\iota^{\prime}
factors as

(2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Y^{\prime},N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Y,N)}}
étale
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}

such that
\iota^{\prime}
is an exact closed immersion and the natural projection
(Y^{\prime},N^{\prime})\to(Y,N)
is étale.

More concretely, if
(P_{X},\to M,Q_{Y}\to N,h:Q\to P)
is a chart for
\iota
such that
h^{\mathrm{gp}}
is surjective, then
(Y^{\prime},N^{\prime})
can be constructed by

(3)

Y^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}Y\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[Q]}\mathbb{Z}[Q^{\prime}]\text{ and }N^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}N|_{Y^{\prime}},

where
Q^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(h^{\mathrm{gp}})^{-1}(P)
.

Proof.

See
[
Kat89
, Prop. 4.1]
and its proof.
∎

Next, we recall the definition of log-crystalline sites, up to the generality we need. Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
and set
W\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W(k)
and
W_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W/p^{n}W
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\gamma
denote the standard PD structure on
W
or
W_{n}
. First, we summarize in the following proposition the universal property and construction of
log PD-envelopes
.

Proposition 2.2
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, consider the following two categories.

(a)

The category
\mathscr{C}
of closed immersions of log schemes
\iota:(X,M)\hookrightarrow(Y,N)
over
W_{n}
such that
M
is integral and coherent and
N
is quasi-coherent.

(b)

The category
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}
of pairs
(\iota,\delta)
, where
\iota
is an exact closed immersions
\iota:(X,M)\hookrightarrow(Y,N)
of integral and quasi-coherent log schemes over
W_{n}
, and
\delta
is a PD structure on the ideal of
Y
defining
X
which is compatible with the standard PD-structure
\gamma
.

Then the natural forgetful functor
\mathsf{F}:\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{C}
admits a right adjoint
\mathsf{D}
, which sends an object to what is called its log PD-envelope. Moreover, if
\iota:(X,M)\hookrightarrow(Y,N)
is an object in
\mathscr{C}
with
M
fine and
N
coherent, and
\iota^{\prime}:(X,M)\hookrightarrow(Y^{\prime},N^{\prime})
is an étale localization as in (
2
), then the log PD-envelope
\mathsf{D}(\iota)
is of the form
(\widetilde{\iota}:(X,M^{\prime})\hookrightarrow(D,M_{D}),\delta)
, where
(\widetilde{\iota},\delta)
is the usual PD-envelope of
\iota^{\prime}
with respect to
\gamma
,
M^{\prime}=M|_{\widetilde{X}}
and
M_{D}=N^{\prime}|_{D}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Bei13
, §1.3, Thm]
and
[
Kat89
, Prop. 5.2]
. Note that the Beilinson’s construction generalizes Kato’s and they agree whenever the latter applies.
∎

Now we fix a log scheme
(X,M)
over
k
of finite type. The
(big absolute) log-crystalline site

((X,M)/W_{n})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
(resp.
((X,M)/W)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
) is the category whose objects are 5-tuples of the form
(U,T,M_{T},\iota,\delta)
such that
U
is a scheme over
X
,
(T,M_{T})
is a log scheme over
W_{n}
(resp.
W_{m}
for some
m
), and
(\iota:(U,M|_{U})\hookrightarrow(T,M_{T}),\delta)
is an object of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}
as in
Proposition

2.2
.
As in
Section

1.7
and to simplify notation, we use
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
to abbreviate the notation
((X,M)/W)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, and impose the following convention.

Convention 2.3
.

When we use
W
or
W_{n}
equipped with the trivial log structure as the base of log-crystalline site, we omit it the log structure from the notation.

Define the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{(X,M)/W_{n}}
(or
\mathcal{O}_{(X,M)/W}
) to be the functor which sends each object
(U,T,M_{T},\iota,\delta)
to
\Gamma(T,\mathcal{O}_{T})
. A
coherent crystal
on
\mathcal{O}_{(X,M)/W_{n}}
(or
\mathcal{O}_{(X,M)/W}
) is a sheaf of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X/W}
-modules
\mathcal{E}
such that for every morphism
g:T^{\prime}\to T
in the given site, the induced map
g^{*}\mathcal{E}(T)\to\mathcal{E}(T^{\prime})
is an isomorphism. The category of
isocrystals
, which we shall denote by
\mathrm{Isoc}((X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
, is defined as the
\mathbb{Q}
-linearization of the category of coherent crystals over
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
To define
F
-isocrystals, let us first call how the
absolute Frobenius
morphism
F_{(X,M)}:(X,M)\to(X,M)
for a log scheme
(X,M)
over
k
is defined: On the underlying scheme it is just the standard absolute Frobenius morphism, and
F^{-1}_{(X,M)}M\to M
is the multiplication by
p
map when
F^{-1}_{(X,M)}M
is canonically identified with
M
. An
F
-isocrystal
consists of an isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
together with an isomorphism
\varphi:F^{*}_{(X,M)}\mathcal{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}
in
\mathrm{Isoc}((X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
. We denote the category of
F
-isocrystals by
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
.

For book-keeping purposes, let us also introduce the category
(X,M)^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
whose objects are inverse systems
\{\mathcal{T}_{n}=(U_{n},T_{n},M_{T_{n}},\iota_{n},\delta_{n})\in((X,M)/W_{n})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that the mod
p^{n}
-reduction of
\mathcal{T}_{m}
is
\mathcal{T}_{n}
whenever
m\geq n
and each
\mathcal{T}_{n}
is flat over
W_{n}
.
We identify such inverse systems with the exact closed immersion of formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes

(U,M|_{U})\hookrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}(T_{n},M_{T_{n}})

with log PD-structure
\varprojlim_{n}\delta_{n}
(cf.
[
Shi00
, Def. 2.2.1]
)
, and call such an object
pro-PD-thickening
.
Given such
\{\mathcal{T}_{n}\}
and a sheaf
\mathcal{E}
of
\mathcal{O}_{(X,M)/W}
-modules, we may evaluate
\mathcal{E}
on
\{\mathcal{T}_{n}\}
and obtain a sheaf of modules
\varprojlim\mathcal{E}(T_{n},M_{T_{n}})
on the formal scheme
\varprojlim T_{n}
.
The lemma below describes categorical products in
(X,M)^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite set and let
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(U_{\lambda},T_{\lambda},M_{T_{\lambda}},\iota_{\lambda},\delta_{\lambda})
be objects of
((X,M)/W_{n})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
(resp.
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}^{\wedge}
) indexed by
\lambda\in\Lambda
. Then the (categorical) product
\prod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}
is given by the log PD-envelope (resp. the
p
-complete log PD-envelope)
\mathcal{T}
of the closed immersion

\iota:\biggl(U\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{X}U_{\lambda},M|_{U}\biggr)\hookrightarrow\biggl(T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{W_{n}}T_{\lambda},M_{T}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Bigl(\prod M_{T_{\lambda}}\Bigr|_{T}\Bigr)^{a}\biggr).

Proof.

This can be read off from
[
Bei13
, §1.5 Prop.]
and the arguments therein.
∎

We also recall the definition of semi-stable
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

Definition 2.5
.

We say that a
p
-adic formal scheme
Y
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is
semi-stable
(resp.
weakly semi-stable
) if
Y
can be covered in étale topology by open affines
U
étale over

{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle y_{1},\cdots,y_{r},y_{r+1}^{\pm},\cdots,y_{n}^{\pm}\rangle/(y_{1}\cdots y_{r}-\varpi))

where
\varpi
is a uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
(resp.
0\neq\varpi
is a non-unit in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
). Here
r,n,\varpi
may depend on
U
.

Now we review the log structures on the
p
-adic discrete valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and its Breuil–Kisin ring, which will also be the setup for the rest of the section.

Example 2.6
.

We let
K
be a complete discretely valued
p
-adic field, with
\mathcal{O}_{K}
the ring of integers and
k
the perfect residue field.
Let
W=W(k)
be the ring of Witt vectors, and let
\mathfrak{S}
be the Breuil–Kisin ring
W(k)[\![u]\!]
.
For a given uniformizer
\pi
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
we can equip the ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with the log structure
M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
associated to the pre-log structure
\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
n\mapsto\pi^{n}
.
Equivalently, the log structure
M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
is given by the monoid
\mathcal{O}_{K}\cap K^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Moreover, it induces a log structure
M_{k}
on the residue field
k
by base change, namely the map of monoids
\mathbb{N}\to k
, sending
n
onto
0^{n}
, and one can equip a hollow log structure
M_{0}
on
W
that is associated to
\mathbb{N}\to W
,
n\mapsto 0^{n}
.
7
7

7

Note that
M_{0}
is different from
M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
even when
W=\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
This marks the difference between the
crystalline log structure
and the
de Rham log structure
, which traces back to Hyodo–Kato’s comparison theorem of log analogue of crystalline-de Rham comparison.

On the other hand, the choice of
\pi
induces a log structure
M_{\mathfrak{S}}
on
\mathfrak{S}
deforming
M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
, defined by
\mathbb{N}\to\mathfrak{S}
,
n\mapsto u^{n}
.
The various (pre-)log structures can be summarized into the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n\mapsto\pi^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n\mapsto u^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n\mapsto 0^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0^{n}}

Denote by
E(u)
the Eisenstein polynomial which generates the kernel of
\mathfrak{S}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Note that with the given log structures, the surjection
\mathfrak{S}\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
is already an exact closed immersion. We let
D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E)
be the
p
-complete PD-envelope of
(E(u))
, and use
M_{\mathfrak{S}}
to denote the induced log structure on
D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E)
. More explicitly,

(4)

D=W\llbracket u\rrbracket[\frac{E(u)^{m}}{m!},\forall m\geq 1]^{\wedge}_{p}

By the formal smoothness of the ring
\mathfrak{S}
over
W
, the log PD-thickening
(D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E),\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathfrak{S}})
is a weakly initial object in the absolute log-crystalline site
\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p}\bigr)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.

Our next example is the log structure on a framed
p
-adic formal scheme with semi-stable reduction.

Example 2.7
.

Let
R
be a
p
-complete algebra over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Assume
R
is (
p
-completely) smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\rangle/(\prod_{j=1}^{s}y_{j}-\pi)
for some integer
s\geq 1
.
Then we can equip
R
with the log structure
M_{R}
associated with the map of monoids

\mathbb{N}^{s}\to R,\quad(n_{1},\ldots,n_{s})\mapsto\prod y_{j}^{n_{j}}.

This in particular enhances the map of rings
\mathcal{O}_{K}\to R
to a map of log pairs
(\mathcal{O}_{K},M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})\to(R,M_{R})
.
Here we note that in the special case when
s=1
and thus
R
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, the log structure
M_{R}
is nothing but the base change of
M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
along the map of rings
\mathcal{O}_{K}\to R
.

More canonically, by
[
ČK19
, Claim. 1.6.1]
the log structure
M_{R}\to R
is equal to the map of monoids

(R\cap(R[1/p])^{\times})\longrightarrow R,

which in particular can be globalized to a log structure on a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, namely

M_{X}:(\mathcal{O}_{X}\cap\lambda_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}^{\times})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X},

where
\lambda:X_{\eta,\mathrm{an}}\to X_{\mathrm{Zar}}
is the canonical map of sites.
We call this latter construction the
standard log structure
on a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.
Here we also remark that by construction, the generic fiber
M_{X_{\eta}}
is the trivial log structure on
X_{\eta}
.

We then record the log structure on perfectoid rings.

Example 2.8

(log structure on perfectoids)
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid ring over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
We let
M_{S}
be the monoid
S\cap S[1/p]^{\times}
on
S
, this then forms a log structure on the ring
S
which is compatible with
M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
under the natural map
\mathcal{O}_{K}\to S
.
More generally, assume
R
is a topologically finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with the standard log structure
M_{R}
as in
Example

2.7
.
Then any map
R\to S
can be naturally extended to a map of log pairs
(R,M_{R})\to(S,M_{S})
, since the image of
y_{j}
in
S
is also contained in
S[1/p]
as well.

For later usage, we give an explicit construction of the Čech nerve for the log PD-thickening associated with Breuil–Kisin ring.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let
(\mathfrak{S},M_{\mathfrak{S}})
be Breuil–Kisin ring with its log structure as defined in
Example

2.6
, and let
(D,\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E)^{\wedge}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathfrak{S}})
be the associated log pro-PD-thickening in the site
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Then the Čech nerve
(D^{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D^{\bullet}})
of the covering object
(D,\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D})
in
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is naturally isomorphic to the cosimplicial
p
-complete PD-polynomials with log structures

([n]\longmapsto\bigl(D^{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}D\{t_{i},~1\leq i\leq n\},~\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,~M_{D^{n}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{u^{a_{0}}u_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots u_{n}^{a_{n}}~|~a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z},~\sum a_{i}\geq 0\}^{a}\bigr).

where
t_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}u/u_{i}-1
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

2.4
, the
(n+1)
-th self coproduct of
(D,\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D})
in
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is the
p
-adic completion of the log PD-envelope for the surjection

(5)

\bigl((D^{\otimes_{W}n+1})^{\wedge}_{p},(\prod_{i=0}^{n}M_{\mathfrak{S}})^{a}\bigr)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p}).

Applying formula (
3
) in
Lemma

2.1
, one finds an exact localization of (
5
) given by

(6)

\bigl((D^{\otimes_{W}n+1})^{\wedge}_{p}[\frac{u}{u_{i}},\frac{u_{i}}{u},~\forall 1\leq i\leq n],\{u^{a_{0}}u_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots u_{n}^{a_{n}}~|~a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z},\sum a_{i}\geq 0\}^{a}\bigr)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p}),

the kernel of which is topologically generated by the elements
uu^{-1}_{i}-1
, for
1\leq i\leq n
.
So the ring
D^{n}
is isomorphic to the
p
-complete PD-envelope of the above surjection, namely

\bigl(W\llbracket u,u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\rrbracket[\frac{E(u)^{m}}{m!},\frac{E(u_{i})^{m}}{m!},\frac{(\frac{u}{u_{i}}-1)^{m}}{m!}~|~\forall 1\leq i\leq n,m\geq 1])^{\wedge}_{p}.

This then is naturally isomorphic to the complete divided power polynomial ring of the variables
u/u_{i}-1
for
1\leq i\leq n
over the ring
D
in
Example

2.6
, namely the ring

D\{\frac{u}{u_{i}}-1,~1\leq i\leq n\}\simeq D\{t_{i},1\leq i\leq n\},

for variables
t_{i}
representing
u_{i}/u-1
.
Here, the log structure of
D^{n}
by construction is associated to the pre-log structure for the monoid
\{u^{a_{0}}u_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots u_{n}^{a_{n}}~|~a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z},~\sum a_{i}\geq 0\}
, which we denote as
M_{D^{n}}
.
∎

The previous lemma in particular allows us to define the
monodromy operator
on the log PD-thickening
(D,M_{D})
.

Construction 2.10

(Monodromy on
D
)
.

We let
p_{0}
and
p_{1}
be the two coface maps from
(D,M_{D})
to
(D^{1},M_{D^{1}})
in the cosimplicial diagram of
Lemma

2.9
.
By construction, they are
W
-linear and are induced by sending the element
u\in D
onto
u
and
u_{1}
in
D^{1}
respectively.
In particular, by taking their difference and translating the formula in terms of
u
and
t_{1}
, we get

D\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}}t_{1}\cdot D\{t_{1}\},\quad u^{m}\mapsto u^{m}-u_{1}^{m}=u^{m}(1-\frac{1}{(1+t_{1})^{m}}).

By taking the image of
p_{0}-p_{1}
in the quotient
t_{1}D\{t_{1}\}/(t_{1}^{[\geq 2]})^{\wedge}_{p}D\{t_{1}\}\simeq t_{1}\cdot D\simeq D
,
we get the
monodromy
operator on
D
, namely the endomorphism

N_{D}\colon D\longrightarrow D,\quad u^{m}\longmapsto mu^{m}.

It in particular sends a power series
f(u)\in D\subset K_{0}\llbracket u\rrbracket
onto the element
f^{\prime}(u)u
.
As a consequence, the kernel of the endomorphism
N_{D}
is equal to
W
, and by induction the subset of nilpotent elements under
N_{D}
is the polynomial subring
W[\log{u}]\subset D
. Moreover, since the Frobenius
\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}}
sends
u
onto
u^{p}
, one readily checks that
N_{D}
and
\varphi_{D}
satisfy the formula
N_{D}\varphi_{D}=p\varphi_{D}N_{D}
.

Alternatively, regarding
D
as a subring of
K_{0}\llbracket u\rrbracket
, one may view
N_{D}
as characterized by the property that
\nabla(f(u))=N_{D}(f(u))d\log(u)
, where
\nabla:D\to D\otimes_{W\{u\}}\Omega^{1}_{(W\{u\},(u{N})^{a})/W}=D\cdot d\log(u)
is the standard differentiation.

2.2.
Logarithmic
F
-isocrystals

In this subsection, we discuss the various descriptions of the category of
F
-isocrystals and their logarithmic analogues for smooth finite type schemes in characteristic
p
.
Before the discussion, we recall our convention that unless otherwise mentioned, the base of any crystalline site is
\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}=W(k)
, and the base of any log-crystalline site is
W(k)
with the trivial log structure.

First, we recall an explicit local formula for
F
-isocrystals over the crystalline site.

Proposition 2.11
.

[
Ogu84
]

let
Z
be a smooth scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p
, and let
Z_{s}
be its reduced special fiber.
The following categories are equivalent:

(a)

The category of
F
-isocrystals over the convergent site
(Z/W)_{\mathrm{conv}}
in the sense of Ogus
[
Ogu84
, §2]
.

(b)

The category of
F
-isocrystals over the crystalline site
Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}
.

In addition, if there is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
Z_{0}
over
W
that admits a Frobenius lift
\varphi_{Z_{0}}
such that
Z\simeq Z\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(W)}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, then the above categories are also equivalent to the following

(c)

The category
\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi,\nabla_{Z_{0}}}(\mathcal{O}_{Z_{0,\eta}})
of flat connections
\mathcal{F}
over the generic fiber
(Z_{0})_{\eta}
with a horizontal
\varphi_{Z_{0}}
-linear Frobenius endomorphism whose linearization
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\varphi_{Z_{0}}^{*}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Similar to
Proposition

2.12
, the equivalence between the first two categories were proved by Ogus in
[
Ogu84
, Prop. 2.18, Ex. 2.7.3]
.
Assume the existence of the lift, then the equivalence between the last two categories is a classical result.
It is well known (e.g., by
[
Sta23
,
Tag 07JH
]
) that the category of isocrystals on
Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}
is equivalent to that of vector bundles on
(Z_{0})_{\eta}
equipped with an integrable and quasi-nilpotent connection. Then
[
Ber96
, Thm 2.4.2]
tells us that the quasi-nilpotence is automatic when a Frobenius structure is present.
∎

Our goal is to establish the analogues of the above facts in the log-crystalline setting.
Let us start with the following.

Proposition 2.12
.

Let
Z
be a finite type scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p
, and let
Z_{s}
be the base change
Z\times_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p)}{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
.

(i)

The natural pullback functor along
Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}\to Z_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
induces an equivalence of categories of
F
-isocrystals

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{{\mathrm{crys}}})\simeq\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}).

(ii)

Assume
Z
admits a log structure
M_{Z}
, and let
M_{s}
be its base change along
{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p)
.
The natural pullback functor along
(Z_{s},M_{s})_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}}\to(Z,M)_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}}
induces an equivalence of categories of
F
-isocrystals

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,M)_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}})\simeq\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{s},M_{s})_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}}).

Proof.

This is known as Dwork’s trick, and part
(i)
follows from the proof of
[
Ogu84
, Prop. 2.18]
.
For part
(ii)
, it can be proved in the same way by replacing the crystalline site of loc. cit. by the log-crystalline site (cf.
[
DLMS24
, Rmk B.23]
).
For readers’ convenience, we explain the construction of the functor that is inverse to the pullback.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be an integer so that the
n
-th power of the Frobenius on
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p
factors through the natural surjection
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p\to k
.
By the functoriality of Frobenius morphisms, we then have a commutative diagram of schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p
that is compatible with their log structures

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{s},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{Z}^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where
\iota:Z_{s}\to Z
is the natural closed immersion.
Now we define a functor from
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{s},M_{s})_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}})
to
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,M)_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}})
, by sending
(\mathcal{F},\varphi_{\mathcal{F}})
onto its pullback
(f^{*}\mathcal{F},\varphi_{f^{*}\mathcal{F}})
along the map of sites
f:(Z_{s},M_{s})_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}}\to(Z,M)_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}}
.

To check that this forms an inverse to the pullback functor along the closed immersion
\iota
, we notice that for an
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
over
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,M)_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}})
,
the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
gives an isomorphism of
F
-isocrystals
F_{Z}^{n,*}\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathcal{E}
in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,M)_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}})
.
Notice that by the commutative diagram above, we have

F_{Z}^{n,*}\mathcal{E}\simeq f^{*}\mathcal{F},

where
\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\iota^{*}\mathcal{E}
is the restriction.
So we get
f^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{E}
, which is functorial with respect to
\mathcal{E}
.
On the other hand, by the functoriality of Frobenius morphisms, the composition
\iota^{*}\circ f^{*}
is equivalent to the pullback functor
F_{Z_{s}}^{n,*}
for the
n
-th power Frobenius
F_{Z_{s}}^{n}:Z_{s}\to Z_{s}
.
Hence thanks to the Frobenius structure on isocrystals again, we see the functor
\iota^{*}\circ f^{*}
is an equivalence on
F
-isocrystals.
∎

By the above proposition, we can simplify the study of log F-isocrystals on a finite type log scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p
to the study of log F-isocrystals on a log
k
-variety.

Definition 2.13
.

Let
Z
be a
k
-variety. Let
F_{Z}
be the absolute Frobenius endomorphism on
Z
. Let
\mathrm{Isoc}^{N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
be the category of pairs
(\mathcal{E},N)
where
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is an F-isocrystal in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
and
N
is an endomorphism of
\mathcal{E}
.
Let
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
be the category of triples
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}},N_{\mathcal{E}})
where
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is an object of
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
and
N_{\mathcal{E}}
is an endomorphism of the isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
, with an induced endomorphism
N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}pF_{Z}^{*}N_{\mathcal{E}}
acting on
F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}
.
We often call
N
the
monodromy operator
on
\mathcal{E}
.

Note that by definition, the isomorphism of isocrystals
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}:F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathcal{E}
is compatible with the operators
N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}}
and
N_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proposition 2.14
.

For every
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}},N_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
, the endomorphism
N_{\mathcal{E}}
is nilpotent.

Proof.

By
[
Ogu84
, Thm 4.1]
it suffices to show that for some closed point
z
on each connected component of
Z
, the fiber of
N
at
z
is nilpotent.
Therefore, we reduce to the case when
Z={\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
is a point itself, so that
\mathcal{E}
is nothing but a
K_{0}
-vector space and
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
is a
\sigma
-linear automorphism of
\mathcal{E}
.
We let
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\mathcal{E}}
be the semi-linear endomophism of
\mathcal{E}
induced from the isomorphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then the condition on the monodromy operator translates to an equality of semi-linear endomorphisms
N_{\mathcal{E}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\mathcal{E}}=p\widetilde{\varphi}_{\mathcal{E}}N_{\mathcal{E}}
, which implies that
N_{\mathcal{E}}\in\mathrm{End}_{K_{0}}(\mathcal{E})
has no nonzero eigenvalues.
Therefore,
(N_{\mathcal{E}})^{\dim\mathcal{E}}=0
by the existence of Jordan normal form.
∎

Construction 2.15
.

Let
P
be a topologically finite type
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree
W
-algebra equipped with the trivial log structure.
Set
D_{P}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(P\otimes_{W}D)^{\wedge}_{p}
.
As
(D,(u{N})^{a})
is (formally) log smooth over
W
, so is
(D_{P},(u{N})^{a})
over
P
. Hence the conormal sequence below is short exact (see e.g.,
[
Ogu18
, IV Thm 3.2.3]
):

(7)

0\to D_{P}\widehat{\otimes}_{P}\Omega^{1}_{P/W}\to\Omega^{1}_{(D_{P},(u{N})^{a})/W}\to\Omega^{1}_{(D_{P},(u{N})^{a})/P}\to 0.

Since the formation of logarithmic diffentials commutes with base change, there is a canonical isomorphism
\Omega^{1}_{(D_{P},(u{N})^{a})/P}\simeq D_{P}\widehat{\otimes}_{D}\Omega^{1}_{(D,(u{N})^{a})/W}
.
The section
D_{P}\to P
given by
u\mapsto 0
of the natural map
P\to D_{P}
provides a splitting of (
7
), or equivalently a decomposition

(8)

\Omega^{1}_{(D_{P},(u{N})^{a})/W}\simeq\bigr(D_{P}\widehat{\otimes}_{P}\Omega^{1}_{P/W}\bigr)\oplus\bigr(D_{P}\widehat{\otimes}_{D}\Omega^{1}_{(D,(u{N})^{a})/W}\bigr)=\bigr(D_{P}\widehat{\otimes}_{P}\Omega^{1}_{P/W}\bigr)\oplus\bigr(D_{P}\cdot d\log(u)\bigr).

The decomposition above and the projection formula implies that given a quasi-coherent
D_{P}
-module
\mathcal{M}
, to give a continuous connection
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}
on
\mathcal{M}
that has the log-pole at
\{u=0\}
, it is equivalent to give the connections

\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{P}\Omega^{1}_{P/W}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}\cdot d\log(u),

where
\mathcal{M}
is viewed as a module over
P
and
D
respectively
8
8

8

This means that we suppressed the notations for the direct image functor after applying the projection formula.
.
Let
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}
denote the
W
-linear endomorphism on
\mathcal{M}
such that

(9)

\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}(m)=\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}(m)\cdot d\log(u),~\forall m\in\mathcal{M}.

Direct computation shows that
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}=0
if and only if
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}^{2}=0,\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}^{2}=0
, and
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}
commutes with
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
(i.e., the following diagram commutes).

(10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{P}\Omega^{1}_{P/W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{P}\Omega^{1}_{P/W}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}}

Construction 2.16
.

Let
Z
be a
k
-variety. There is a natural fully faithful functor of sites
Z_{\mathrm{crys}}\to(Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
given be sending a PD-thickening
(T,U)\in Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
to
(T,U,(0{N})^{a})
. This induces a natural forgetful functor
\mathsf{F}:\mathrm{Isoc}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
.
Now we construct a
residue functor

\mathrm{Res}
which equips a monodromy operator
N_{\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})}
on
\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})
for each
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Isoc}((Z,0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
which will fit into the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})}.}
forget
\scriptstyle N
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}

Fix such an isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
.
Our goal is to construct for each pro-PD-thickening
(T,U)\in Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
an endomorphism
N_{\mathcal{E}}(T)
of
\mathcal{E}(T,U,(0{N})^{a})
which is functorial in
(T,U)
.

We may assume
T={\mathrm{Spf}}(P)
is affine and perform
Construction

2.15
for
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{Spf}}(D_{P}),U,(u{N})^{a})
.
Note that the exact surjection of the log pairs
(D_{P},(u{N})^{a})\to(P,(0{N})^{a})
that is given by
u\mapsto 0
induces a morphism between objects in
(Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, so that by the crystalline nature of
\mathcal{E}
there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{D_{P}}P\simeq\mathcal{E}(T,U,(0{N})^{a})
.
By the Leibniz rule, for any
f(u)\in D\subseteq K_{0}[\![u]\!]
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
, we have

\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}(f(u)m)=uf^{\prime}(u)m+f(u)\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}(m).

This implies that
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}(u\mathcal{M})\subseteq u\mathcal{M}
. Therefore,
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},\partial_{u}}
induces an endomorphism on

\mathcal{M}\cdot d\log(u)/\mathcal{M}\cdot du\simeq\mathcal{M}/u\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{E}(T,U,(0{N})^{a}),

which is the sought after
N_{\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})}(T,U)
. The commutativity of the diagram (
10
) implies that
N_{\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})}(T,U)
is
P
-linear.
Putting
N_{\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})}(T,U)
together as
(T,U)
runs through objects in
Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
, we obtain the desired endomorphism
N_{\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})}
on
\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})
.
The construction is functorial, yielding the functor
\mathrm{Res}:\mathcal{E}\mapsto(\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E}),N_{\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})})
.

The functor
\mathsf{F}
clearly preserves Frobenius structures when they are present, and naturally induces a functor
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}:\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
which is denoted by
\mathsf{F}
as well.
In addition, the residue functor
\mathrm{Res}
can be upgraded to a functor
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
, as we shall see below.

Proposition 2.17
.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
, let
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})
be the
F
-isocrystal
\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
over the crystalline site
Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
, and let
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},N_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})
be the isocrystal with the monodromy operator
\mathrm{Res}(\mathcal{E})
. Then the triple
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}},N_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})
is naturally an object in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
, yielding a functor

\mathrm{Res}\colon\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}}).

Proof.

We let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be the underlying isocrystal of
\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})
over
Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
.
Note that
F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}
is an isocrystal on
(Z,(0{N})^{a})_{\mathrm{crys}}
in its own and
\mathsf{F}(F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E})=F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
Let
N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
be the monodromy operator on
F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
such that
\mathrm{Res}(F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E})=(F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E},N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})
.
Now we compare the following two diagrams.

(11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pF_{Z}^{*}N_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}

The first diagram commutes by the functoriality of
\mathrm{Res}
, and our goal is to show that the second one also commutes. Therefore, we reduce to showing that
N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}=pF_{Z}^{*}N_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
.

Zariski locally we can always embed
Z
into a smooth formal scheme over
W
, and by taking PD-envelope, we find an object
(T={\mathrm{Spf}}(P),U)\in Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
which admits a Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{P}
compatible with that of
Z
. Moreover, such
(T,U)
is weakly final, so it suffices to show that the above equality is satisfied once evaluated at
(T,U)
. Now perform
Construction

2.15
. The object
D_{P}
is equipped with a diagonal Frobenius action
\varphi_{D_{P}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varphi_{P}\widehat{\otimes}_{W}\varphi_{D}
.
Let
\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}(D_{P},U,(u{N})^{a})
.
Then by
Construction

2.16
, the monodromy operator
N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
is computed by taking the residue of the pullback (log) connection
\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}(\nabla_{\mathcal{M}})
on
\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}=(F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime})(D_{P})
. Hence the equation
N_{F_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(T,U)=pF_{Z}^{*}N_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(T,U)
follows from the fact that
\varphi_{D}^{*}(d\log(u))=d\log(u^{p})=p\cdot d\log(u)
.
∎

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
f:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be a morphism between
k
-varieties. Then the following diagrams of categories commute.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}((Z^{\prime},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{N}(Z^{\prime}_{\mathrm{crys}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z^{\prime},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}

Proof.

This is clear from the construction of
\mathrm{Res}
.
∎

Motivated by the above results, we make the following construction.

Definition 2.19
.

Let
P
be a topologically finite type

-complete and

Remark 2.20
.

(a)

By
Construction

2.15
, there are natural identifications
\mathrm{Coh}^{\nabla}({D_{P}}[1/p])\simeq\mathrm{Coh}^{\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])
and
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla}({D_{P}}[1/p])\simeq\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])
.
We remark that if a log connection
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}
on a
{D_{P}}
-module
\mathcal{M}
decomposes into
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}\oplus\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
as in
Construction

2.15
, then
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}
is quasi-nilpotent if and only if both
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
are quasi-nilpotent.

(b)

As in
Construction

2.16
and proof of
Proposition

2.17
, given
(\mathcal{M},\nabla_{\mathcal{M}})\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])
, we can attach a natural monodromy operator
N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\mathcal{M}/u\mathcal{M}
by reducing
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
mod
u
.
Hence there is a natural functor
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},N}(P[1/p])
induced by
u\mapsto 0
.

The following equivalence on Frobenius equivariant flat connections over two different base rings is inspired by Breuil’s work
[
Bre97
, §6]
and can be used to prove the higher dimensional version of loc. cit..
Roughly speaking, the statement can be thought as a pro-analogue of Dwork’s trick.

Theorem 2.21
.

Consider the set up in
Definition

2.19
.
We view
P
(resp.
D=W\{u\}
) as a
{D_{P}}
-algebra (resp.
W
-algebra) via the surjection induced by
u\mapsto 0
. Then the base change functor
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi}({D_{P}}[1/p])\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi}(P[1/p])
induced by
u\mapsto 0
is an equivalence of categories, with the quasi-inverse of which given by

(\mathcal{M}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}})\mapsto(D\widehat{\otimes}_{W}\mathcal{M}_{0}\simeq D_{P}[1/p]\otimes_{P}\mathcal{M}_{0},\varphi_{D}\otimes\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}).

Equivalently, we have the following two statements.

(i)

For every
(\mathcal{M},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}})\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi}({D_{P}}[1/p])
with mod
u
reduction
(\mathcal{M}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}})
, there exists a unique Frobenius-equivariant section
s
to the reduction map
\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

(ii)

In Part
(i)
, the morphism
D\widehat{\otimes}_{W}\mathcal{M}_{0}\to\mathcal{M}
induced by
s
is a Frobenius-equivariant isomorphism, with the Frobenius action on the domain given by
\varphi_{D}\otimes\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
.

Proof.

As the Tate twists on both categories are compatible with the functor, it suffices to show the equivalence for the subcategories consisting of effective objects (namely those Frobenius modules where the inverse of the Frobenius structures can be defined on a finitely generated submodule over
D_{P}
(resp.
P
)).
So we let
\mathcal{L}\subseteq\mathcal{M}
be a
{D_{P}}
-lattice such that
\varphi^{-1}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{L})\subseteq\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}\mathcal{L}
.

To begin, we produce a sequence of maps
\{\psi_{n}:\varphi_{T}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}\}
as below.
Let
\iota:\varphi_{P}^{*}\mathcal{M}\to\varphi_{{D_{P}}}^{*}\mathcal{M}=(\varphi_{P}^{*}\mathcal{M})\otimes_{D,\varphi_{D}}D
be the
P[1/p]
-linear map which sends an element
x
to
x\otimes 1
, and set
\psi_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\iota\circ\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}
.
Note that
\psi_{1}
is semi-linear with respect to
\varphi_{D}
.
Then we have the following commutative diagram:

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{P}^{*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{{D_{P}}}^{*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{P}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Now we iterate the construction by constructing the diagram below, where the map
\varphi^{i,*}_{P}\mathcal{M}\to\varphi^{i-1,*}_{P}\mathcal{M}
is given by pulling back
\psi_{1}
along the map
\varphi^{i-1}_{P}
.

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}^{n-1,*}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}^{n-1,*}\mathcal{M}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}

Let
\psi_{n}:\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
be the composite of the maps on the top row.

Now we produce the section map.
Given any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{L}/u\mathcal{L}
, we let
x_{n}
be its unique preimage in
\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{L}_{0}\subset\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Then we note the following lemma.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and let
y_{n},z_{n}
be any two lifts of the element
x_{n}\in\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{L}_{0}
in
\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{L}
.
Then
\psi_{n}(y_{n})-\psi_{n}(z_{n})\in p^{n}!\mathcal{L}
.

Proof.

Consider the top row of Diagram
13
.
As both
y_{n},z_{n}
are lifts of the element
x_{n}
along the mod
u
surjection
\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}\to\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}_{0}
, we have the difference element
y_{n}-z_{n}
is contained in
u\mathcal{L}
.
By the
\varphi_{D}^{n}
-semi-linearity of the map
\psi_{n}
, we then have

(14)

\displaystyle\psi_{n}(y_{n})-\psi_{n}(y_{n}^{\prime})\in u^{p^{n}}\mathcal{L}=p^{n}!\cdot\frac{u^{p^{n}}}{p^{n}!}\mathcal{L}\subseteq p^{n}!\mathcal{L},

where the last inclusion follows from the construction that
u^{p^{n}}/p^{n}!\in D
.
∎

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
y_{n}\in\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}
be a sequence of lifts of
x_{n}
. It follows from the above lemma that
\psi_{n}(y_{n})
converges in the
p
-adic topology of
\mathcal{L}
and its limit is independent of the choice of
y_{n}
’s.
Therefore, we obtain a well-defined map
\mathcal{L}_{0}\to\mathcal{L}
by sending
x_{0}
to
\lim_{n}\psi_{n}(y_{n})
. By inverting
p
, we obtain a Frobenius-equivariant section map
s:\mathcal{M}_{0}\to\mathcal{M}
.
One readily checks that the construction of
s
is independent of the choice of the lattice
\mathcal{L}
in the beginning.

To see that the section map
s
is the unique Frobenius-equivariant section, we note that if
s^{\prime}
is another such a section, then Diagram (
13
) can be extended as below:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{P}^{n-1,*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{P}^{n,*}\mathcal{M}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{P}^{n-1,*}\mathcal{M}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{0},}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{n,*}_{P}(s^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{n-1,*}_{P}(s^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}

Given
x\in\mathcal{M}_{0}
and the preimages
x_{n}\in\varphi^{n,*}_{P}\mathcal{M}_{0}
as before, we may take
y_{n}
to be
{\varphi^{n,*}_{P}(s^{\prime})}(x_{n})
.
Then by construction, we get
s(x)=\lim_{n}\psi_{n}(y_{n})
. This completes the proof of
(i)
.

Now we prove
(ii)
.
As above, we let
\mathcal{L}
be a lattce that is preserved by
\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}^{-1}
, and write
(\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}})
for
((D\otimes_{W}\mathcal{L}_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}[1/p],\varphi_{D}\otimes\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}})
, with
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=(D\otimes_{W}\mathcal{L}_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
the induced lattice of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
We let
h
be the natural map
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{L}
induced by
s
, which naturally extends to a map
\widetilde{h}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}
.
Since the element
u
is contained in the Jacobson ideal of the ring
D_{P}
and both
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
are finitely generated over
D_{P}
, we know by Nakayama’s lemma that the map
h
and hence
\widetilde{h}
are surjections.
In addition, since the composition of
h
with the map
\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}_{0}
is a surjection of
P
-modules, the kernel module
\ker(h)
is contained in
u\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.
We want to show that in fact
\ker(h)=0
.
Indeed, consider the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}\widetilde{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

On the one hand, by taking the Frobenius pullback, we know that
\ker(\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}h)
is contained in
u^{p}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.
So by inverting the element
p
and the commutative diagram above, we see that
\ker(\widetilde{h})
is contained in
u^{p}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
On the other hand, by the explicit construction of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, we know
u^{p}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=u^{p}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.
Hence we have
\ker(h)=\ker(\widetilde{h})\cap\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\subset u^{p}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.
Iterating this process, we must have
\ker(h)\subseteq u^{p^{m}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
However, as
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is
u
-separated, we must have
\ker(h)=0
.
∎

Corollary 2.23
.

In the setting of
Definition

2.19
, the base change functor
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},N}(P[1/p])
induced by
u\mapsto 0
is an equivalence of categories. The quasi-inverse functor is given by sending
(\mathcal{M}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}})\in\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},N}(P[1/p])
to the object
(\mathcal{M},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}},\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P},\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u})
defined by

(15)

\bigl(D\widehat{\otimes}_{W}\mathcal{M}_{0},~\varphi_{D}\otimes\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},~\mathrm{id}_{D}\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},~[\mathrm{id}_{D}\otimes N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}+N_{D}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}]\cdot d\log(u)\bigr)

in
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])
.

Proof.

First, one checks by routine computations that the formula (
15
) indeed defines an object of
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])
(cf.
Remark

2.20
).
We remark that the condition that
N_{\varphi_{P}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{0}}=p\varphi_{P}^{*}N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
ensures that the log-connection
\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}
, defined by putting
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
together, is horizontal with respect to the linear isomorphism
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}:\varphi_{D_{P}}^{*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{M},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}},\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P},\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u})
is an object of
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])
with mod
u
reduction
(\mathcal{M}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}})
.
Then
Theorem

2.21
tells us that there is a unique Frobenius-equivariant isomorphism
\mathcal{M}\simeq D\widehat{\otimes}_{W}\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
It remains to show that under this isomorphism, both
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
have to be given by the formula in (
15
).
Note that by putting
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},P}
and
\nabla_{\mathcal{M},u}
together to form a log connection
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}
on
\mathcal{M}
, we obtain an equivalence of categories
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])\simeq\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla}({D_{P}}[1/p])
(cf.
Construction

2.15
).
So it suffices to show that
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}
is the unique log connection for which
\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}
is horizontal and whose mod
u
reduction gives
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
and
N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
.
Indeed, if
\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
is another such log connection, then
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}-\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is a Frobenius equivariant
{D_{P}}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{({D_{P}},(u{N})^{a})/W}
whose mod
u
reduction is
0
, where we equip
\mathcal{M}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{D_{P}/W}
with the the Frobenius structure
defined by
\varphi_{\mathcal{M}}\otimes\mathrm{id}
.
So the map
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}-\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
is a morphism between two objects in
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi}({D_{P}}[1/p])
which vanishes under the reduction functor
\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi}({D_{P}}[1/p])\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi}(P[1/p])
.
Since this reduction functor is in particular fully faithful, we see the map
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}-\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}
must be zero.
∎

Theorem 2.24
.

Suppose that
Z
is a smooth
k
-variety. Then the residue functor

\mathrm{Res}:\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})

is an equivalence of categories. Moreover, every object of
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})
is locally free.

Proof.

We may assume that
Z
is affine and lifts to some smooth formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(P)
over
W
.
Moreover, assume that
F_{Z}
lifts to
\varphi_{P}
.
Apply
Construction

2.15
and then we obtain the following commutative diagram.

(16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}\bigl((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}\bigr)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{{\mathrm{crys}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},N}(P[1/p])}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mapsto 0}

The left vertical arrow is given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}\bigl((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}\bigr)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla}({D_{P}}[1/p])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Coh}^{\varphi,\nabla_{P},\nabla_{u}}({D_{P}}[1/p])}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the first equivalence is provided by
[
Kat89
, Thm 6.2]
and the second by
Construction

2.15
.
The right vertical arrow in Diagram
16
is an equivalence, provided by
Proposition

2.11
.
The bottom horizontal arrow is an equivalence, thanks to
Corollary

2.23
. Therefore,
\mathrm{Res}
must also be an equivalence.

Now given
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
, it is locally free if and only if the
{D_{P}}[1/p]
-module
\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}({D_{P}},Z,(u{N})^{a})
is locally free, as
({\mathrm{Spf}}({D_{P}}),Z,(u{N})^{a})
is a weakly final object in
(Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Since
Z
is smooth, the isocrystal
\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})
on
Z_{\mathrm{crys}}
is automatically locally free (cf.
[
Xu19
, Lem. 4.9]
).
Therefore, the
P[1/p]
-module
\mathcal{M}_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{F}(\mathcal{E})({\mathrm{Spf}}(P),Z)
is locally free.
Now note that
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\mathcal{M}/u\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{Spf}}(P),Z,(0{N})^{a})
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{M}
must also be locally free as
\mathcal{M}\simeq D_{P}[1/p]\otimes_{P[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0}
, thanks to the equivalence by
Theorem

2.21
.
∎

Remark 2.25
.

The log version of the local-freeness assertion
[
Xu19
, Lem. 4.9]
is not true without assuming the existence of Frobenius action.
Moreover, it is not true that
\mathrm{Isoc}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
is an equivalence even when
Z
is smooth. Here is an example: Let
Z={\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
be a point. Set
\mathcal{M}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W
, viewed as a
D=W\{u\}
-module via
u\mapsto 0
.
Then we may define a “sky-scraper” log-connection on the
D
-module
\mathcal{M}
by equipping it with the zero log connection
\nabla_{\mathcal{M}}=0
.
The resulting isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
on
({\mathrm{Spec}}(k),(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is clearly not locally free.
On the other hand, both
\mathcal{E}
and the trivial isocrystal
\mathcal{O}_{(Z,(0{N})^{a})/W}[1/p]
on
(Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
have the isomorphic image in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{N}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
.

However, the isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
cannot be equipped with a Frobenius structure, because
K_{0}
is not isomorphic to
\varphi^{*}_{W\{u\}}K_{0}\simeq K_{0}[u]/u^{p}
as a
W\{u\}
-module.

Combining all the previous results established in this subsection, we obtain the following equivalent descriptions of
F
-isocrystals over the log-crystalline site, extending
Proposition

2.11
to the log setting.

Corollary 2.26
.

Let
Z
be a smooth scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}/p
, let
M_{Z}
be the log structure
(\pi{N})^{a}
, and let
(Z_{s},M_{s})
be the reduced special fiber.
The following categories are equivalent:

(a)

The category
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,M_{Z})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
of
F
-isocrystals over the log-crystalline site
(Z,M_{Z})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.

(b)

The category
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{s},M_{Z_{s}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
of
F
-isocrystals over the log-crystalline site
(Z_{s},M_{Z_{s}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.

(c)

The category
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
of
F
-isocrystals with nilpotent endomorphisms over the crystalline site
Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}
.

In addition, if there is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
Z_{0}
over
W
that admits a Frobenius lift
\varphi_{Z_{0}}
such that
Z\simeq Z_{0}\otimes_{W}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p)
, then the above categories are also equivalent to the following

(d)

The category
\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi,\nabla_{Z_{0}},N}(Z_{0,\eta})
of the tuples
(\mathcal{F},\varphi_{\mathcal{F}},\nabla_{\mathcal{F},Z_{0}},N_{\mathcal{F}})
, where
(\mathcal{F},\varphi_{\mathcal{F}},\nabla_{\mathcal{F},Z_{0}})
is an object of
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{Z_{0}}}(Z_{0,\eta})
and
N_{\mathcal{F}}
is a nilpotent horizontal endomorphism on
\mathcal{F}
such that
N_{\varphi_{Z_{0}}^{*}\mathcal{F}}(\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varphi_{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\circ N_{\mathcal{F}}\circ\varphi_{\mathcal{F}}
) is equal to
p\varphi_{Z_{0}}^{*}N_{\mathcal{F}}
.

In addition, the canonical functor

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(\pi{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})

is fully faithful with essential image consisting of objects such that
N=0
in
(c)
and
(d)
.

Finally, we record a tautological lemma about the compatibility of the pullback functor with respect to the evaluation.

Lemma 2.27
.

Let
f:Z_{s}\to Z_{s}^{\prime}
be a map of
k
-varieties, and let
f_{0}:Z_{0}\to Z_{0}^{\prime}
be a map of
p
-adic formal schemes over
W
with compatible Frobenius lifts.
The pullback of
F
-isocrystals along
f
is compatible with the coherent pullback along
f_{0}
under the equivalences in
Corollary

2.26

2.3.
Relative log crystalline cohomology

In this subsection, we establish a crystal property of the log-crystalline cohomology in a relative setting, and in particular prove a relative version of the Hyodo–Kato comparison theorem.
We start with a remark commenting on the relationship between various constructions over the big and the small log-crystalline sites.

Remark 2.28
.

In many standard references, e.g.,
[
Shi08
,
Kat89
,
HK94
]
, one considers the small log crytalline site, whereas in the present paper we use the big (absolute) log crystalline site throughout.
It is well-known that the notion of crystals and their cohomology are independent of the choice of the big or the small crystalline site (see for example the arguments in
[
Guo21
, Cor. 2.2.8, Prop. 3.1.7]
), for the reader’s convenience let us briefly explain it as below.

Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme with a fine log structure
M
. Let
(X,M)_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
be small log-crystalline site, consists of those objects
(U,T,M_{T},\iota,\delta)
in the big site
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
such that
U\to X
is étale and
M_{T}
is fine (i.e., integral and coherent).
Zariski locally,
(X,M)
admits a closed immersion into a log-smooth scheme over
W(k)
.
We let
(\mathcal{D},M_{\mathcal{D}})
be the log pro-PD-envelope of the closed immersion, which is an object in both the small and the big sites.
So by the log-smoothness, the object
(\mathcal{D},M_{\mathcal{D}})
is a covering object of both
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
and
(X,M)_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, up to shrinking
X
by an open subscheme.
In addition, the Čech nerves of the covering object
(\mathcal{D},M_{\mathcal{D}})
within the two sites are by construction identical to each other, as the embedding
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}\hookrightarrow(X,M)_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
preserves finite limits.
As a consequence, as in the classical translation between the (iso)crystal and the (iso)-stratifications (cf.
[
Shi08
, (4.3.9)]
), we see the category of (iso)crystals over
(X,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is equivalent to the category of those over
(X,M)_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, identifying the cohomology of (iso)crystals computed in the big and the small sites.

In the following, we let
f_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
denote the morphism of the small log-crystalline topoi
(Y,M_{Y})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}\to(X,M_{X})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, for a map of fine log
k
-varieties
f:(Y,M_{Y})\to(X,M_{X})
.

Proposition 2.29
.

Let
X
be a smooth variety over
k
equipped with the log structure
M_{X}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(0{N})^{a}
.
Let
(Y,M_{Y})
be a fine log
k
-variety and
f:(Y,M_{Y})\to(X,M_{X})
be a proper and log-smooth morphism of Cartier type. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

(i)

Let
g:X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism between smooth
k
-varieties and
f^{\prime}:(Y^{\prime},M_{Y^{\prime}})\to(X^{\prime},M_{X^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(0{N})^{a})
be the pullback of the map
f
along the map
g
.
Then the canonical base change morphism

g^{*}R^{i}f_{{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y,M_{Y})/W}[1/p])\to R^{i}f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y^{\prime},M_{Y^{\prime}})/W}[1/p])

is an isomorphism.

(ii)

The higher direct image sheaf
R^{i}f_{{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y,M_{Y})/W}[1/p])
is an
F
-isocrystal, i.e., an object of
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X,M_{X})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}})
.

Proof.

We first mention that the evaluation of the coherent crystalline sheaf
\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R^{i}f_{{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y,M_{Y})/W})
on an object
(T,U,M_{T})\in(X,M_{X})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
can be computed by localization.
Namely, consider the functor of topoi

f_{Y_{U}/T,{\mathrm{crys}}*}:((Y|_{U},M_{Y}|_{Y_{U}})/(T,M_{T}))^{\sim}_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}\to T^{\sim}_{\mathrm{zar}}

introduced in
[
Shi08
, Def. 1.4]
. Then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Y,M_{Y})^{\sim}_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((Y|_{U},M_{Y}|_{Y_{U}})/(T,M_{T}))^{\sim}_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,M_{X})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}^{\sim}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\sim}_{\mathrm{zar}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{T}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{{\mathrm{crys}}*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{Y_{U}/T,{\mathrm{crys}}*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{T}}

where
j_{T}^{*}
(resp.
e_{T*}
) is the obvious restriction (resp. evaluation) functor. Since both
e_{T*}
and
j_{T}^{*}
are exact, and
j^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y,M_{Y})/W})=\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{U},M_{Y}|_{Y_{U}})/(T,M_{T})}
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

(17)

\mathcal{F}(T,U,M_{T})=e_{T*}(R^{i}f_{{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y,M_{Y})/W}))\simeq R^{i}f_{Y_{U}/T,{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{U},M_{Y}|_{Y_{U}})/(T,M_{T})}).

Here we note that as the map
f
is assumed to be smooth, the right hand side of (
17
), after inverting
p
, can be computed by log-convergent cohomology
[
Shi08
, Thm 2.36]
.
We shall repeatedly use this fact below.

We then produce an
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
on
(X,M_{X})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
and will eventually show that it coincides with
\mathcal{F}[1/p]
as sheaves over the small log-crystalline site.
Without loss of generality, let us assume that
X={\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{s})
is affine and admits a lifting
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
over
W
.
To construct
\mathcal{E}
, we take
({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})
to be the covering object
({\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0}\{u\}),X,(u{N})^{a})
of
(X,M)_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
and consider its Čech nerve
({D_{P}}^{\bullet},X,M_{{D_{P}}^{\bullet}})
.
Note that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, each structural morphism in the Čech nerve
({D_{P}}^{n},X,M_{{D_{P}}^{n}})\to({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})
is analytically flat.
Therefore, by
[
Shi08
, Cor. 3.10]
(using the conditions (1) and (2) of loc. cit.), for each projection morphism
p_{i}:({D_{P}}^{1},X,M_{{D_{P}}^{1}})\to({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})
there is a canonical isomorphism
p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[1/p]\simeq\mathcal{F}({D_{P}}^{1},X,M_{{D_{P}}^{1}})[1/p]
.
It is tautological that the resulting transition map
p_{0}^{*}\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[1/p]\simeq p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[1/p]
satisfies the cocycle condition. Hence
\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[1/p]
together with this transition map defines an isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
on
(X,M)_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
The fact that
\mathcal{E}
has the structure of an
F
-isocrystal is the consequence of
[
HK94
, Prop. 2.24]
. By
Theorem

2.24
, the existence of Frobenius structure forces
\mathcal{E}
, and hence
\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[1/p]
, to be locally free.

Now, let the maps
g
and
f^{\prime}
be as in
(i)
.
Let
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})
be an object of
(X^{\prime},(0{N})^{a})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, which is also an object of the big log crystalline site
(X,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Since
({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})
is a covering object of
(X,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
as well, the map
g
lifts to a morphism
\widetilde{g}:(T^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})\to({D_{P}},M_{D_{P}})
between the log schemes.
Moreover, as the
{D_{P}}[1/p]
-module
\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[1/p]
is locally free, by
[
Shi08
, Cor. 3.10]
(using the conditions (1)’ and (2) of loc. cit.), the canonical base change map below is an isomorphism

(18)

\widetilde{g}^{*}\mathcal{F}({D_{P}},X,M_{D_{P}})[\frac{1}{p}]\simeq R^{i}f^{\prime}_{Y^{\prime}/T^{\prime},{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y^{\prime},M_{Y^{\prime}})/(T^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})}[\frac{1}{p}]).

This in particular implies the base change formula of the log-crystalline cohomology in Part
(i)
.

Suppose now that
X^{\prime}
is étale over
X
. Then
(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})
is an object of
(X,M_{X})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
In particular, the left (resp. right) hand side of
18
is canonically identified with
\mathcal{E}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})
(resp.
\mathcal{F}(T^{\prime},X^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}})[1/p]
). This implies that
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{F}[1/p]
and hence we obtain Part
(ii)
.
∎

We now prove the relative version of the Hyodo–Kato isomorphism over a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme.

Theorem 2.30
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
equipped with the standard log structure
M_{X}
.
Let
(Y,M_{Y})
be a fine log
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
f:(Y,M_{Y})\to(X,M_{X})
be a proper and log-smooth morphism with Cartier type reduction.
Suppose that there is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X_{0}
over
W
such that
X\simeq X_{0}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

(i)

For each choice of uniformizer
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
there is an isomorphism of flat connections

(19)

(R^{i}f_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{s},M_{Y_{s}})/W})(X_{0},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{W}K\simeq R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Y_{\eta},M_{Y_{\eta}})/X_{\eta}}.

(ii)

For every point
x_{0}\in X_{0}(W)
with the special fiber
x_{s}\in X_{s}(k)
and the base extension
x\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}x_{0}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}\in X(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, the base change of the isomorphism (
19
) along the closed immersion
x_{0}\to X_{0}
, namely the isomorphism

(20)

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}((Y_{x_{s}},M_{Y_{x_{s}}})/(W,(0{N})^{a}))\otimes_{W}K\simeq\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{dR}}((Y_{x_{\eta}},M_{Y_{x_{\eta}}})/K),

is identical to the Hyodo–Kato isomorphism map
\rho_{\pi}
in
[
HK94
, Thm. 5.1]
.

Proof.

Set
\mathcal{E}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R^{i}f_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{s},M_{Y_{s}})/W}[1/p]
. Then Part
(ii)
of
Proposition

2.29
says that
\mathcal{E}
is an
F
-isocrystal on
(X_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, and Part
(i)
says that the restriction
x_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}
which is an
F
-isocrystal on
({\mathrm{Spec}}(k),(0{N})^{a})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
(or equivalently a
(\varphi,N)
-module over
K_{0}
) is indeed canonically isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}((Y_{x_{s}},M_{Y_{x_{s}}})/(W,(0{N})^{a}))[1/p]
.

To prove the proposition we closely follow the argument of
[
HK94
, Thm 5.1]
.
Without loss of generality, let us assume that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
,
X_{s}={\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{s})
and
X_{0}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
are all affine.
Let
\varphi_{R_{0}}
be the lift of the absolute Frobenius
F_{R_{s}}
of
R_{s}
and
\varphi_{R_{0}\{u\}}
be the endomorphism of
R_{0}\{u\}
which acts as
\varphi_{R_{0}}
on
R_{0}
and sends
u
to
u^{p}
.
Similarly, let
\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}}
be the Frobenius endomorpshim on
\mathfrak{S}=W[\![u]\!]
which sends
u
to
u^{p}
and acts as
\sigma
on
W
.
Let
D
be the log-PD envelope of the exact surjection
(\mathfrak{S},(u{N})^{a})\to(\mathcal{O}_{K},(\pi{N})^{a})
given by sending
u
to
\pi
(cf.
Example

2.6
) and set
D_{R_{0}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(D\otimes_{W}R_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
.
Choose
r\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large such that
p\mid\pi^{r}
in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Then we obtain a commutative diagram

\hbox to145.88pt{\vbox to52.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 72.23343pt\lower-24.73613pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
