\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

By taking the log PD-envelops of the vertical surjections, we obtain a morphism
W\{u\}\to D
, which we denote by
g
, following the notation of
loc. cit.
Note that
g
induces a morphism
R_{0}\{u\}\to D_{R_{0}}
by base change, which we denote by the same letter.

By
Theorem

2.21
, there exists a unique Frobenius equivariant isomorphism

s:\mathcal{E}(R_{0}\{u\},R_{s},(u{N})^{a})\simeq\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{R_{0}}R_{0}\{u\}.

When
Y={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is just a point, the above becomes an isomorphism

s:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}((Y_{s},M_{Y_{s}})/(W,(0{N})^{a}))\otimes_{W}W\{u\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{H}^{i}((Y_{s},M_{Y_{s}})/(W\{u\},(u{N})^{a})).

Alternatively, one obtains such an isomorphism by applying
[
HK94
, Prop. 4.13]
. More precisely, the result in
loc. cit.
produces a Frobenius-equivariant isomorphism between two complexes of sheaves on
Y_{s,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
, which gives an isomorphism as above after we take
\mathrm{H}^{i}(Y_{s,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}},-)
.

We define an isomorphism
h_{\pi}
using the following commutative diagram of the isomorphisms.

(21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigr(\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{R_{0}}R_{0}\{u\}\bigr)\otimes_{g}D_{R_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}(R_{0}\{u\},R_{s},(u{N})^{a})\otimes_{g}D_{R_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{\varphi^{r}_{R_{0}}}D_{R_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}(D_{R_{0}},R_{s},(u{N})^{a})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varphi^{r}_{R_{0}})^{*}\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{R_{0}}D_{R_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{R_{0}}D_{R_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\otimes_{g}D_{R_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ast)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi^{r}\otimes 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\pi}}

Here in the diagram
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}^{r}:(\varphi^{r}_{R_{0}})^{*}\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}(R_{0},R_{s},(0{N})^{a})
is the Frobenius isomorphism given by the
F
-isocrystal structure of
\mathcal{E}
. The isomorphism
(\ast)
is induced by
g
and the crystalline nature of
\mathcal{E}
, viewed as a morphism
(R_{0}\{u\},R_{s},(u{N})^{a})\to(D_{R_{0}},R_{s},(u{N})^{a})
between objects in
(X_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\textrm{$\ell$-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
One checks that the definition of
h_{\pi}
is independent of the choice of
r
. We then define an isomorphism
\mathcal{E}(X_{0},X_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{K_{0}}K\simeq\mathcal{E}(X,X_{p=0},M_{X}=(\pi{N})^{a})
by the
K
-linearization of the following maps

(22)

\mathcal{E}(X_{0},X_{s},(0{N})^{a})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}(X_{0},X_{s},(0{N})^{a})\otimes_{R_{0}}D_{R_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{\pi}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}(D_{R_{0}},R_{s},(u{N})^{a})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u\mapsto\pi}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}(X,X_{s},(\pi{N})^{a}).

Now, using
[
Kat89
, Thm 6.4]
(see also the paragraph above
[
Shi08
, Thm 2.36]
), one obtains a canonical isomorphism

R^{i}f_{Y_{p=0}/X,{\mathrm{crys}}*}(\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{p=0},M_{Y_{p=0}})/(X,M_{X})})\simeq R^{i}f_{*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Y,M_{Y})/(X,M_{X})}

where
\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Y,M_{Y})/(X,M_{X})}
is the (relative) log de Rham complex of the morphism
f
. After inverting
p
, one obtains an isomorphism

(23)

\mathcal{E}(X,X_{s},M_{X})\simeq R^{i}f_{\eta*}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Y_{\eta},M_{Y_{\eta}})/X_{\eta}},

which finishes the proof of Part
(i)
.

For Part
(ii)
, we note that for the given point
x_{0}\in X_{0}(W)
, there is a commutative diagram of log schemes

(24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{Spf}}(W),(0{N})^{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}),(\pi{N})^{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{s},(0{N})^{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{0},(0{N})^{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,M_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{s},(0{N})^{a}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{s}}

Therefore, one indeed obtains an isomorphism as in (
20
) from (
19
) via a base change.
Then one can check readily that the construction of (
23
) agrees with the construction of
\rho_{\pi}
in
[
HK94
, Thm. 5.1]
.
Here the essential point is that the diagram
[
HK94
, (5.2.1)]
about certain complexes of sheaves on
Y_{s,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
gives rise to diagram (
21
) after we take
\mathrm{H}^{i}(Y_{s,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}},-)
, and we have chosen notations to facilitate the comparison.
∎

3.
Period sheaves

We recall the construction and the properties for the crystalline horizontal period sheaf and the crystalline period sheaf with connection in this section, and introduce their semi-stable analogues, for a rigid space with good reduction.

3.1.
Horizontal period sheaves

We first recall the construction of horizontal period sheaves and study their basic properties. Before doing so, we first discuss horizontal period rings. We assume the basics of perfectoid algebras as in
[
Sch13a
]
.
As before, we let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be the ring of integers of some fixed
p
-adic field with a perfect residue field
k
, and recall our conventions in
Section

1.7
.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-algebra. Let
S^{\flat}
be the tilt
\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}S
.
Equip
S
with the standard log structure
M_{S}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}S\cap S[1/p]^{\times}
.

(i)

Set
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W(S^{\flat})
, which is equipped with a canonical surjection
\theta:{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to S
, sending the Teichmüller lift
[(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots)]\in W(S^{\flat})
onto the element
x_{0}
.
Let
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)
be the completion of
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)[1/p]
at
\ker(\theta)
, and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
be the localization
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)[1/\mu]
.
Recall that the constant
\mu
was introduced in
Section

1.7
.

(ii)

Let
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
be the
p
-completion of the PD-envelope of the surjection
\theta
. Set
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)[1/p]
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)[1/\mu]={\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)[1/\mu]
.

(iii)

Let
M_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
be the log structure on
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
associated to the monoid
\theta^{-1}(M_{S})
, and let
M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)}
be the log structure induced by
M_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
.

(iv)

The Frobenius operator
\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
induces Frobenius operators
\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)},\varphi_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)}
and
\varphi_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)}
on the respective rings.

Remark 3.2
.

The pro log PD envelope of the map
\theta
, when viewed as a surjection of log pairs
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),M_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)})\to(S,M_{S})
, is precisely
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}})
, because
\theta
is by construction exact.
As a consequence, the triple
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}})
forms an object in
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.

Recall from
Example

2.6
that we introduced the ring
D\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E(u))^{\wedge}_{p}
, the
p
-complete PD-envelope for the surjection
\mathfrak{S}=W\llbracket u\rrbracket\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
. We use
M_{D}
to denote the induced log structure of
M_{\mathfrak{S}}=(u{N})^{a}
on
D
.
Then the triple
(D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E(u))^{\wedge}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathfrak{S}})
forms a pro-object in the log crystalline site
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})_{{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}}
that is weakly initial.
Let
\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}}
be the Frobenius operator on
\mathfrak{S}
which acts as
\sigma
on
W
and sends
u
to
u^{p}
.

Now we construct the semi-stable period rings.

Construction 3.3
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-algebra.
Define
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
to be the
p
-complete log PD envelope of the surjection

\bigl((\mathrm{A}_{\inf}(S),M_{\mathrm{A}_{\inf}(S)})\otimes_{W}(\mathfrak{S},M_{\mathfrak{S}})\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\xrightarrow{\theta\otimes(\text{mod}~E(u))}(S,M_{S}).

Let
\varphi_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}
be the Frobenius operator on
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
induced by
\varphi_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)}\otimes\varphi_{\mathfrak{S}}
on the left hand side above.
To compute
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
explicitly, we first form the exact localization of the the source by slightly adapting
Lemma

2.1
.
By
Definition

3.1
, the map of monoids
M_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)}\to M_{S}
induces an equivalence of associated log structures on the ring
S
.
Moreover, since the image of
u\in M_{\mathfrak{S}}
in
S
coincides with that of
[\pi^{\flat}]\in\mathrm{A}_{\inf}(S)
, the
p
-complete exact localization for the map of log pairs above is given by

\bigl({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\otimes_{W}\mathfrak{S}[\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]},\frac{[\pi^{\flat}]}{u}]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}.

To get the ring
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
, we then take the
p
-complete divided power envelope of the surjection

\bigl({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\otimes_{W}\mathfrak{S}[\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]},\frac{[\pi^{\flat}]}{u}]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow S.

Notice that since the kernel of the above map is generated by
\ker(\theta)
and
u/[\pi^{\flat}]-1
, we can rewrite the ring
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
as the
p
-complete divided power polynomial for the variable
u/[\pi^{\flat}]-1
, namely

{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\simeq{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\langle\frac{(u/[\pi^{\flat}]-1)^{n}}{n!},n\in\mathbb{N}\rangle={\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}-1\}.

Here the log structure
M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}(S)
is by construction associated to the map of monoids

\left(M_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}\times u{N}\right)\coprod_{[\pi^{\flat}]{N}\times u{N}}N^{(1)}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S),

with
N^{(1)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{u^{x}\cdot(\pi^{\flat})^{y}~|~x+y\geq 0\}
being the submonoid of
u{Z}\times(\pi^{\flat}){Z}
.

We also note that by construction and
Remark

3.2
, the associated log divided power thickening
({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)})
is naturally identified with the coproduct of
(D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E(u)),\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathfrak{S}})
and
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})
in the log crystalline site
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, fitting into a diagram of pro log PD thickenings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(D_{\mathfrak{S}}(E(u)),\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathfrak{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}).}

Similar to
Construction

2.10
, we can define a monodromy operator on
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
that is linear with respect to
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
.

Construction 3.4

(Monodromy operator on
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}
)
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-algebra.
By
Construction

2.10
, the Čech nerve of the covering object
(D,\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D})
in
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is isomorphic to the cosimplicial complete log divided power polynomials

(D^{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D^{\bullet}})=(D\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{\bullet}\},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,\{u^{a_{0}}\cdots u_{\bullet}^{a_{\bullet}}~|~\sum a_{i}\geq 0\}^{a}),

where
t_{i}
is the formal variable corresponding to the element
u/u_{i}-1
.
On the other hand, by
Remark

3.2
, we can associate the perfectoid ring
S
with the log pair
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})
, such that the triple
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})
forms an object in
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
In particular, we can form the coproduct of the cosimplicial objects
(D^{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D^{\bullet}})
with
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})
in the log crystalline site
(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/p})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, to get the
cosimplicial object
({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet},S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet}})
, which fits into a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(D^{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D^{\bullet}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet},S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet}}).}

Moreover, by
Construction

3.3
, the zero-th term
({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{0},S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{0}})
is naturally isomorphic to

({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)})\simeq({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}-1\},S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}).

The explicit construction of
(D^{\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{K}/p,M_{D^{\bullet}})
in
Lemma

2.9
further provides an identification

({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet},S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet}})\simeq({\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{\bullet}\},S/p,M_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{\bullet}}),

such that the two coface maps
p_{0},p_{1}:{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\to{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)^{1}\simeq{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\{t_{1}\}
send a power series
f(u)\in{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)={\mathrm{A_{crys}}}\{u/[\pi^{\flat}]-1\}
onto the elements
f(u)
and
f(\frac{u}{t_{1}+1})
respectively in
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\{t_{1}\}={\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\{u/[\pi^{\flat}]-1,t_{1}\}
.

Now similar to
Construction

2.10
, since the difference map
p_{0}-p_{1}
has image in
t_{1}\cdot{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\{t_{1}\}
, we define the
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
-linear endomorphism
N_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}
as the composite of the following maps:

{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{0}-p_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}t_{1}\cdot{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\{t_{1}\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mod t_{1}^{[\geq 2]}}}{{\longrightarrow}}t_{1}\cdot{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\simeq{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)

When
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
is viewed as a subring of the power series ring
\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)[\![u]\!]
, the endomorphism
N_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}
is given by the formula
f(u)\mapsto uf^{\prime}(u)
, similar to the endomorphism
N_{D}
in
Construction

2.10
.
In particular, the kernel of
N_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}
is
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
, and by induction, the submodule of nilpotent elements is given by
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)[\log{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}]
, and is isomorphic to the polynomial of one variable over
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
.
Moreover, by explicit calculations, one check immediately that Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}
and monodromy operator
N_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)}
satisfy the formula that
N_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}\varphi_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}=p\varphi_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}N_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-algebra.
We define
({\mathrm{A_{st}}}(S),M_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}(S)})
to be the log pair given by the ring
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)[\log{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}]
together with the log structure induced by
M_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
.
It is equipped with an
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
-linear nil endomorphism
N_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}(S)}
that vanishes on
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
, together with a natural Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}}
that is compatible with
\varphi_{{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}}
, satisfying
N_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}}\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}}=p\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}}N_{{\mathrm{A_{st}}}}
.

Remark 3.6
.

The subring
{\mathrm{A_{st}}}(S)\subseteq{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)
is
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
-invariant because
\sigma\in{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
acts on
{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}
by

\log{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}\mapsto\log{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}-\chi(g)\cdot\log{\epsilon},

where
\chi
denotes the cyclotomic character.
Note that
\chi(g)\cdot\log{\epsilon}\in{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
.

Finally, we summarize the relation of various horizontal period rings in the following.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-algebra.
Then there are natural maps of horizontal period rings

{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S),

that are functorial with respect to
S
, and are compatible with Frobenius structures and log structures whenever defined.
Moreover, the first two maps are injective, and the last map induces an injection from
{\mathrm{A_{st}}}(S)
into
\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
.

Proof.

The first map exists as
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
is the
p
-complete divided power envelope of
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
with respect to
\theta
(cf.
Definition

3.1
).
The injectiveness of the first map follows from the fact that the ideal
\ker(\theta)
is generated by a non-zero-divisor
[
Sch13a
, Lem. 6.3]
.
The second map together with its injectiveness follows from the explicit formula
Construction

3.3
.
To construct the third map, we first notice that by construction, the map
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
naturally extends to an injection
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\to\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
(
[
TT19
, Prop. 2.23]
), which further extends to a map from
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
-polynomial ring

f:{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)[u]\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S),\quad u\longmapsto[\pi^{\flat}],

where
\ker(f)=(u-[\pi^{\flat}])
.
Notice that since the canonical surjection
\theta:\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\to S[1/p]
sends
[\pi^{\flat}]
onto the invertible element
\pi
, the map
f
naturally extends to a map

g:{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)[\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}-1]\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S).

Moreover, the image of
u/[\pi^{\flat}]-1
under the map
g
admits all
p
-adic divided power power series.
Hence the map
g
naturally extends to a map

{\mathrm{\widehat{A}_{st}}}(S)\simeq{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}-1\}\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S).

The latter in particular sends the element
\log{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}
onto
\log{\frac{\pi}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}\in\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
.
Finally, to check the induced map
{\mathrm{A_{st}}}(S)\to\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
is injective, by the explicit formula in
Construction

3.4
, it suffices to check that the element
\log{\frac{\pi}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}\in\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)
is transcendental over the subring
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
.
This then follows from
[
Shi22
, Prop. 2.27]
, where the ring
\mathrm{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{max}}(S)
(which in loc. cit. is denoted as
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{max}}(\Lambda)
for
\Lambda=S
) contains our
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)
as a subring (
[
Shi22
, Lem. 2.34]
).
∎

Now we are ready to sheafify the above constructions.
We fix a
p
-torsionfree
p
-adic formal scheme
X
that is topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, together with the standard log structure
M_{X}
as in
Example

2.7
.
In particular, the log structure
M_{X_{\eta}}
on the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
is trivial.
Here we emphasize that
X
is not assumed to be regular.

We implicitly assume the equivalence for the category of topoi
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})\simeq{\mathrm{Shv}}({\mathrm{Perfd}}/X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
, where
{\mathrm{Perfd}}/X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
is the site defined over the category of affinoid perfectoid objects over
X_{\eta}
and is equipped with the pro-étale topology (cf.
[
Sch13a
, Lem. 4.6, Prop. 4.8]
).
We first recall various structure sheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
. Let
\nu:X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}\to X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
be the natural projection. Let
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\nu^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}}
be the uncompleted structure sheaf on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, with subring of integral elements
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}
. Then we define

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}/p^{n},~\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\eta}}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}[1/p],\text{ and }\mathcal{O}^{\flat+}_{X_{\eta}}=\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}/p.

For an object
V\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}/X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
with
\widehat{V}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
, we have (
[
Sch13a
, Lem. 4.10, 5.10]
)

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}(\widehat{V})=S,~\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\eta}}(\widehat{V})=S[1/p],\text{ and }\mathcal{O}^{\flat+}_{X_{\eta}}(\widehat{V})=S^{\flat}.

We also define a natural log structure on
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}
by
M_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}\cap(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\eta}})^{\times}
, which by construction is compatible with
M_{X}
.

Definition 3.8
.

The sheaf of rings
\mathbb{A}_{\inf}
together with its log structure
M_{\mathbb{A}_{\inf}}
is defined as follows: Given any object
V\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}/X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
with
\widehat{V}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
, we set (cf.
[
Sch13a
, Def. 6.1, Thm. 6.5]
)

(\mathbb{A}_{\inf},M_{\mathbb{A}_{\inf}})(V)=({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),M_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}).

In a completely analogous way, we define sheaves

?=\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}},\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}

on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
from corresponding constructions on
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-algebras.
10
10

10

These constructions a priori only define pro-étale sheaves over
X_{\eta,C}
. But the resulting sheaves carry natural
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
-actions, with which they are naturally viewed as pro-étale sheaves on
X_{\eta}
.

We denote the log structures on
?
by
M_{?}
, the Frobenius operators on
?=\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
by
\varphi_{?}
, and monodromy operator on
?=\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
by
N_{?}
.

Remark 3.9
.

Recall that the log structure
M_{X_{\eta}}
over the rigid space
X_{\eta}
is trivial by assumption.
So for any affine open subscheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subseteq X
and any affinoid perfectoid space
{\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R)_{\eta}
, the structure map
R\to S
canonically lifts to a map of log pairs
(R,M_{R})\to(S,M_{S})
.
In particular, we get the following natural diagram of ringed sites with log structures

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},(\mathcal{O}_{X},M_{X})\bigr)\longleftarrow\bigl(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}},M_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}})\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigl((X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},(\mathbb{A}_{\inf},M_{\mathbb{A}_{\inf}})\bigr).}

Proposition

3.19
sheafifies to the following statement.

Corollary 3.10
.

Let
(X,M_{X})
be a topological finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
(\mathcal{O}_{K},M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})
, such that
M_{X_{\eta}}
is trivial.
There are natural injections of period sheaves over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
that are compatible with Frobenius structures and log structures whenever defined:

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}[\log{\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]}}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

Similar statements hold for their localizations after we inverting
p
or
\mu
.

3.2.
Period sheaves with connection

In this subsection, we recall the construction of the crystalline and the de Rham period sheaves with connection, and introduce their semi-stable analogue,
for a smooth and affine
p
-adic formal scheme.
This will be used to define an explicit functor sending crystalline and semi-stable local systems to their associated
F
-isocrystals.

We start by fixing the setup for the
p
-adic formal scheme in this subsection.

Convention 3.11
.

We fix a smooth and affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
By deformation theory, there is a
p
-adic formal scheme
X_{0}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
that is smooth affine over
W
, together with a base change isomorphism
R_{0}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}\simeq R
.
Moreover, by the complete smoothness of
R_{0}
over
W
, the Frobenius endomorphism of
R_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}k=R_{0}\otimes_{W}k
lifts to an endomorphism
\varphi_{R_{0}}:R_{0}\to R_{0}
that is semi-linear with respect to
\varphi_{W}
.
As in
Example

2.7
, we let
M_{X}
and
M_{X_{0}}
be the standard log structures on
X
and
X_{0}
respectively.
Here we note that
M_{X}
and
M_{X_{0}}
restricts to the same log structure
M_{X_{s}}=(0{N})^{a}
on the reduced special fiber
X_{s}
.
Then we have a natural isomorphism of log pairs
(X_{0},M_{X_{0}})\times_{({\mathrm{Spf}}(W),M_{W})}({\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}),M_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})\simeq(X,M_{X})
.
We also let
X_{\eta}
be the generic fiber of
X
, where the induced log structure
M_{X_{\eta}}
is trivial; similarly for
X_{0,\eta}
and
M_{X_{0,\eta}}
.

In addition, we say
X
is
small
if there is a
p
-completely étale morphism onto a
p
-adic torus
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,x_{r}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
.
Here we note that for a general smooth
p
-adic formal scheme, the collection of its small affine open sub formal schemes form a basis for the Zariski topology.

We now recall the construction of crystalline period sheaves with connection, essentially following
[
TT19
]
with some adjustments due to the ramification.

Definition 3.12
.

Assume the setup of smooth affine
X
in
Convention

3.11
.

(i)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
with its log structure
M_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
, connection
\nabla
, and Frobenius structure
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
, is given as follows

•

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is the
p
-completion of the PD-envelope of the surjection

\bigr(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}\otimes_{W}R_{0}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\xrightarrow{\theta\otimes\iota}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}},

where
\iota
is the composed structure map
R_{0}\to R\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}
.

•

The log structure
M_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is induced from the tensor product log structure of
\bigl(({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}},M_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})\otimes_{W}(R_{0},M_{R_{0}})\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}
.

•

The connection
\nabla:{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}/W}
is the one induced from the differential map
R_{0}\to\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}/W}
.

•

The Frobenius structure
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
is induced from the tensor product Frobenius
\varphi_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}\otimes_{\varphi_{W}}\varphi_{R_{0}}
on
({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\otimes_{W}R_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
.
It is horizontal with respect to
\nabla
.

(ii)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
is defined by inverting the element
p
on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
, with the induced log structure, the connection, and the Frobenius structure.

(iii)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
is given on an object
V\in({\mathrm{Perfd}}/X_{\eta,C})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with
\widehat{V}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
, by
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)={\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)[1/\mu]
.
It is equipped with the induced log structure, the connection, and the Frobenius structure.

Remark 3.13
.

By construction (cf.
[
TT19
, Def. 2.9]
), the sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
is nothing but the pullback (or restriction) of the sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}((X_{0,\eta})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
of loc. cit. along the natural étale covering map of rigid spaces
X_{\eta}\to X_{0,\eta}
.
In particular, various structural results of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
in loc. cit. applies naturally to our
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
.

By
Definition

3.8
and
Definition

3.12
, there is a natural map of log pairs of sheaves

({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}},M_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}})\longrightarrow({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}},M_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}),

which is compatible with Frobenius structure and has image in
({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})^{\nabla=0}
.
The following results of Tan–Tong and Guo–Li give a more concrete description of the relation.

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
X
be as in
Convention

3.11
.

(i)

[
TT19
, Cor. 2.17]

The connection
\nabla
of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
naturally induces an isomorphism in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
:

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\simeq({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})^{\nabla=0}.

(ii)

[
TT19
, Prop. 2.13]

Assume
X
is
p
-completely étale over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,x_{r}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
, and let
V
be the affinoid perfectoid pro-étale cover of
X_{\eta}
given by
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\langle x_{i}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)_{\eta}\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{i}^{\pm 1}\rangle)_{\eta}}X_{\eta}
.
When restricted onto
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{V}
, the natural map
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
induces an isomorphism of sheaves of rings

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}|_{V}\{v_{i}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}|_{V},\text{ given by }v_{i}\mapsto\frac{1\otimes x_{i}}{[x_{i}^{\flat}]\otimes 1}-1\in{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(V)

for a fixed choice of
p
-power roots
x_{i}^{\flat}
of

.

(iii)

[

Let

R_{0}

X
along
R_{0}\to R_{0}^{\prime}

which respects the Frobenius structures and connections

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}|_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}.

We then recall the construction of

of Scholze

Definition 3.15
.

Let
X
be a topologically finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
with smooth generic fiber.

(i)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}

•

The sheaf

where

\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}\to\Omega^{1}_{X_{\eta}/K}

(ii)

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}

.
It is equipped with the induced connection.

Finally, we introduce the semi-stable period sheaves with connections.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
X
be as in
Convention

3.11
.

(i)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
with its log structure
M_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
, connection
\nabla
, Frobenius structure
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}

•

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
is defined as the tensor product

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}},

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\to{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}
.

•

The Frobenius structure
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}

\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}\otimes_{\varphi_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}}}\varphi_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}}
.

•

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}
-linear endomorphism

of
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}
along the map
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
.

(ii)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
is defined by inverting the element
p
at
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
, with the induced log structure, the connection, the Frobenius structure, and the monodromy operator.

(iii)

The sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
is given on an object
V\in({\mathrm{Perfd}}/X_{\eta,C})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)={\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)[1/\mu]

It is equipped with the induced log structure, the connection, the Frobenius structure, and the monodromy operator.

As quick consequences of the explicit construction in
Construction

3.4
, we get the following properties of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
.

Corollary 3.18
.

Let
X
be as in
Convention

3.11
.
The restriction of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
to
(X_{\eta,C})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
-equivariantly identified with the polynomial ring of one variable
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}[\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])]
over
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
, such that the following properties hold.

(i)

The Frobenius structure
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
acts as
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and sends
\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])
to
p\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])
.

(ii)

The monodromy operator
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
is the unique
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
-linear endomorphism which vanishes on the subring
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
, and sends
\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}
to
i\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i-1}
.
In particular
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
is a nil operator.

(iii)

Both
\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
and
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
are both horizontal with respect to the connection
\nabla
, and satisfy the formula that

N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}=p\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}.

(iv)

Let
R^{\prime}_{0}
be a
p
-completely étale algebra over
R_{0}
, and let
X^{\prime}
be the base change of
X
along
R_{0}\to R_{0}^{\prime}
.
We let
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R^{\prime}_{0}}
be the sheaf in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
defined for the data
(X^{\prime},R_{0}^{\prime})
.
Then we have a natural isomorphism of sheaves over
X^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
identifying all the structures

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}|_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R^{\prime}_{0}}.

Moreover, assume
X
is
p
-completely étale over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,x_{r}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
, and let
V
be the affinoid perfectoid pro-étale cover of
X
given by
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\langle x_{i}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}\rangle)_{\eta}\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{i}^{\pm 1}\rangle)_{\eta}}X_{\eta}
.

(v)

The restriction
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}|_{V}
admits an isomorphism with
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}|_{V}\{v_{i}\}[\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])]
, compatible with that in
Proposition

3.14
.
(ii)
.

As in
Proposition

3.7
, the various period sheaves with connections naturally relate to each other.
This is summarized in the following statement.

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
X
be as in
Convention

3.11
.
There is a natural commutative diagram of injections of period sheaves in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}

with the following properties:

(i)

The arrows in the bottom row respect connections, and the top row is obtained from the second row by taking horizontal sections.

(ii)

The first square is a pushout diagram, and the arrows respect the Frobenius structures. In addition, the first column is obtained from the second by taking the
N=0
sections.

In addition, there is an entirely similar conclusion after we invert
p
or
\mu
in the above diagram.

Proof.

Let us first forget the third column and consider the rest of the diagram.
By
Definition

3.17
, the sheaf of rings
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
is defined as the pushout of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}
over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Moreover, both
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}
admit maps to
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
, through
Proposition

3.16
.
(iii)
and
Corollary

3.10
separately.
So to get a map from
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
to
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
, it suffices to check that the above two maps are compatible after restricted to
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}
.
This, together with the injectiveness, can then be done pro-étale locally, and follows from the explicit descriptions of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
as in
Corollary

3.18
.
(v)
and
Proposition

3.16
.
(ii)
respectively.
The rest of the claims follow from
Corollary

3.18
.

As the sheaves
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
are
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-linear, the natural map from
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
naturally extends to their
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-linear extension.
To show the injectiveness of the maps
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}\to\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
, by
Corollary

3.18
.
(v)
and
Proposition

3.16
.
(ii)
again it suffices to consider the map of horizontal period sheaves.
This then follows from
[
Shi22
, Prop. 2.27]
, where
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{max}}
contains
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
as a subring (
[
Shi22
, Lem. 2.34]
).
∎

Remark 3.20
.

As in
Section

3.1
, we can consider the complete semi-stable period sheaf with connection
\mathcal{O}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
, defined as the complete coproduct of the log PD-thickenings
({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}},\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{\eta}}/p,M_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}})
and
(D_{R_{0}\llbracket u\rrbracket}(E),R/p,(u{N})^{a})
in
(R/p,M_{R/p})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Here
R_{0}\llbracket u\rrbracket
is the higher dimensional Breuil–Kisin ring associated with the
W
-model
R_{0}
.
Just like how we construct
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}
in
Section

3.1
, we can then take the submodule of nilpotent elements under the monodromy operator of
\mathcal{O}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
.
One can check that this latter construction coincides with
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
in
Definition

3.17
.

Finally, we consider the pro-étale cohomology for period sheaves with connections.
In the following, we let
\nu:X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}\to X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
be the natural map of sites,
and by abuse of notation we denote the restriction of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}
to
X_{\eta}
still by
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}
when this implication is clear.

Theorem 3.21

(Zero-th cohomology of period sheaves)
.

Let
X
be as in
Convention

3.11
.

(i)

There is a natural isomorphism of sheaves of rings over
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
that identifies their connections:

\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}\longrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

(ii)

There is a natural Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of sheaves of rings over
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
that identifies their connections:

\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}\longrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}.

(iii)

There is a natural Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of sheaves of rings over
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
that identifies their connections:

\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}\longrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}.

Proof.

Part
(i)
was shown in
[
Sch13a
, Cor. 6.19, Thm 7.6]
.
To get the other two parts, we first notice that by explicit constructions in
Proposition

3.14
.
(ii)
and
Corollary

3.18
.
(v)
, as well as
Proposition

3.19
, there are natural injections

(25)

\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

Moreover, by
Corollary

3.18
.
(iv)
and the fact that
\nu_{*}
is left exact, we get the following injections of sheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}

(26)

\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}\hookrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\hookrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\hookrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

The maps are compatible with connections and Frobenius structures whenever applicable.
Moreover, since the sheaf of rings
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is
K
-linear, by tensoring (
26
) with
K
we obtain another sequence

(27)

\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}=\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\hookrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\hookrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\longrightarrow\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

The injectivity of all but the last arrow in (
27
) is clear, and we claim that the last arrow is also injective.
By the explicit constructions in
Proposition

3.14
.
(ii)
,
Corollary

3.18
.
(v)
, and
Proposition

3.16
.
(ii)
we have isomorphisms

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\simeq\bigl(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\bigr)\{v_{i}\},~{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\simeq\bigl(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\bigr)\{v_{i}\},~~{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}\simeq\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\llbracket v_{i}\rrbracket,

where the natural maps among them are compatible with the formal variables
v_{i}
and are induced from
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\to\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}\to\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
.
So to show the claimed injectivity, it suffices to show the injectivity of the following maps

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\longrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\longrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

This then follows for example from
[
Shi22
, Prop. 2.27]
, where
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{max}}
contains
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
as a subring (
[
Shi22
, Lem. 2.34]
).

Now, given that the morphisms in (
27
) are all injective, we see that the algebra of global sections
\Gamma(X_{\eta},\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})
is a
R_{0}[1/p]
-subalgebra of
\Gamma(X_{\eta},\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}})\simeq R[1/p]
whose
K
-linearization is a subalgebra of
R[1/p]
as well.
Notice that since
R[1/p]=R_{0}[1/p]\otimes_{K_{0}}K
, the above implies that the inclusion
R_{0}[1/p]\to\Gamma(X_{\eta},\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})
becomes an isomorphism after tensoring with
K
.
As a consequence, thanks to the full faithfulness of the map
K_{0}\to K
, we see the ring
\Gamma(X_{\eta},\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})
must be equal to
R_{0}[1/p]
. This proves
(ii)
and
(iii)
follows similarly.
∎

Here we note that by taking the submodules of horizontal sections, we get cohomology of horizontal period sheaves as below.
In the following, we let
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(K_{0})}
be the preimage of the étale structure sheaf of
{\mathrm{Spa}}(K_{0})
along the map
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}\to{\mathrm{Spa}}(K_{0})_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Corollary 3.22
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

(i)

The natural map
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(K_{0})}\to\nu_{*}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
is a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism.

(ii)

The natural map
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(K_{0})}\to\nu_{*}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
is a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism.

(iii)

The natural map
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(K_{0})}\to\nu_{*}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

As the maps above are canonically defined, to check the isomorphisms it suffices to assume that
X
is smooth and affine, where the claims follow from
Proposition

3.19
,
Theorem

3.21
, and the observation that
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}^{\nabla=0}=K_{0}
.
∎

4.
Crystalline and semi-stable local systems

In this section, we give two definitions of crystalline and semi-stable local systems over the generic fiber of a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.
The first definition is in the spirit of Faltings (cf.
[
Fal89
]
), which is defined using horizontal period sheaves in
Section

3.1
and in particular allows a general setup where
X
is not regular.
In the special case when
X
is smooth, affine and when an unramified model is chosen (cf.
Convention

3.11
), we give the second definition by introducing the functors
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
on the category of
p
-adic local systems over
X_{\eta}
, using period sheaves with connections introduced in
Section

3.2
.
The second definition in particular comes with a
functoriality
for the
F
-isocrystals that are associated with the given crystalline or semi-stable local systems, which will be crucial for our eventual application.

4.1.
Crystallinity and semi-stability a la Faltings

This subsection is devoted to the definitions of crystalline local systems and semi-stable local systems for general
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, extending that of Faltings (cf.
[
Fal89
, p. 67]
).
We fix a topologically finite type
p
-adic formal schemes
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and as in
Example

2.7
we let
M_{X}
be its standard log structure.

We start with a construction of the vector bundles over horizontal period sheaves that arises from a given (locally free)
F
-isocrystal on the (log-)crystalline site (cf.
[
GR24
, Def. 2.25]
).

Construction 4.1
.

(i)

Let
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})
be an
F
-isocrystal on
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
, which naturally extends to an
F
-isocrystal on
X_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}
by
Proposition

2.12
.
(i)
.
Then we define the sheaves
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
as follows:
Let
U\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}/(X_{\eta,C})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
be any object and set
\widehat{U}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
. As the morphism
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)\to S/p
is a pro-PD-thickening with a Frobenius endomorphism in
X_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}
, it makes sense to set

•

\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})(U)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p)[1/p]=\bigl(\lim_{r}\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)/p^{r},S/p,\gamma)\bigr)[1/p]
, where
\gamma
is the canonical PD-structure and

•

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})(U)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})(U)[1/\mu]
.

Here as in
[
GR24
, Lem. 2.26]
,
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
(resp.
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
) forms a sheaf of modules over
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
(resp.
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
) and the sheaf condition follows from the crystal property together with the sheaf condition of
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
(resp.
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
).
One checks that
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(-)
(resp.
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(-)
) defines a functor
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})

Moreover, the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
naturally equips
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
with isomorphisms

\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}},\varphi}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}),\quad\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}},\varphi}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}).

(ii)

Similarly, given a locally free
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
(which by the equivalence in
Proposition

2.12
.
(ii)
can be identified with an
F
-isocrystal on
(X_{p=0},M_{X_{p=0}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
), we can consider the evaluation of
\mathcal{E}
at the pro-log-PD-thickening
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})
in
(X_{p=0},M_{X_{p=0}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
This defines vector bundles
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
in
{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
over
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
respectively, and both are equipped with Frobenius structures.

(iii)

In the above two situations, we define
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})
by

\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}},\quad\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}.

By construction,
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})
is equipped with the tensor product Frobenius structure
\varphi_{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes\varphi_{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}}}
and the monodromy operator
\mathrm{id}\otimes N_{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{st}}}
, and similarly for
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})
.
We also define
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E})
by

\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}},\quad\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

Remark 4.2
.

Here we note that the natural forgetful functor of sites
(X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}\to X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
sends the log PD-thickening
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p,M_{{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S)})
onto the PD-thickening
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p)
.
In particular, for a given
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})
, the associated
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundle
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
defined via
Construction

4.1
.
(i)
coincides with the
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundle
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
defined via
Construction

4.1
.
(ii)
,
where
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
is the
F
-isocrystal over the log crystalline site that is induced from
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
.

Remark 4.3

(Site-theoretic interpretation)
.

A more conceptual way of constructing
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
in
Construction

4.1
is to consider a functor of ringed topoi

c_{X}\colon(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}^{\sim},\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})\longrightarrow(X_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}^{\sim},\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{crys}}),

sending an affinoid perfectoid space
{\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
onto the pro-PD-thickening
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S),S/p)
.
The functor is cocontinuous and induces a morphism of ringed topoi
c_{X}
in the same direction (
[
Sta23
,
Tag 00XN
]
).
In particular, for an
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
over
X_{p=0}
(or equivalently over
X_{k}
by
Proposition

2.12
), the pullback
c_{X}^{*}\mathcal{E}
is by construction equal to the vector bundle
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
over the pro-étale site
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
The same applies to the logarithmic setting, which we shall not repeat.

Now we are ready to define the crystallinity and the semi-stability.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
X
be a topologically finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
M_{X}
be its standard log structure as in
Example

2.7
, and let
T
be a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
that admits a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice.

(i)

We call
T
is a
crystalline local system
(with respect to
X
) if there is a locally free
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})
, together with a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundles

\vartheta:\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T.

(ii)

We call
T
is a
semi-stable local system
(with respect to
X
) if there is a locally free
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
, together with a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundles

\vartheta:\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T.

We use
{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})
or
{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{st}}(X_{\eta})
to denote the full subcategory of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems over
X_{\eta}
that are crystalline or semi-stable with respect to the
X
separately.

From
Definition

4.4
, the only difference between the crystallinity and the semi-stability is that the
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
comes from either the crystalline site or the log crystalline site of the special fiber.
In particular, under the natural inclusion functor (
Corollary

2.26
), it is clear that a crystalline local system is semi-stable.

Remark 4.5

(Equivalent definition using
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
)
.

Assume the same setup as in
Definition

4.4
, the local system
T
is semi-stable if and only if there is an
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
, together with a Frobenius and monodromy equivariant isomorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
-vector bundles

\vartheta^{\prime}:\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T.

This follows as the monodromy operator on
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{E})=\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
is equal to
\mathrm{id}\otimes N_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}}
(
Construction

4.1
.
(iii)
), and the kernel of
N_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}}:\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}\to\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{st}}
is
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
(by
Definition

3.5
and
Construction

3.4
).

11
11

11

We thank Heng Du for explaining this equivalence to us.

We refer the reader to
Lemma

4.14
for a related discussion.

Remark 4.6

(Unnecessity of filtration)
.

The careful reader might notice that even when
X
is smooth, our definition of crystalline or semi-stable local systems above does not mention anything about filtration, which is different from either Faltings (
[
Fal89
]
) or Tan–Tong (
[
TT19
]
).
The idea is that the datum of filtration should be completely encoded and determined by the de Rhamness of the local system (where the latter is a purely generic condition and makes no reference to the choice of the integral model
X
), which is implied by the crystallinity or the semi-stability, as we will see very soon.
In fact when
X
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, it is shown in
[
GR24
, Prop. 2.38]
that the two versions of crystallinity coincide, and there is a canonical filtration on the
K
-linear base extension of the
F
-isocrystal such that it is unique in an appropriate sense.

Remark 4.7

(Independence of models)
.

We emphasize that the definitions of the crystallinity and the semi-stability in
Definition

4.4
a priori depend on the choice of the integral model
X
.
However, one readily tells from our criteria
Theorem

1.1
that for local systems over a smooth rigid space, the crystallinity (resp. the semi-stability) is independent of the choices of smooth (resp. semi-stable) models.
In fact, this independence statement can already be deduced from purity results, for which we refer the reader to
[
DLMS24
, Cor. 5.5]
.

We also comment on a natural variant for the de Rhamness.

Remark 4.8

(de Rham local system a la Faltings)
.

One can give a similar definition for the de Rham local system as in
Definition

4.4
and
Construction

4.1
as well, without referring to
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
: Namely, a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
with an integrable connection over
X_{\eta}
can be identified with a crystal
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
over the infinitesimal site
(X_{\eta}/K)_{\inf}
(cf.
[
Guo21
]
), and since
(\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\eta}})
is a pro-infinitesimal thickening (of sheaves) over
(X_{\eta}/K)_{\inf}
, one can naturally evaluate a crystal
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
at the thickening to get a
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-vector bundle
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
.
When
\mathcal{E}
is equipped with a locally split filtration satisfying Griffiths transversality, one can further enhance
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
with a natural filtration as well.

12
12

12

A better formulation, in light of recent advance of the stacky approach in
p
-adic geometry, is to identify
\mathcal{E}
with a coherent sheaf over the
analytic filtered de Rham stack

X_{\eta}^{{\mathrm{dR}}+}
, where the latter naturally admits a
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-valued point.
Taking the pullback functor onto the
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-point gives a filtered vector bundle over
\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
(hence over
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
).
We leave the details to interested readers.

Then the de Rhamness can be defined by requiring a filtered
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-linear isomorphism
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})\simeq T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
.
One can check using the rigidity of de Rham local systems (
[
LZ17
]
) that this notion coincides with the usual one, even when
X_{\eta}
is not smooth over
K
.
For our purposes, we would not pursue this definition further and will instead recall an alternative definition of Brinon and Scholze in next subsection.

4.2.
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors: local formulae

In this subsection, using the period sheaves with connection, we construct the
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors from
p
-adic local systems to their associated
F
-isocrystals, for a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.
This produces a local formula for the associated (log-)
F
-isocrystal in terms of a given
p
-adic local system
T
, and in particular is functorial with respect to
T
.

We start by recalling the following result of Liu–Zhu on
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert correspondence.
Recall the natural map of sites
\nu:X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}\to X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
as in
[
Sch13a
]
.

Theorem 4.9
.

[
LZ17
, Thm. 1.5, Thm. 3.9.(iv)]

Let
X_{\eta}
be a smooth rigid space over
K
.

(i)

There is a natural functor

D_{\mathrm{dR}}:\mathrm{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\nabla}(X_{\eta}),\quad T\longmapsto\nu_{*}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}),

where
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\nabla}(X_{\eta})
is the category of vector bundles with flat connection over
X_{\eta}
, and the connection of
D_{\mathrm{dR}}
is equal to
\nu_{*}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{T}\otimes\nabla_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}}}T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\eta}/K}\bigr)
.

(ii)

[
Bri08
, Prop. 8.2.5]
There is a natural injective linearization map of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-vector bundles that is compatible with their
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-linear connections

\alpha_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}:D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}\longrightarrow T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

Moreover, when
\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T)=\mathrm{rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}}(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T))
, the map is an isomorphism.

(iii)

The functor
D_{\mathrm{dR}}
is compatible with the pullback along any map of smooth rigid spaces.
In particular,
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
is a coherent sheaf over
X_{\eta}
.

The above in particular gives an equivalent definition for a
p
-adic local system to be de Rham (cf.
[
Sch13b
, Def. 8.3]
), which we recall as below.

Definition 4.10
.

Let
X_{\eta}
be a smooth rigid space over
K
.
A
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
is called
de Rham
if the rank of
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
is equal to
\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T)
.

Corollary 4.11

(Rigidity of de Rham local system)
.

Let
X_{\eta}
be a smooth connected rigid space over
K
.
A
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
is de Rham if and only if there is a closed point
x\in X_{\eta}
such that the restriction
T|_{x}
is a de Rham representation.

We then consider variants of the
D_{\mathrm{dR}}
functor that can be used to test the crystallinity and the semi-stability.
Below we use the equivalences in
Proposition

2.11
and
Corollary

2.26
to interpret an
F
-isocrystal as a finitely projective
R_{0}[1/p]
-module with integrable connection, Frobenius structure, and nilpotent endomorphism, and we recall the notations therein.

Theorem 4.12

(
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors)
.

Assume the setup of a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
as in
Convention

3.11
.

(i)

There is a natural left exact tensor functor

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\colon:{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi,\nabla_{X_{0}}}(X_{0,\eta}),\quad T\longmapsto\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\bigr)(X_{\eta}),

together with a functorial injection
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}:D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
that is compatible with Frobenius structures and connections.

(ii)

There is a natural left exact tensor functor

D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\colon{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi,\nabla_{X_{0}},N}(X_{0,\eta}),\quad T\longmapsto\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\bigr)(X_{\eta}),

together with a functorial injection
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}:D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
that is compatible with Frobenius structures, connections and nilpotent structures.

(iii)

There is a functorial Frobenius-equivariant inclusion of flat connections over
R_{0}[1/p]

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T),

which identifies
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
as the kernel of the nilpotent operator
N_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}
on
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
.
Moreover, their base extensions along
K_{0}\to K
naturally embed into the flat connection
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(X_{\eta})
.

Remark 4.13
.

Before we discuss the proof, we warn the reader that for general
p
-adic local system
T
, unlike
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
, which is a
coherent sheaf
over the rigid space
X_{\eta}
, neither
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
or
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
do not glue to a coherent sheaf in general.
However, we will see in
Theorem

5.5
that the ranks of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
and
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
will not decrease when restricted onto an open subspace, and both
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
and
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
are analytic local if their ranks are equal to
\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T)
.

Proof.

Since
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
is a coherent sheaf and
X
is assumed to be affine, below we do not distinguish between the sheaf
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
with its module of global sections
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(X_{\eta})
.

We start with Part
(i)
.
First we notice that there is a map
\nabla:D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\to D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}
defined by

\nu_{*}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}\bigr)(X_{\eta})
.
Indeed, since the coherent module
\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}
over
R_{0}[1/p]
is locally free, it follows from
Theorem

3.21
.
(ii)
that there is a natural isomorphism

(28)

\nu_{*}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}\bigr)(X_{\eta})\simeq D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}.

So we get a
K_{0}
-linear continuous map
\nabla:D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\to D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}
.

By the flatness of
T
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the construction in
Theorem

4.9
.
(i)
, and the fact that the injection
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is compatible with connections (
Proposition

3.19
), we see the map
\nabla
is a
K_{0}
-linear integrable connection and
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is a
R_{0}[1/p]
-submodule of
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
where the connections are compatible.
Hence by the finiteness of
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
over
R_{0}[1/p]
, the submodule together with the integrable connection
(D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T),\nabla)
is a flat connection over
R_{0}[1/p]
.
Note that since
{\mathrm{Spa}}(R_{0}[1/p])
is a smooth rigid space over
K_{0}
,
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is necessarily locally free, i.e., is a vector bundle.

In addition, the Frobenius endomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
induces on
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(\nu_{*}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})\bigr)(X_{\eta})
a
\varphi_{R_{0}}
-linear endomorphism
\widetilde{\varphi}_{D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)}
, and we denote the linearization of which by
\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)}:\varphi_{R_{0}}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\to D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
.
As the Frobenius structure of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is compatible with its connection, we see the linearized Frobenius structure
\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)}
commutes with the connection of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
as well.
Note that since
\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)}
is an injective horizontal morphism between two flat vector bundles of the same rank, it must be an isomorphism.
Hence the data
(D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T),\nabla,\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)})
defines an object in
\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi,\nabla_{X_{0}}}(X_{0,\eta})
.
Here we also remark that the construction of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
together with its additional structures is functorial in
T
.

By taking adjunction with respect to the map of ringed sites
\nu
,
we get a natural diagram of sheaves over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, which is functorial with respect to
T
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)\otimes_{R[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}}

So to show the injectiveness of
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
, it suffices to show the other three arrows are injective.
The right vertical map is an injection by
Proposition

3.19
and the flatness of
T
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
On the other hand, the injectiveness of
\alpha_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}\colon D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)\otimes_{R}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
follows from
Theorem

4.9
.
(ii)
.
Finally, the left vertical map
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)\otimes_{R}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
can be factored as

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}\longrightarrow D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)\otimes_{R[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}},

where the first map is injective by the local freeness of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
over
\mathcal{O}_{X_{0,\eta}}
and
Proposition

3.19
, and the second map is injective since
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K\to D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
is an injective and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}
.

For Part
(ii)
, the
R_{0}[1/p]
-module
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\nu_{*}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})
is equipped with a Frobenius structure
\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}
by that of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
, together with a map commuting with
\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}
:

\nabla\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\nu_{*}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}\bigr).

By the same reasoning as in
(i)
, the last map above is identified with a natural
K_{0}
-linear flat connection
\nabla:D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\to D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\Omega^{1}_{R_{0}[1/p]/K_{0}}
that commutes with
\varphi_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}
.
Moreover, the
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
-linear monodromy operator
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}:{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
induces a
\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}=R_{0}[1/p]
-linear nilpotent

13
13

13

Here the endomorphism is nilpotent because
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
is finitely generated over
R_{0}[1/p]
and
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
acts nilpotently on any section.

endomorphism
N_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}
on
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
, which commutes with the connection and the Frobenius structure.
So we get an object in
\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi,\nabla_{X_{0}},N}(X_{0,\eta})
, which by construction is functorial with respect to the local system
T
.
Furthermore, similar to the proof for
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
above, the canonical map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}:D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
is an injection.

Finally, we prove
(iii)
. Since
T
is locally free, the functor
T\otimes-
is exact. As the functor
\nu_{*}
is left exact, the exact sequence (cf.
Proposition

3.19
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}}

gives rise to another exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)}}

This proves the first statement. The second statement follows from a similar argument together with the fact that the canonical
K
-linear map
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}
is injective (
