).
∎

4.3.
Crystallinity and semi-stability via
D
-functors

After introducing the
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors, we show that the crystallinity and the semi-stability, in the sense of Faltings, can be translated into a rank condition on the value of the
D
-functors.
This extends the well-known formula from the case of the point to the smooth affine setting.

To facilitate the discussion of crystalline and semi-stable local systems, we need the following observations on vector bundles over
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Assume the setup of a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
as in
Convention

3.11
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{0}
be a finite projective module over
R_{0}[1/p]
equipped with a
R_{0}[1/p]
-linear nilpotent endomorphism
N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
.
Define
\mathcal{M}:={\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
and let
N:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
be the additive map defined by
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}+\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
.
Then
\mathcal{M}
is generated as an
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
-module by
\ker(N_{\mathcal{M}})
.

Proof.

To simplify the notations, we let
N_{1}
be the endomorphism
\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}\otimes N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{1}:={\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Then by
Corollary

3.18
, pro-étale locally the sheaf of ring
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
is naturally identified with the polynomial ring of one variable
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}[\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])]
over
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
.
This allows us to write any element
x\in\mathcal{M}
as a sum
\sum_{i=0}^{n}x_{i}\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}
, where
x_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
.
Choose
m\gg 0
such that
N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}^{m}=0
.
Since
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
kills
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and sends
\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}
onto
i\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i-1}
, by solving equations explicitly we find

(29)

\ker(N)=\mathrm{im}(\psi:\mathcal{M}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}),\textrm{ where }\psi:x_{0}\mapsto\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{(-N_{1})^{i}(x_{0})}{i!}\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}.

Now we let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
be the
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}
generated by
\ker(N)
.
To show the equality
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}
, it suffices to show that any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
in contained in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
We prove this by induction on the minimal number
n(x_{0})\in\mathbb{N}
such that
N_{1}^{n(x_{0})+1}(x_{0})=0
.
If
n(x_{0})=0
, then by definition
x_{0}
is in
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
For general
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
, we have
\psi(x_{0})\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and

x_{0}=\psi(x_{0})-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{(-N_{1})^{i}(x_{0})}{i!}\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}.

Notice that
n(N_{1}^{i}(x_{0}))<n(x_{0})
for each
i\geq 1
.
So by induction,
N_{1}^{i}(x_{0})\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
for each
i\geq 1
. Hence the above equality implies that
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition 4.15
.

Assume the setup of a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
as in
Convention

3.11
. Let
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
be an
F
-isocrystal over
(X_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
and
(\mathcal{M}_{0},\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}})
be the associated object in
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}(X_{0,\eta})
provided by
Corollary

2.26
.

(i)

There is a natural isomorphism of the following
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
-vector bundles with connection and Frobenius structure

•

\bigl(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}},\ \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes\nabla_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}},\ \varphi_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}\bigr)
;

•

\bigl({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0},\ \nabla_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}+\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},\ \varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}\otimes\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}\bigr)
.

In particular, by taking the horizontal sections, we get

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\simeq({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0})^{\nabla=0}.

(ii)

There is a natural isomorphism of the following
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
-vector bundles with connection, Frobenius structure, and monodromy operator

•

\bigl(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}},\ \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes\nabla_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}},\ \varphi_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes\varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}},\ \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\bigr)
;

•

\bigl({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0},\ \nabla_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}+\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},\ \varphi_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes\varphi_{\mathcal{M}_{0}},\ N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}+\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}\bigr).

In particular, by taking the horizontal sections for the kernel of the monodromy operators, we get

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\simeq({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0})^{\nabla=0,N=0}.

Proof.

When the
F
-isocrystal comes from
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
(i.e., when
N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}=0
), Part
(i)
was shown in the proof of
[
GR24
, Prop. 2.36.(i)]
, replacing the ring
R
in loc. cit. by our
R_{0}
(which is in particular smooth over
W
).
Part
(ii)
follows by taking the base change along
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
.
For
F
-isocrystal over
(X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, except for the monodromy operators, the proof for Part
(i)
follows from the equivalent description of the log
F
-isocrystals in
Corollary

2.26
.
Alternatively, one can also prove it similarly to
[
GR24
, Prop. 2.36.(i)]
, with the only difference being that we regard Equation (6) in loc. cit. as a diagram of log divided power thickenings: for
D(1)
and
R
their log structures are defined by the monoid
p{N}
, and for
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
the log structures are defined in
Definition

3.8
and

3.12
respectively.
To see the identification of the two monodromy operators, we know from
Lemma

4.14
that
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0}
is generated by the kernel of the nilpotent endomorphism
N=N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}+\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}\otimes N_{\mathcal{M}_{0}}
on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
In particular, since
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
is sent into
\ker(N)
under the natural morphism
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{M}_{0}
, the restriction of
N
on the
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
-submodule generated by
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
is exactly
\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}\otimes N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
.
∎

As a consequence of
Proposition

4.15
, there is a natural commutative diagram of categories as below (the categories
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})
and

are defined in the obvious way):

(30)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}(R_{0}[1/p]),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
Corollary

2.26
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}

where the left column does not depend on the choice of the unramified model
R_{0}
, and the rest of the diagram depends on the choice of

together with the isomorphism

.

In the following, we let
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})^{\circ}
and
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})^{\circ}
) be the full subcategories of

and
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})
defined by the essential image of the objects in the category
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
along the arrows in (
