).

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

(i)

The induced functor
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(-)

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})^{\circ}

is an exact equivalence.

(ii)

Assume
X
is affine and admits an unramified model
R_{0}
together with a Frobenius endomorphism.
The tensor product functor
\mathbb{M}\mapsto\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
induces an exact equivalence of subcategories

{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})^{\circ}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})^{\circ}.

Proof.

For Part
(ii)
, since the connection of an object in
{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})^{\circ}
is solvable, by
Lemma

4.14
, the tensor product functor
\mathbb{M}\mapsto\mathbb{M}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
admits an inverse functor defined by taking the horizontal section, namely

\mathcal{M}\longmapsto\mathcal{M}^{\nabla=0}.

For Part
(i)
, since the functor is local with respect to the Zariski topology of
X
, to check that it is an equivalence, it suffices to assume
X
is affine and admits an unramified model with a Frobenius endomorphism.
Then we notice that by the projection formula of finite projective modules and by
Theorem

3.21
.
(iii)
, for a flat connection
\mathcal{M}_{0}
over
R_{0}[1/p]
, the direct image functor along the map of sites
\nu:X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}\to X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
induces a natural isomorphism of flat connections

\mathcal{M}_{0}\simeq\nu_{*}(\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}).

This gives an inverse to the right vertical arrow in (
30
), namely the functor

{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}(R_{0}[1/p])\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})^{\circ}.

As a consequence, the diagram (
30
) can be refined into the following commutative diagram of equivalences

(31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}})^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}})^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((X_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Vect}}^{\varphi,\nabla_{R_{0}},N}(R_{0}[1/p]),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)(Y_{\eta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
Corollary

2.26
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}}

where the two vertical arrows on the right are equivalences.
So Part
(i)
follows.
∎

Another useful observation is that the maps
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
and
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
are automatically isomorphic when the ranks of
F
-isocrystals are maximal, extending the well-known result in the case of a point.

Theorem 4.17
.

Assume the setup of a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
that is small as in
Convention

3.11
, and let
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
be of rank
d
.

(i)

If the rank of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is
d
, then the map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
is an isomorphism.

(ii)

If the rank of
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
is
d
, then the map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
is an isomorphism.

Here we note that if either of the two assumptions above is true, then the
K
-linear extension of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
(resp.
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
) is naturally isomorphic to
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(X_{\eta})
, thanks to the injectivity in
Theorem

4.12
.
(iii)
and the fact that
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
has at most rank
d
over
X_{\eta}
.

For the convenience of the discussion, we recall a construction of the absolute Galois group for rigid spaces as below.

Construction 4.18
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be a connected smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
\overline{R}
be the integral closure of
R
in a fixed connected maximal pro-finite-étale extension of
R[1/p]
and let
S=\overline{R}^{\wedge}
be its
p
-completion.
Let
\tilde{X}
be the formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)
.
By assumption, the generic fiber
\tilde{X}_{\eta}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
is an affinoid perfectoid space and is a pro-Galois cover of
X_{\eta}
, and we denote by
G_{X_{\eta}}={\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline{R}/R)
the Galois group,
14
14

14

In
[
Bri08
]
(resp.
[
Shi22
]
),
G_{X_{\eta}}
is denoted by
\mathcal{G}_{R}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{R_{K}}
).

which is naturally isomorphic to the algebraic fundamental group of
X_{\eta}
(cf.
Construction

6.23
).
Moreover, we let
S_{0}
be the
p
-completion of the connected maximal pro-finite-étale extension of
R
inside of
S
.
Then
S_{0}\to R
is a
p
-complete pro-Galois cover whose Galois group, which we denote as
G_{X_{k}}
.
We let
I_{X}
be the kernel of the surjection
G_{X_{\eta}}\to G_{X_{k}}
and call it the inertial subgroup.

Proof of
Theorem

4.17
.

Following
Construction

4.18
, since the local system
T
and the period sheaves
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
are all trivializable when restricted onto the pro-finite-étale cover
\tilde{X}_{\eta}
, we reduce the question to showing that the natural injection of free continuous
G_{X_{\eta}}
-representations of the same rank over
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
below is an isomorphism

(32)

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\longrightarrow T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta});

Similarly for
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
.

We let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}
be a basis of
T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})[1/p]
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}
be a basis of
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
over
R_{0}[1/p]
.
Then there is a
d\times d
matrix
M
in
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
(resp.
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
) such that
(f_{i})=(e_{i})M
.
We let
e=\wedge_{i=1}^{d}e_{i}
, let
f=\wedge_{i=1}^{d}f_{i}
, and let
m=\det M
, then we have the equality
f=me
, and it suffices to show that
m
is an invertible element in
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
(resp.
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
); equivalently, the top wedge product of
Equation

32
is an isomorphism of rank one representations of
G_{X_{\eta}}
.
Here we note that by construction, since the group
G_{X_{\eta}}
acts trivially on
f
, we have

g(m)g(e)=g(me)=g(f)=f=me,~\forall g\in G_{X_{\eta}}.

In particular, since the group
G_{X_{\eta}}
preserves
\wedge^{d}T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})[1/p]=\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cdot e
, the equality above implies that the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cdot m
is a
G_{X_{\eta}}
-sub-representation of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
(resp.
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
).

To proceed, we notice that since the element
t\in\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
is invertible in both
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
, for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}mt^{r}
is contained in the
\mathrm{Fil}^{0}
part of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
but not in the
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}
part.
We then want to prove the following.

Lemma 4.19
.

The element
b
is contained in
S_{0}[1/p]
.

Proof.

By assumption, there is a
p
-complete étale morphism from
X
onto
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
.
We start by taking the reduction
\bar{b}
of the element
b
in

{\mathrm{gr}}^{0}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})=\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\simeq S[1/p][\frac{v_{1}}{\overline{\xi}},\ldots,\frac{v_{n}}{\overline{\xi}}]

(
[
Sch13a
, Cor. 6.15]
, cf.
Proposition

3.16
.
(ii)
).
Then the representation
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cdot b
of
G_{X_{\eta}}
becomes trivial after tensoring with
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
(i.e., after reducing the
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}
-part).
So by
[
Shi18
, proof of Thm. 5.15]
, the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local system that corresponds to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cdot b
is a Hodge–Tate representation of zero Hodge–Tate weight, and the inertial subgroup
I_{X}
acts on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cdot b
via a finite quotient.
In particular, there is a connected finite étale extension
S_{1}[1/p]
of the maximal unramified extension
S_{0}[1/p]
within
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
such that
\bar{b}
is contained in
S_{1}[1/p]
.
On the other hand, by Hensel’s lemma, the finite étale extension
S_{1}[1/p]
is naturally a subring of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
.

Now we let
b^{\prime}
be the element in
S_{1}[1/p]
that maps onto
\bar{b}
along the surjection
\mathrm{Fil}^{0}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\to\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
.
Now we claim that the element
b
is in fact equal to
b^{\prime}\in S_{1}[1/p]
.
This is because otherwise
I_{X}
acts on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cdot(b-b^{\prime})
through finite quotient as well.
If
b-b^{\prime}\neq 0
, then for
i\geq 1
,
b-b^{\prime}
lies in
\mathrm{Fil}^{i}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})
but not
\mathrm{Fil}^{i+1}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})
, and hence has nonzero image in
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})(i)
. However, by relative Sen theory
[
Shi18
]
,
I_{X}
cannot act on a nonzero element of
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})(i)
through a finite quotient, which yields a contradiction. Indeed, by
[
Bri08
, Lem. 3.3.1]
, there is a
G_{X_{\eta}}
-equivariant embedding
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})(i)\hookrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in T_{X_{\eta}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}({\mathrm{Spa}}(\overline{L}^{\wedge}_{p}))(i)
, where
T_{X_{\eta}}
is the set of prime ideals of
S
that are above the ideal
\pi R\subset R
, and
{\mathrm{Spa}}(\overline{L}^{\wedge}_{p})
is a complete algebraic extension of the Shilov point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
of
X_{\eta}
.
If there is an element
c\in\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})(i)
such that
I_{X_{\eta}}
acts through the finite quotient, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in T_{X_{\eta}}
, the initial subgroup of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L}
would act on the
\mathfrak{p}
-th factor
c_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}({\mathrm{Spa}}(\overline{L}^{\wedge}_{p}))(i)
through a finite quotient as well.
However, the latter was impossible for
i\neq 0
, by Sen’s theory on Hodge–Tate representations of weight
i
, which in this generality was proved in
[
Bri03
, Prop. 6]
.

The preceeding paragraph implies that the element
b
in
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
is contained in the subring
S_{1}[1/p]
.
Moreover, thanks to
Theorem

3.21
.
(ii)
and
Theorem

3.21
.
(iii)
, we know the intersection of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
(res.
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
) and
S_{1}[1/p]
within the ring
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
is the maximal unramified subring
S_{0}[1/p]
.
Hence the element
b
is contained in
S_{0}[1/p]\subset{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
.
∎

By the preceeding lemma, the determinant
m
of the transition matrix
M
is an element in
S_{0}[1/p]\cdot t^{r}
.
So we have the following commutative diagram of
G_{X_{\eta}}
-equivariant injections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge^{d}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}S_{0}[1/p]\cdot t^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge^{d}T(\tilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}S_{0}[1/p]\cdot t^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wedge^{d}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\tilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},\wedge^{d}T}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})}

where the second line is the base change of the first line along
S_{0}[1/p]\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})
and one has a completely similar diagram for
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
.

Now we let
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}
be the
p
-complete extension of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
by adding a compatible system of
p^{1/n}
-roots of
\pi
, and let
S_{2}
be the
p
-completion of the ring
S_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}[x_{1}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}},\ldots,x_{n}^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}]
.
Then there is a natural injection of rings
S_{0}\to S_{2}
such that it splits as a
S_{0}
-module.
So to show that
\iota
is an isomorphism, by the split injectivity of

S_{0}[1/p]\to S_{2}[1/p][\frac{v_{1}}{\bar{\xi}},\ldots,\frac{v_{d}}{\bar{\xi}}]=\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}({\mathrm{Spf}}(S_{1})_{\eta}),

it suffices to show the Galois equivariant tensor product
\iota\otimes_{S_{0}[1/p]}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}({\mathrm{Spf}}(S_{1})_{\eta})
is an isomorphism.
This then follows from Liu–Zhu’s p-adic Simpson correspondence
[
LZ17
, Thm. 2.1.(ii)]
, since the underlying module of the rank one flat connection
\wedge^{d}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}R[1/p]=D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\wedge^{d}T)\simeq D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\wedge^{d}T)(X_{\eta})
coincides with the underlying module of the rank one Higgs field
\mathcal{H}(\wedge^{d}T)(X_{\eta})
by
[
LZ17
, Cor. 3.12.(ii), Thm. 3.8.(ii)]
.
One treats
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(\wedge^{d}T)
in a completely analogous way.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

Theorem

4.17
should not be surprising:
the classical case for
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
was known long time ago and follows from an algebraic fact that the period rings
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
are
(K_{0},{\mathrm{Gal}}(K))
-regular (cf.
[
BC09
, Def. 5.1.1, Prop. 9.1.6]
), and we learned the general strategy from loc. cit.
Actually, by considering the extra data of connection, one can try to use a modified argument of loc. cit. to prove the higher-dimensional analogues, and it would follow if the category of continuous flat connections over
S_{0}[1/p]
is abelian, which we do not know due to the non-noetherianity of
S_{0}[1/p]
.

In fact, the careful reader might notice that in many past treatments of the relative
p
-adic Hodge theory before Liu–Zhu (for example
[
Bri08
]
,
[
AI12
]
, and
[
TT19
]
), it was not clear if the map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}:D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is automatically an isomorphism once the source and target have the same rank.

Using the above constructions, we are able to give an equivalent definition of crystalline and semi-stable local systems over an affine base with good reduction.
Precisely, we have the following statement.

Theorem 4.21
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be as in
Convention

3.11
.

(i)

A
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
of rank
d
over
X_{\eta}
is crystalline with respect to
X
if and only if
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is of rank
d
over
R_{0}[1/p]
.
Moreover, if
T
is crystalline, then
T
is naturally associated with
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
via the map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}^{\nabla=0}
and the equivalence in
Proposition

4.15
.

(ii)

A
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
of rank
d
over
X_{\eta}
is semi-stable with respect to
X
if and only if
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
is of rank
d
over
R_{0}[1/p]
.
Moreover, if
T
is semi-stable, then
T
is naturally associated with
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
via the map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}^{\nabla=0,N=0}
.

Proof.

Assuming that
K
is unramified, it was proved in
[
TT19
, Prop. 3,21]
that the local system
T
is crystalline if and only if
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is of rank
d
and the map
\alpha_{\mathrm{crys}}
is an isomorphism.
Despite the assumption of loc. cit. being stronger, the same strategy can be carried to our setting, as we shall explain.

We first assume
T
is crystalline and is associated with an
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
via the map
\vartheta:\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\simeq T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
.
By taking the base change along
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and
then the direct image along
\nu:X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}\to X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
, the map
\vartheta
induces an isomorphism

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\simeq\bigl(\nu_{*}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})\bigr)(X_{\eta}),

where by
Proposition

4.15
.
(i)
, the latter together with its Frobenius structure and connection is naturally isomorphic to

\bigl(\nu_{*}(\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})\bigr)(X_{\eta}),

where
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is the associated
R_{0}[1/p]
-vector bundle corresponding to
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
under
Proposition

2.11
.
Moreover, by the calculation in
Theorem

3.21
.
(ii)
and the local freeness of
\mathcal{M}_{0}
over
R_{0}[1/p]
, we have
\bigl(\nu_{*}(\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})\bigr)(X_{\eta})\simeq\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
This shows that the
F
-isocrystal
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
Furthermore, by
Proposition

4.15
.
(i)
again, the induced map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}:\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is naturally identified with
\vartheta\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\colon\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
, which by assumption is an isomorphism.

Conversely, assume
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
is of the same rank as that of
T
.
By
Theorem

4.17
, the map
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}:\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is an isomorphism.
We let
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
be the
F
-isocrystal over
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
associated with
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
under
Proposition

2.11
.
Then by
Proposition

4.15
, we may replace
\mathcal{M}_{0}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
by
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
to get a Frobenius-equivariant isomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
-vector bundles

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\simeq T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}.

In particular, by taking the horizontal sections and
Proposition

3.14
.
(i)
we get a Frobenius-equivariant isomorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundles

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})=(\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})^{\nabla=0}\simeq(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})^{\nabla=0}=T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}.

Hence the
p
-adic local system
T
is associated with
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
, in the sense of
Definition

4.4
.

To check
(ii)
, the above argument goes without change, except that we use the input from
Proposition

4.15
.
(ii)
instead. ∎

As a quick consequence of the local formulae for the associated
F
-isocrystals above, we see the association of Faltings in
Definition

4.4
can be made functorial.

Corollary 4.22
.

Assume the setup of a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
as in
Convention

3.11
.
For a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
, we let
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0},T}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0},T}
) be the
F
-isocrystal over
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
(resp.
(X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
) corresponding to
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
via
Proposition

2.11
(resp.
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
via
Corollary

2.26
).

(i)

There are natural Frobenius equivariant injections of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundles as below that are functorial in
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})

\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0},T})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0},T})\hookrightarrow T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}.

(ii)

The second arrow, which we denote as
\vartheta_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0},T}
, is an isomorphism when
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{st}}(X_{\eta})
.

(iii)

The composition, which we denote as
\vartheta_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0},T}
, is an isomorphism when
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})
.

Remark 4.23
.

As in Tan-Tong
[
TT19
]
and Andreatta–Iovita
[
AI12
]
, we can enhance
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
and
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
by an additional data of filtration (after base changing to
K
).
One can then check that the isomorphisms
\alpha_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
and
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
in
Theorem

4.21
are in fact filtered isomorphisms after base changing to
K
.
We refer the reader to
Remark

4.6
and
[
GR24
, §2]
for discussions.

Remark 4.24
.

As a consequence of
Theorem

4.21
, we get an explicit local candidate for the associated
F
-isocrystal of a crystalline or semi-stable local system.
If one is willing to consider the additional filtration as in
Remark

4.23
, it can be shown as in
[
GR24
, Cor. 2.35]
that the associated filtered
F
-isocrystal of a given crystalline or semi-stable local system is unique up to unique isomorphism.

5.
Global Crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functors

In this section, we analyze the behavior of the crystallinity, the semi-stability, and the functors
D_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
for arbitrary
p
-adic local systems with respect to the pullback along a map of
p
-adic formal schemes.
As consequences, we introduce crystalline analogues of the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert correspondence on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems over rigid spaces with good reduction, and give a quick proof of the purity result for crystalline and semi-stable local systems.

5.1.
Pullback compatibility for general
p
-adic formal schemes

The first subsection is devoted to showing that the crystallinity or semi-stability of a local system in the sense of Faltings is preserved under pullback along a map of
p
-adic formal schemes, without any regularity assumption.

We start with a preparation on the pullback of an
F
-isocrystal versus its associated
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundle defined in
Construction

4.1
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a map of
p
-adic formal schemes equipped with standard log structures (
Example

2.7
), and let
\mathcal{E}
be an isocrystal over either
X_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}
or
(X_{p=0},M_{X_{p=0}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}}
-vector bundles over
X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
:

(33)

f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\bigl(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X}(\mathcal{E})\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}}(f_{p=0}^{*}\mathcal{E}).

The isomorphism is functorial in
\mathcal{E}
, and is Frobenius equivariant when
\mathcal{E}
has a Frobenius structure.

For later applications, here we note that the
F
-isocrystal
f_{p=0}^{*}\mathcal{E}
is naturally identified with
f_{k}^{*}(\mathcal{E})
under the equivalence in
Proposition

2.12
.
In particular, the statement applies to both the setting of the mod
p
special fibers and that of the reduced special fibers as well.

Proof.

We first notice that the map of mod
p
reduction
X^{\prime}_{p=0}\to X_{p=0}
naturally induces a functor of sites
X^{\prime}_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}\to X_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}
, sending a
p
-complete divided power thickening
(A,A/I,g:{\mathrm{Spec}}(A/I)\to X^{\prime}_{p=0})
over
X^{\prime}_{p=0}
onto the same divided power thickening with the structure morphism composed by
f_{p=0}
, namely

(A,A/I,f_{p=0}\circ g:{\mathrm{Spec}}(A/I)\to X_{p=0})\in X_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}},

and is compatible with the log structures when
(A,A/I,g)
is equipped with one.
On the other hand, the pullback isocrystal
f_{p=0}^{*}\mathcal{E}
is defined by sending a
p
-complete divided power thickening
(A,A/I,g)\in X^{\prime}_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}
onto the
A
-module
\mathcal{E}(A,A/I,f_{p=0}\circ g)
.
So for any perfectoid algebra
(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime})
over
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
, since the divided power thickening
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S^{\prime}),S^{\prime}/p)
is naturally an object over
X^{\prime}_{p=0,{\mathrm{crys}}}
, we see by
Construction

4.1
that

\mathbb{B}^{+}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}(f_{p=0}^{*}\mathcal{E})(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime})=\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S^{\prime}),S^{\prime}/p),

where we regard
({\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(S^{\prime}),S^{\prime}/p)
as a divided power thickening over
X_{p=0}
by composition with the map
f_{p=0}
as above.
Moreover, if there is another perfectoid algebra
(S[1/p],S)
over
X_{\eta}
together with a morphism
(S[1/p],S)\to(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime})
that is compatible with
f
, then the above formula implies a natural base change isomorphism

\mathbb{B}^{+}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E})(S[1/p],S)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(S)}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{+}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}(f_{p=0}^{*}\mathcal{E})(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime}),

which by construction is compatible with the Frobenius structure when
\mathcal{E}
underlies an
F
-isocrystal, and holds without change for (
F
-)isocrystals over the log crystalline sites.
Hence by considering the above constructions over all affinoid perfectoid objects over
X_{\eta}
and
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
, and inverting the elements
p
and
\mu
locally, we get a natural pullback formula

f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}(f_{p=0}^{*}\mathcal{E}),

which is functorial with respect to
\mathcal{E}
and is compatible with Frobenius structures.
∎

Similarly, we have the following compatibility of étale pullbacks.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a map of
p
-adic formal schemes, and let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
-vector bundles over
X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
:

(34)

f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}}^{*}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}})\simeq f_{\eta}^{-1}T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}.

Proof.

By definition, the sheaf
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}
is defined as the pro-étale sheaf associated to the presheaf which sends an affinoid perfectoid object
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
in
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
to

T(U)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(U)}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S[1/p],S).

Its
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-linear pullback, when restricted onto the object
U
, is the sheaf associated to the presheaf which sends an affinoid perfectoid object
V={\mathrm{Spa}}(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime})
in
X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{U}
to

\bigl(T(U)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(U)}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S[1/p],S)\bigr)\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S[1/p],S)}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime}),

which is naturally isomorphic to

\bigl(T(U)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(U)}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(V)\bigr)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(V)}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S^{\prime}[1/p],S^{\prime}).

Hence (
34
) holds because it holds when evaluated on affinoid perfectoid objects.
∎

The main statement this subsection is the following.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a map of topologically finite type
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Assume
T
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
that is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
X
, and is associated to the
F
-isocrystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
over
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
(resp. over
(X_{k},M_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
) via an isomorphism of vector bundles over
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}

\vartheta:\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\sim}T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}.

Then the étale preimage
f_{\eta}^{-1}T=T|_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
X^{\prime}
, and is associated to the
F
-isocrystal
f_{k}^{*}(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
via the isomorphism of vector bundles over
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
obtained by the following composition

\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}(f_{k}^{*}\mathcal{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eqn: lem: pullback formula on crystalline side})}}{{\simeq}}f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\vartheta}}{{\simeq}}f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eqn: pullback formula on etale side})}}{{\simeq}}f_{\eta}^{-1}T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X_{\eta}}.

Proof.

We take the
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
-linear pullback of the isomorphism
\vartheta
along the map of ringed sites
(X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X^{\prime}})\to(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X})
, to get

f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X}(\mathcal{E})\simeq f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}}^{*}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X}).

The rest follows from the isomorphisms in
Lemma

5.1
and
Lemma

5.2
.
∎

As a quick consequence, we see the restriction at closed (i.e., classical) points always preserves the crystallinity and the semi-stability.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
X
be a topologically finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
that is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
X
.

Then for any closed point
x\in X_{\eta}
, the restriction
T|_{x}
is a crystalline (resp. semi-stable) representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x)}
.

Here for a finite field extension
K^{\prime}/K
, we implicitly identify a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over a point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(K^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
with a continuous
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation of the Galois group
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By replacing
X
by an affine open subscheme, the closed immersion of rigid space
x\to X_{\eta}
can be obtained from a map of
p
-adic formal schemes
\tilde{x}:{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(x)})\to X
.
The rest follows from
Theorem

5.3
.
∎

5.2.
Pullback injectivity of
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}

Next we study the behavior of the pullbacks of the
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors for general
p
-adic local systems, along a map of smooth affine
p
-adic formal schemes with a compatible choice of Frobenius structures.
We will show that the functors
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
, when viewed as taking values in
F
-isocrystals over the special fiber, are compatible with the pullback functors in an appropriate sense (
Theorem

5.5
).

Theorem 5.5
.

We assume the following setup:

(a)

Let
K^{\prime}/K
be a finite extension with perfect residue fields
k^{\prime}/k
.

(b)

Let
X_{0}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
and
X_{0}^{\prime}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0}^{\prime})
be as in
Convention

3.11
and
f_{0}:X^{\prime}_{0}\to X_{0}
be a Frobenius equivariant map over
\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}
.

(c)

Let
X^{\prime}=X^{\prime}_{0}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
, let
X=X_{0}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}}\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
f:X^{\prime}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime})\to X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be the natural extension of the map
f_{0}
.

Then we have the following.

(i)

There is a natural Frobenius equivariant injection of flat connections over
R^{\prime}_{0}[1/p]
that is functorial with respect to
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})

\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}\colon(f_{0})_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T).

(ii)

There is a natural Frobenius and monodromy equivariant injection of flat connections over
R^{\prime}_{0}[1/p]
that is functorial with respect to
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})

\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f}\colon(f_{0})_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{st}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T).

(iii)

Assume the map
f
(thus the map
f_{0}
) is finite étale.
Then the above injections are isomorphisms.

Moreover, the injections above are compatible with the composition, in the sense that if there is a further map
g:X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
arising from a Frobenius equivariant map of smooth
p
-adic formal schemes
g^{\prime}_{0}:{\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime\prime}_{0})\to X^{\prime}_{0}
, then we have an equality of maps

\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},g\circ f}=\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},g}\circ(g_{0})_{\eta}^{*}\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f},

and similarly for the semi-stable analogues.

Below we give the proof of the theorem for
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
; the analogues for
D_{\mathrm{st}}
can be proved by simply replacing
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
and
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
by
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{st}}
and
D_{\mathrm{st}}
respectively in the following arguments.
By the coherence of
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
and the affineness of the rigid spaces involved, we also temporarily abuse the notation
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
(similarly for
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}(T))
) with its global section
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(X_{\eta})
.

We start with the case for an arbitrary map
f_{0}
.

Proof of
Theorem

5.5
.
(i)
.

We first assume
K^{\prime}=K
for the moment, and thus
X^{\prime}=X^{\prime}_{0}\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}})}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
.
By assumption, there is a natural commutative diagram of ringed sites that are equivariant with respect to Frobenius structures and connections:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}},R^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}},R_{0}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}

The diagram then induces a natural transformation

(f_{0})_{\eta}^{*}\nu_{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T)\longrightarrow\nu^{\prime}_{*}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}^{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T).

Similar to the proof of
Lemma

5.2
, the target sheaf
\nu^{\prime}_{*}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}^{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T)
is canonically isomorphic to
\nu^{\prime}_{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}f_{\eta}^{-1}T)
.
So by the definition of the functor
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
, the above map can be identified with a natural Frobenius-equivariant map of flat connections over
R^{\prime}_{0}[1/p]

\iota_{\mathrm{crys}}:(f_{0})_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T),

which is functorial with respect to
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Analogously, we have a natural map of flat connections over
X^{\prime}_{\eta}

\iota_{\mathrm{dR}}:f_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T),

which fits into a naturally commutative diagram with
\iota\otimes_{K_{0}}K
, namely

(35)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl((f_{0})_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\bigr)\otimes_{K_{0}}K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X^{\prime}_{\eta}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathrm{dR}}}

Here we note that by
Theorem

4.12
.
(iii)
the right vertical map is an injection, and by
Theorem

4.9
.
(iii)
the bottom map is an isomorphism.

To show the injectivity of the map
\iota_{\mathrm{crys}}
, it suffices to show the injectivity of the base change
\iota_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K
.
Notice that since we assumed
K=K^{\prime}
, we have
X^{\prime}=X^{\prime}_{0}\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}})}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
.
So the flat connection
\bigl((f_{0})_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\bigr)\otimes_{K_{0}}K
is naturally isomorphic to
f_{\eta}^{*}(D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K)
, and the map
\iota_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K
is identified with the following map of flat connections over
R[1/p]

f_{\eta}^{*}(D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K.

So to show the injectivity of
\iota\otimes_{K_{0}}K
, by the commutative diagram (
35
) above, it suffices to show the injectivity of the following map of flat connections

f_{\eta}^{*}(D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K)\longrightarrow f_{\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(T).

This then follows from
Theorem

4.12
.
(iii)
, where we showed that
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{K_{0}}K\to D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(T)
is an injection of flat connections over
X_{\eta}
.

Next, to extend the proof of
(i)
to the case of a general base extension
K^{\prime}/K
, we can now reduce the original question to the setting when
K^{\prime}/K
is totally ramified,
R_{0}=R_{0}^{\prime}
, and
R^{\prime}=R\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
. In particular,
f_{\eta}:X^{\prime}_{\eta}=X_{\eta}\times_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(K)}{\mathrm{Spa}}(K^{\prime})\to X_{\eta}
is nothing but a finite étale cover given by extension of scalars.
In this case, the question amounts to showing the injectivity of the map

(36)

\bigl(\nu_{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T)\bigr)(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow\bigl(\nu^{\prime}_{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\bigr)(X^{\prime}_{\eta}).

We let
\mathcal{F}
be the étale sheaf
\nu_{*}({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T)
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
.
Then the injectivity of the map in Equation (
36
) is equivalent to the injectivity of

\mathcal{F}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow(f_{\eta}^{-1}\mathcal{F})(X^{\prime}_{\eta}),

where the latter is nothing but
\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime}_{\eta})
.
Hence the claim follows from the assumption that
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is an étale cover.

Finally, we check that the injections
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
are compatible with the composition
X^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{g}X^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}X
, namely
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},g\circ f}=\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},g}\circ(g_{0})_{\eta}^{*}\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
. As the natural map
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\to D_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(T)
is injective, the equality
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},g\circ f}=\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},g}\circ(g_{0})_{\eta}^{*}\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
follows from the commutativity of the diagram (
35
) and the equality
\iota_{{\mathrm{dR}},g\circ f}=\iota_{{\mathrm{dR}},g}\circ(g_{0})_{\eta}^{*}\iota_{{\mathrm{dR}},f}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.5
.
(iii)
.

Thanks to the injectivity shown in
(i)
, in order to prove the base change isomorphism, we are free to replace
X_{0}^{\prime}
by a further finite étale cover.
So we assum
f_{0}:X_{0}^{\prime}\to X_{0}
is a finite Galois cover with the Galois group
H
.
Before we discuss the two cases separately, we recall some general construction.

As in
Construction

4.18
, we let
S
be the
p
-completion of the integral closure of
R
in a fixed connected maximal pro-finite-étale extension of
R[1/p]
, and let
\tilde{X}
be the formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)
.
By assumption, the generic fiber
\tilde{X}_{\eta}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
is an affinoid perfectoid space and is a pro-Galois cover of
X_{\eta}
with the Galois group
G_{X_{\eta}}
.
Then both the
p
-adic local system
T
and the sheaf
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
are trivialized at
\tilde{X}_{\eta}
, and their sections at
\tilde{X}_{\eta}
admit continuous actions of
G_{X_{\eta}}
.
So by translating the global section of the pro-étale sheaf into the continuous group cohomology with respect to the
G_{X_{\eta}}
-cover
\tilde{X}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
(cf.
[
Sch13a
]
), we have

(37)

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)=\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\bigr)(X_{\eta})\simeq\bigl(T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\bigr)^{G_{X_{\eta}}}.

In this case,
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is a finite Galois cover of rigid spaces with
H\simeq G_{X_{\eta}}/G_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
.
We let
\tilde{X}={\mathrm{Spf}}(S)
be as in
Construction

4.18
, then the affinoid perfectoid space
\tilde{X}_{\eta}
is covering both
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
and
X_{\eta}
under the pro-étale topology, with the Galois group being
G_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
and
G_{X_{\eta}}
respectively.
Moreover, we have the following formulae for
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
and
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}^{\prime}}(f^{-1}T)
:

(38)

\displaystyle D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\bigr)^{G_{X_{\eta}}};

(39)

\displaystyle D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\simeq

\displaystyle\bigl(T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\bigr)^{G_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}}\simeq\bigl(T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\bigr)^{G_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}},

where the last isomorphism follows from the equality that
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}|_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}
in
Proposition

3.14
.
(iii)
.
We let
M
be the finite
R^{\prime}_{0}[1/p]
-module

\bigl(T(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{X}_{\eta})\bigr)^{G_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}}.

Then by Equation (
38
) we get

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\simeq M^{H};\quad D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\simeq M.

Hence to show the surjection of
f_{0,\eta}^{*}D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R^{\prime}_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)
, it suffices to prove that for a finite
R^{\prime}_{0}[1/p]
-module
M
with a semi-linear action by the finite group
H
, the following natural map is an isomorphism

M^{H}\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}R^{\prime}_{0}[1/p]\longrightarrow M.

The latter follows from Galois descent, so we are done.
∎

5.3.
Weak
F
-isocrystals

To prepare for the construction of thecrystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor later, in this subsection, we introduce the
weak
F
-isocrystal
, defined as the sheaf of
F
-isocrystals that satisfies an injectivity condition.

Definition 5.6
.

Let
Z
be a scheme of finite type over
k
.
A
sheaf
of
(F
-)isocrystals
\mathcal{E}
on
Z
is the datum which assigns to each open subscheme
U\subseteq Z
an object
\mathcal{E}_{U}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{{\mathrm{crys}}})
, and assigns to each inclusion of open subscheme
U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U
a restriction morphism
\mathrm{res}_{U^{\prime},U}:\mathcal{E}_{U}|_{U^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{E}_{U^{\prime}}
, such that the following two conditions hold:

(a)

For a sequence of inclusions
U^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U
, we have
\mathrm{res}_{U^{\prime\prime},U^{\prime}}\circ\mathrm{res}_{U^{\prime},U}=\mathrm{res}_{U^{\prime\prime},U}
.

(b)

If
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
is a Zariski cover of
U
, and
(T,V)
is a
p
-torsionfree pro-PD-thickening in
Z_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
such that
T_{p=0}
is flat over
Z
, then the following natural sequence

(40)

0\to\mathcal{E}_{U}(T,V)\to\prod_{\alpha\in I}\mathcal{E}_{U_{\alpha}}(T|_{V_{\alpha}},V_{\alpha})\rightrightarrows\prod_{\alpha,\beta\in I}\mathcal{E}_{U_{\alpha,\beta}}(T|_{V_{\alpha,\beta}},V_{\alpha,\beta})

of
\mathcal{O}_{T}
-modules is exact, where
U_{\alpha,\beta}=U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}
,
V_{\alpha}=V\cap U_{\alpha}
and
V_{\alpha,\beta}=V\cap U_{\alpha,\beta}
.

Denote the category of sheaves of crystalline (
F
-)isocrystals by
{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)})
, whose morphisms are defined in the obvious way.
If
M
is a log structure on
Z
, in a completely analogous way we define the sheaf of log crystalline
F
-isocrystals on
(Z,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
, and in the above notations we replace
Z
by
(Z,M)
.
Similarly for the category
{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N})
of objects that are equipped with nilpotent endomorphisms.

Remark 5.7
.

Let
Z_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{\prime}
be a basis of the Zariski topology of
Z
; for example, the subcategory of affine open subschemes in
Z
.
Then the inclusion functor of sites
Z_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{\prime}\to Z_{\mathrm{Zar}}
naturally defines an equivalence of categories

{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi})\simeq{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}).

So to give a sheaf of
F
-isocrystal, it suffices to produce the data on a slightly smaller subcategory.

Remark 5.8
.

The usual notion of the
F
-isocrystal satisfies the Zariski descent.
In particular, the category
\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)}(Z)
is naturally viewed as the full subcategory of
{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)})
of
coherent
objects, i.e., those whose restriction morphisms are all isomorphisms.

We also note that the pullback functor naturally extends to the notion of weak isocrystals.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
f:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be a morphism between finite type
k
-schemes.
Define the pullback functor
f^{*}\colon{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)})\to{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)})
by the Zariski sheafification of the crystalline pullback functor for isocrystals.
Define the logarithmic analogue similarly.

For the sake of constructing the crystalline Riemann-Hilbert functors, it is helpful to consider a slightly weaker notion:

Definition 5.10
.

We say that
\mathcal{E}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)})
is a
weak (
F
-)isocrystal
if all restriction morphisms are injective, and we denote the full subcategory of weak (
F
-)isocrystals by
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{(\varphi)}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
.
The log crystalline analogues are defined similarly, and we denote the corresponding category by
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{(\varphi)}((Z,M)_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
.

Note that
\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
is a full subcategory of
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{(\varphi)}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
and there is a natural projection
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{(\varphi)}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{(\varphi)}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
given by “taking global sections”

\mathcal{E}\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{Z}.

To analyze the discrepancy between a weak isocrystal and an actual isocrystal, we introduce the following notions.

Definition 5.11
.

Let
Z
be a connected smooth variety over
k
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be either in
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
or in
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}((Z,(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
.
15
15

15

Recall that by
Theorem

2.24

\mathcal{E}
is always locally free.

•

We let
\mathrm{rank}^{+}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\max_{U}\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{U})
and
\mathrm{rank}^{-}(\mathcal{E})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\min_{U}\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{U})
, where
U
runs through all affine open subschemes of
Z
.

•

We let
U_{i}
be the union of all affine open subschemes
V\in Z_{\mathrm{Zar}}
such that
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{V})\geq i
.
Then the
rank stratification
of
\mathcal{E}
is defined as the finite chain of open immersions

Z=U_{\mathrm{rank}^{-}(\mathcal{E})}\supseteq U_{\mathrm{rank}^{-}(\mathcal{E})+1}\supseteq\cdots\supseteq U_{\mathrm{rank}^{+}(\mathcal{E})}.

•

We call
U_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U_{\mathrm{rank}^{+}(\mathcal{E})}
the
pure locus
of
\mathcal{E}
.

•

Given a point
z\in Z
, we say that a connected open
U
containing
z
is a
stable open neighborhood
if
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{E}_{U})
achieves the maximum among all open neighborhoods of
z
.

We give an explicit description of the pullback functor for weak F-isocrystals.

Remark 5.12
.

Let
f:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be a morphism between smooth connected varieties over
k
. Given
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
, the pullback
f^{*}\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime})
can be explicitly described as follows:
For every point
z^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime}
, choose a stable open neighborhood
U_{z^{\prime}}
of
f(z^{\prime})
.
Then
f^{*}\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{\mathrm{crys}})
is the unique Zariski sheaf of isocrystals such that
(f^{*}\mathcal{E})_{f^{-1}(U)}=g_{\mathrm{crys}}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{U}
, where
g
is the map of
k
-schemes
f^{-1}(U)\to U
, and
g_{\mathrm{crys}}^{*}
is the usual pullback functor of isocrystals.
It is not hard to check to that
f^{*}\mathcal{E}
lies in
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z)
and is independent of the choice of the stable open neighborhoods
U_{z^{\prime}}
.

Notice that for every
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
, the restriction
i^{*}\mathcal{E}
is an object in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(U_{\mathcal{E}})
for the open immersion
i:U_{\mathcal{E}}\hookrightarrow Z
, where
U_{\mathcal{E}}
is the pure locus.
In particular, a weak isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
is an isocrystal if and only if
\mathrm{rank}^{+}(\mathcal{E})=\mathrm{rank}^{-}(\mathcal{E})
.
Below we provide a simple example that is not an isocrystal.

Example 5.13
.

If
i:U\hookrightarrow Z
is the inclusion of an open subscheme, then there is a natural functor
i_{!}:\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(U)\to\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
such that for every
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(U_{\mathrm{crys}})
, we define
(i_{!}\mathcal{E})_{V}=\mathcal{E}_{V}
when
V\subseteq U
and
(i_{!}\mathcal{E})_{V}=0
otherwise.
If
U\neq Z
, then for every nonzero
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(U_{\mathrm{crys}})
, the pure locus
i_{!}\mathcal{E}
is equal to
U
.
In particular, the object
i_{!}\mathcal{E}
is always in
\mathrm{wIsoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
but not in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{\mathrm{crys}})
.

5.4.
Global Riemann–Hilbert functor

In this subsection, we show that the
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
and the
D_{\mathrm{st}}
functors, once translated into
F
-isocrystals over the special fiber, are independent of the choice of the unramified models.
This will in particular allows us to obtain a global version of the Riemann–Hilbert functor, sending
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems to weak
F
-isocrystals.

We start with the independence of models for the
D
-functors.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
as in
Convention

3.11
, and assume
X
is small.
Then the
F
-isocrystals corresponding to
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)
and
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)
are independent of the choice of the unramified model
R_{0}
and its Frobenius structure.

Proof.

Let
R_{0}
and
R_{0}^{\prime}
be two unramified models of
X
that are equipped with Frobenius endomorphisms.
We let the ring
A
be the
p
-complete divided power envelope of the surjection

(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow R_{k},

and let
p_{1}:R_{0}\to A
and
p_{2}:R^{\prime}_{0}\to A
be the two structure maps.
Then the ring
A
is equipped with a continuous differential
\nabla:A\to A\otimes_{(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}}\Omega^{1}_{(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}/W}
over
W
and a Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{A}
, which are compatible with that of
R_{0}
and
R_{0}^{\prime}
.
In addition, by the smallness of
X
, the map
p_{1}
identifies
A
with the
p
-complete divided power polynomial ring
R_{0}\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}\}
.
Here
u_{i}
is the image of the element
x_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes x_{i}^{\prime}\in R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime}
, and
x_{i}
and
X_{i}^{\prime}
are local coordinates in
R_{0}
and
R_{0}^{\prime}
respectively whose images in
R_{k}
coincide.

Here we note that the explicit formula above in particular implies that the continuous relative differential over
R_{0}
, namely the natural map

\nabla_{A/R_{0}}:A\to A\otimes_{(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}}\Omega^{1}_{(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}/R_{0}},

has the kernel equal to
R_{0}
.
The same holds for the map
p_{2}
and
\nabla_{A/R_{0}^{\prime}}
as well.

Next, we define the the pro-étale sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
to be the
p
-complete divided power envelope for the surjection

(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+},

and let
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
be its localization defined by pro-étale locally inverting the element
\mu
, where the latter is naturally a sheaf of algebras over
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
.
Analogous to
Definition

3.12
, there are a natural Frobenius endomorphism and an
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-linear connection on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
that are compatible with that of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}^{\prime}}
.
Moreover, the relative differential
\nabla_{A/R_{0}}
on
A
induces a differential morphism on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
that is linear over
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
, for which we use the same notation
\nabla_{A/R_{0}}
.
Here by the smallness and the explicit construction of the divided power envelope, the sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
is naturally identified with the complete divided power polynomial rings, namely we have

(41)

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}.

The latter implies that the kernel of the relative differential
\nabla_{A/R_{0}}:{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\otimes_{(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}}\Omega^{1}_{(R_{0}\otimes_{W}R_{0}^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{p}/R_{0}}
is equal to
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
.
The same applies to
R_{0}^{\prime}
as well, which we shall not repeat.
We also let
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},A}
be the tensor product
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\otimes_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}
, and similarly define its localization
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},A}
.
By
Definition

3.17
and the discussion analogous to above, one naturally obtains an absolute connection, two relative connections, and a Frobenius structure on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},A}
that are compatible with the previous constructions.
Furthermore, the nil operator on
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{st}}}
induces a nil operator on
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},A}
whose kernel is
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
.

We then let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
.
Similar to what we did for
Theorem

4.12
, we define the following Frobenius equivariant connections over
A[1/p]

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}(T)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A})(X_{\eta}),\quad D_{{\mathrm{st}},A}(T)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},A})(X_{\eta}),

where the second term admits a natural nil endomorphism
N_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},A}(T)}
that is induced from
N_{{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},A}}
, and there is a natural injection
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}(T)\to D_{{\mathrm{st}},A}(T)
identifies the source as the kernel of
N_{D_{{\mathrm{st}},A}(T)}
.
Moreover, the canonical injection of period sheaves
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\to{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
induces the equivariant map of abelian groups

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}(T),

and similar ones for
R_{0}^{\prime}
, and
D_{{\mathrm{st}},*}
for
*\in\{R_{0},R_{0}^{\prime}\}
.

Now we claim that the linearizations below are isomorphisms

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}A[1/p]\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}(T),\quad D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}A[1/p]\longrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{st}},A}(T).

For the first map, since
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
is the union of the subsheaves
\cup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\cdot\mu^{-n}
, and the same is true for
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
, it suffices to show that the following map is an isomorphism

16
16

16

Here by the finiteness of
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\cdot\mu^{-n}(X_{\eta})
over
R_{0}
, we implicitly identify the left hand side below with the non-complete tensor product.

(42)

\bigl((T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu^{n}})(X_{\eta})\otimes_{R_{0}}A\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu^{n}})(X_{\eta}).

Similarly, for the second map, since
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{st}},*}
is locally the union of the direct sum
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},*}\cdot\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
, it suffices to show that the following map is an isomorphism

(43)

\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=0}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu^{n}}\cdot\log(\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]})^{i})(X_{\eta})\otimes_{R_{0}}A\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=0}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\cdot\frac{1}{\mu^{n}}\cdot\log(\frac{u}{[\pi^{\flat}]})^{i})\bigr)(X_{\eta})

By the explicit formula of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}
, the pro-étale sheaf is
p
-completely free over
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
; namely there is a free
R_{0}
-module
M=\bigoplus_{I}R_{0}
, where
I
is some index set, such that

{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\simeq\bigl({\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{0}}M\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}.

We then make the observation, so that by setting the
\mathcal{F}
below to be the pro-étale sheaf
\bigoplus_{i=0}T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\cdot\mu^{-n}\cdot\log(u/[\pi^{\flat}])^{i}
, one checks that (
42
) and (
43
) are both isomorphisms.

Lemma 5.15
.

We let
\mathcal{F}
be a
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree
R_{0}
-linear pro-étale sheaf over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, and let
M=\oplus_{I}R_{0}
be a free
R_{0}
-module.
There is a natural isomorphism of
p
-complete
R_{0}
-modules

\bigl(\mathcal{F}(X_{\eta})\otimes_{R_{0}}M\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\bigl(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R_{0}}M\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}(X_{\eta}).

Proof.

By translating the two terms into limits, we reduce to establishing the following isomorphism

\lim_{n}\bigl(\mathcal{F}(X_{\eta})/p^{n}\otimes_{R_{0}/p^{n}}M/p^{n}\bigr)\simeq\lim_{n}\bigl((\mathcal{F}/p^{n}\otimes_{R_{0}/p^{n}}M/p^{n})(X_{\eta})\bigr).

By the universal coefficient theorem, the above is an injection where the cokernel is the limit

\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{1}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R_{0}}M)[p^{n}].

Since the cohomology commutes with the filtered colimit, the cokernel is isomorphic to

\lim_{n}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{1}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{F})\otimes_{R_{0}}M\bigr)[p^{n}].

To show the vanishing of the latter, we note that the direct sum
\bigoplus_{I}R_{0}
is contained in the direct product
\prod_{I}R_{0}
. Hence it suffices to show the vanishing of the following

\lim_{n}\bigl(\prod_{I}\mathrm{H}^{1}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{F})\bigr)[p^{n}].

The last term is further isomorphic to the limit

\lim_{n}\prod_{I}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{1}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{F})[p^{n}]\bigr),

which, by exchanging the order of the limit functors, is isomorphic to

\prod_{I}\lim_{n}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{1}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{F})[p^{n}]\bigr).

Finally, since the pro-étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree, we know
\lim_{n}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{1}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{F})[p^{n}]\bigr)
vanishes, which finishes the proof. ∎

We let
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},*}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})
for
*\in\{R_{0},R_{0}^{\prime},A\}
be the
F
-isocrystals defined by the Frobenius equivariant flat connections
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},*}
.
Then by the Frobenius equivariant isomorphisms of the flat connections above, we obtain the isomorphism of
F
-isocrystals

q_{R_{0},R_{0}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}^{\prime}},

and similarly for
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},*}
.
This shows the independence of the
F
-isocrystals associated to the
p
-adic local system
T
.

Finally, the isomorphism
q_{R_{0},R_{0}^{\prime}}
constructed above in fact satisfies the cocycle condition.
To see this, let
R_{i}
for
i=0,1,2
be three unramified models with Frobenius structures.
Similarly to the first paragraph above, for any
i\neq j
in
\{0,1,2\}
, we form the coproduct of the divided power thickenings
A_{i,j}
, and let
A_{0,1,2}
be the coproduct of the three.
By the previous discussions, we get isomorphic
F
-isocrystals

q_{R_{i},R_{j}}\colon\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{i,j}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{j}}.

In addition, by the equality of the compositions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{i,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0,1,2},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{j,k}}

the two compositions of isomorphisms among
F
-isocrystals below are naturally identified

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{i,j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{0,1,2}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{j,k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here in a way completely analogous to the first several paragraphs above, we define
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{0,1,2}}
to be the
F
-isocrystal associated to the flat connection
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{0,1,2}}(T)
over
A_{0,1,2}[1/p]
.
As a consequence, by replacing the middle arrows with the isomorphism towards and outwards
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{0,1,2}}
, we see that the composition
q_{R_{i},R_{j}}\circ q_{R_{j},R_{k}}
is equal to the isomorphism
q_{R_{i},R_{k}}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

Analogous to
[
BMS18
, §13]
, another approach to prove
Theorem

5.14
is to consider the collection
\mathrm{Frame}_{R}
of all finite subsets
\Sigma\subset R^{\times}
, such that

•

the map
k[(x_{u}^{\pm 1})_{u\in\Sigma}]\xrightarrow{x_{u}\mapsto u\text{~mod~}\pi}R_{k}
is surjective;

•

there exists a subset
\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}\}\subset\Sigma
such that the map
k[x_{u_{1}}^{\pm 1},\ldots,x_{u_{r}}^{\pm 1}]\to R_{k}
is étale.

Here the collection
\mathrm{Frame}_{R}
is non-empty after replacing
R
by some (
p
-complete) Zariski localization, is closed under finite unions, and in particular has no reference to any specific coordinates.
For each
\Sigma\in\mathrm{Frame}_{R}
, we can then let
A\coloneq A_{\Sigma}
be the
p
-complete
p
-adic divided power envelope for the surjection

W\langle(x_{u}^{\pm 1})_{u\in\Sigma}\rangle\longrightarrow R_{k}.

As in the proof of
Theorem

5.14
, one can produce the period sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{\Sigma}}
, the flat connection
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{\Sigma}}(T)
, and the
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{\Sigma}}
, for each
\Sigma\in\mathrm{Frame}_{R}
.
Moreover, any unramified model
R_{0}
admits a map to some object
A_{\Sigma}
, identifying the latter as a
p
-complete divided power polynomial ring over
R_{0}
.
Hence arguing similarly as in the last paragraphs of
Theorem

5.14
, we get the equivalences of
F
-isocrystals
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A_{\Sigma}}
for
\Sigma\in\mathrm{Frame}_{R}
.

We let
X_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{w}
be the category of small affine open subsets for a given smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, and equip it with the
p
-complete Zariski topology.
Then
X_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{w}
forms a basis of the Zariski site
X_{\mathrm{Zar}}
, and by
Remark

5.7
the inclusion functor of the sites induces an equivalence of the categories

{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi})\simeq{\mathrm{Shv}}(X_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{w},\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}),

and a similar statement holds for the logarithmic analogue, or the one with nilpotent endomorphisms.
For a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
, the construction in
Section

4.2
(cf.
Corollary

4.22
) and
Theorem

5.14
allow us to functorially define two presheaves of
F
-isocrystals on
X_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{w}
, whose values at an object
U
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}\colon U

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},U,T}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(U_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}});

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\colon U

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},U,T}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N}(U_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}).

Here
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},U,T}
canonically embeds into
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},U,T}
as the kernel of the nilpotent endomorphism on the target.
Our next result shows that in fact the above presheaves are weak
F
-isocrystals.

Proposition 5.17
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
.
Then the presheaves of
F
-isocrystals (resp. with a nilpotent morphism)
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
) is a weak
F
-isocrystal over
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
.

Proof.

In the following we focus on the proof for
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
; the argument showing
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}(Z_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi,N})
is the same and we will not repeat.
Let
U
be an object in
X_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{w}
, and let
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a Zariski cover of
U
in
X_{\mathrm{Zar}}^{w}
.
By the deformation theory, we let
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
be an unramified model of
U
that is equipped with a Frobenius structure
\varphi_{R_{0}}
.
Then by the
p
-complete étaleness of the maps
U_{i}\to U
, the given data
(R_{0},\varphi_{R_{0}})
uniquely induces the unramified models with Frobenius structures
(R_{0,i},\varphi_{R_{0,i}})
for each
U_{i}
, such that the latter are compatible with
(R_{0},\varphi_{R_{0}})
; similarly for the fiber produces
U_{i}\times_{U}U_{j}
.
Here we also note that by taking the generic fiber, the collection of rigid spaces
\{(U_{i})_{\eta}\}_{i\in I}
forms an analytic cover of
U_{\eta}
.

Now we recall from the construction in
Theorem

4.12
that we have

\displaystyle D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\bigr)(U_{\eta}),

\displaystyle D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i}}(T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i}}\bigr)((U_{i})_{\eta}),

\displaystyle D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i,j}}(T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i,j}}\bigr)((U_{i,j})_{\eta}).

On the other hand, by the localization formula in
Proposition

3.14
.
(iii)
, the pro-étale sheaf
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i}}
on
(U_{i})_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
is naturally identified with the restriction
\bigl(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}\bigr)|_{(U_{i})_{\eta}}
, and similarly for each
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i,j}}
.
We let
\mathcal{F}
be the pro-étale sheaf
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}
over
U_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
Then for each open subspace
V\subset U
, by the tautological formula
\mathcal{F}|_{V}(V)=\mathcal{F}(V)
, we get

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)=\mathcal{F}(U_{\eta}),\quad D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i}}(T)=\mathcal{F}((U_{i})_{\eta}),\quad D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0,i,j}}(T)=\mathcal{F}((U_{i}\times_{U}U_{j})_{\eta}).

As a consequence, since a pro-étale sheaf over
U_{\eta}
in particular satisfies the descent with respect to the analytic cover
\{(U_{i})_{\eta}\to U_{\eta}\}_{i\in I}
, we get a short exact sequence of abelian groups that are compatible with Frobenius and the connection structures

\mathcal{F}(U_{\eta})\rightarrow\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}((U_{i})_{\eta})\rightrightarrows\prod_{i,j\in I}\mathcal{F}((U_{i}\times_{U}U_{j})_{\eta}).

Finally, by the weak initial property of the unramified model
(R_{0},R_{k})
in the crystalline site
U_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
, we get the sheaf condition for general flat pro-PD-thickenings in
U_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
(cf.
Definition

5.6
).
This finishes the proof of the sheaf property of
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
.

For the injectivity in
Definition

5.10
, we let
U^{\prime}\subseteq U
be any open immersion of small affine open subspaces in
X
.
By the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},U,T}
and
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},U^{\prime},T}
are independent of the unramified models (
Theorem

5.14
), we may translate the problem into the injectivity of a map between flat connections.
We let
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
be a Frobenius equivariant unramified model of
U
.
Similarly to the above, the open subspace
U^{\prime}
admits a Frobenius equivariant unramified model
R_{0}^{\prime}
that is compatible with
R_{0}
.
So the claim follows from
Theorem

5.5
.
(i)
.
∎

By taking the pullback along the morphism of the ringed topoi

.

Proposition 5.18
.

Proof.

We let
x
be a closed point of

, and let
U
be a small affine open subspace of

that is stable with respect to the both weak

Moreover, for a given choice of an unramified model

of

and a Frobenius structure

, by

4.22
we have the injective maps

We now show that the maps defined in loc. cit. do not depend on the choice of the data
(R_{0},\varphi_{R_{0}})
, using the strategy proving

5.14
.
We will refer the reader for the notations introduced in the proof of loc. cit. therefore.

We let
R_{0}
and

be any two unramified models of

that are equipped with the Frobenius endomorphisms.
We let divided power envelope ring

As in loc. cit., the structural map

.
So by the construction of

and

, there is an equivariant commutative diagram of pro-étale sheaves

(45)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)\otimes_{R_{0}[1/p]}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}}

such that the vertical maps become isomorphic after tensoring the sources with

.
As a consequence, by

3.14
.
(i)
, the explicit formula in (

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0},T})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}|_{U_{\eta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},A,T})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0},T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0},T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}|_{U_{\eta}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{R_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{A}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where each vertical arrows are isomorphisms.
The arrows in the first and the last rows by

are all injective, hence ones in the second row.
As a consequence, for

respectively.

Finally, to see that the maps

(resp.
