) for different
U
indeed glue, it suffices to show that for any affine open subspace
V\subset U
, the maps
u_{U}
and
u_{V}
(resp.
v_{U}
and
v_{V}
) are compatible under the restriction.
To this end, since the above maps can be constructed using any unramified models, we may fix an unramified model
(R_{0},\varphi_{R_{0}})
of
U
and let
(S_{0},\varphi_{S_{0}})
be the unique unramified model of
V
that is compatible with the former.
It is then left to check the compatibility of
u_{R_{0}}
and
u_{S_{0}}
(resp. the maps
v_{*}
), which follows from
Theorem

5.5
.
∎

We are now ready to show the pullback compatibility for a map of smooth
p
-adic formal schemes, without any assumption on the unramified models.

Proposition 5.19
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a map of smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, with
f_{s}
the induced map on reduced special fibers, and let
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
There are natural injections of weak
F
-isocrystals

\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}:f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T},\quad\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f}:f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T},

that are compatible to each other via the nilpotent operators.

Proof.

We first notice that by taking the pullback of the injections of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-sheaves in
Proposition

5.18
along the map
f_{\eta}:Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, and by the canonical identifications in
Lemma

5.1
and
Lemma

5.2
, we get natural injections of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-sheaves over
Y_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

(47)

\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T})\hookrightarrow(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\otimes\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}.

On the other hand, applying
Proposition

5.18
at the local system
f_{\eta}^{-1}T
over
Y_{\eta}
, we have the injections

(48)

\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T})\hookrightarrow(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\otimes\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}.

So to show the claim, it suffices to show that the subsheaf
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{*,T})
of
f_{\eta}^{-1}T\otimes\mathbb{B}_{*,Y}
is contained in another subsheaf
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T})
, for
*\in\{{\mathrm{crys}},{\mathrm{st}}\}
.

As the containment can be checked locally, we may assume
Y
is affine and admits an unramified model
R_{0}
with a Frobenius endomorphism.
In addition, by shrinking
Y
onto an open neighborhood of a fixed point
y\in Y
if necessary, we assume that
Y
is the stable neighborhood of all the weak
F
-isocrystals discussed above, so that each of them are actual
F
-isocrystals over
Y_{s}
.
Under the assumption, by the equivalence of categories in
Proposition

4.16
, since both
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{*,T})
and
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T})
come from
F
-isocrystals, we may use the commutative diagram (
31
) and translate the question into flat connections.

Now we apply the tensor product of the maps in (
47
) with
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
, to get a natural equivariant injections of
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}
-vector bundles over
Y_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\hookrightarrow(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)\otimes{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}.

By further taking the section of this injection at
Y_{\eta}
, and by the definition of
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)
in
Theorem

4.12
, we get an injection of flat connections over
R_{0}[1/p]

\bigl(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T})\otimes_{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}\bigr)(Y_{\eta})\hookrightarrow D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T),

where the left hand side is equal to the flat connection
f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}(R_{0},R_{k})
with additional structures (cf. the commutative diagram (
31
)).
This gives the required injection, thanks again to the equivalences of various categories of loc. cit..
∎

Assembling the results in this subsection, we obtain the
crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functors
in general.

Theorem 5.20

(Crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor)
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
be of rank
d
.

(i)

There is a natural weak
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
) of rank
\leq d
over
X_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
(resp. over
(X_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
) that is functorial in
T
, such that
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
is the kernel of the nilpotent endomorphism
N_{\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}}
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
, under the equivalence in
Theorem

2.24
.

(ii)

For any affine open subscheme
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subset X
with an unramified model
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
and a Frobenius endomorphism, the evaluation induces isomorphisms of
R_{0}[1/p]
-vector bundles that are compatible with connections, Frobenius structures, and monodromy operators

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}(U,U_{k})\simeq D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T),\quad\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}(U,U_{k},(0{N})^{a})\simeq D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T).

(iii)

Assume that
f:Y\to X
is a map of smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, with
f_{s}
the induced map on reduced special fibers.
Then we have natural injections of weak
F
-isocrystals

\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}:f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T},\quad\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f}:f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T}.

The maps are isomorphisms if
f
is finite étale.

(iv)

In the setting of
(iii)
, assume
f
is
p
-completely smooth.
Then both
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
and
\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f}
are isomorphisms of weak
F
-isocrystals.

(v)

The local system
T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) if and only if the weak
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
) is an
F
-isocrystal of rank
d
.

Under the assumptions, the map
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
(resp.
\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f}
) in
(iii)
is an isomorphism for any morphism
f:Y\to X
of smooth
p
-adic formal schemes.

Proof.

Part
(i)
and Part
(ii)
follow from
Proposition

5.17
and its local construction.
On the other hand, Part
(iii)
follows from
5.19
, and Part
(v)
follows from
Theorem

4.21
.
So it is left to check Part
(iv)
.

We first notice that if the map
f
is
p
-completely étale, to check that the injections
\iota_{*,f}
are isomorphic, by deformation theory, it suffices to assume
X
is smooth affine as in
Convention

3.11
and
f
is induced from a map of Frobenius equivariant unramified models
f_{0}:{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0}^{\prime})\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
.
Under the assumption, when the map
f
is finite étale, the claim follows from
Theorem

5.5
.
(iii)
.
So to extend the claim to general
p
-complete étale morphisms, we may assume the map
f
to be a (
p
-complete) open immersion.
In this case, we let
x
be a fixed closed point of
X
.
By shrinking
X
(and hence
Y
) to a neighborhood of
x
if necessary, since the statement is Zariski local with respect to
X
, we may assume
X
and
Y
are stable open neighborhoods of
x
for all the weak
F
-isocrystals in the statement (cf.
Definition

5.11
).
Under the assumption, the claim follows since by definition, the stable open neighborhood means that the
F
-isocrystals above satisfy the restriction formula with respect to further open immersions.

For a general
p
-completely smooth morphism
f
, thanks to the cases above, we may reduce to considering the case when the map
f
admits an Frobenius equivariant unramified model
f_{0}
, and the latter is given by the projection
X_{0}\times\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{W}\to X_{0}
, where
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{W}={\mathrm{Spf}}(W\left<x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right>)
.
We let
s_{0}:X_{0}\to X_{0}\times\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{W}
be the fiber at the origin.
By shrinking around any fixed point of
X
if necessary, we assume again that
X
is the stable open neighborhood of that point for all the weak
F
-isocrystals in the statement.
So all the weak
F
-isocrystals are now actual
F
-isocrystals, and the pullback functors in the statement are the usual pullback functors for
F
-isocrystals.
Here we also notice that the composition
f_{0}\circ s_{0}
is equal to the identity map
\mathrm{id}_{X_{0}}
.
If the injective map of flat connections
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
is not an isomorphism, by
[
Ogu84
, Thm. 4.1]
so is the pullback of
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
along
(s_{0})_{\eta}^{*}
.
On the other hand,
notice that the map of
F
-isocrystals
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},\mathrm{id}_{X}}
is an isomorphism.
The latter forces the map
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
to be surjective, thanks to the fact that the map of isocrystals
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
is compatible with the composition of
p
-adic formal schemes.
This finishes the proof of
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
, and the case for
\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f}
follows similarly.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

Here different from
[
TT19
]
, we make no assumption on the ramification degree of
K
.
In particular, the formal scheme
X
does not have to be defined over an unramified model.
Moreover, we emphasize that we make no assumption on the
p
-adic local system
T
.

Remark 5.22
.

Consider the setting of
Theorem

5.20
.
(iii)
(or
Proposition

5.19
).
We refer the morphisms
\iota_{*,f}
(
*={\mathrm{crys}},{\mathrm{st}}
) as the
base change morphisms
for
T
along the map
f
. This terminology is inspired by
[
Sta23
,
Tag 02N6
]
.
Note that thanks to the identification offered by
Lemma

5.1
,
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f})
is naturally viewed as an injection
f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}}^{*}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}))\hookrightarrow\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},f^{-1}_{\eta}(T^{\prime})})
.
Moreover, in the special case when the map
f
comes from a map of unramified models as in
Theorem

5.5
, we then obtain a commutative diagram:

(49)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}}^{*}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{*}_{\eta,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},f^{-1}_{\eta}(T^{\prime})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f_{\eta}^{-1}(T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\eta}^{*}\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Y}(\iota_{{\mathrm{st}},f})}
Lem.
5.2
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},f_{\eta}^{-1}(T)}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}

There is a completely analogous diagram for
\iota_{{\mathrm{crys}},f}
, which we do not spell out.

6.
Normalizations and finite covers

In the proof of the pointwise criteria later, one of the key ingredients is to descend the crystallinity or the semi-stability along suitable finite covers.
As a preparation, we discuss normalizations of rigid analytic spaces and formal schemes in this section. Then we will introduce the notion of effective sets and prove an
\ell
-adic analogue of the main theorems.

6.1.
Preliminaries

We first recall some basic facts from
[
Con99
]
.
As before, we let
K
be a
p
-adic field with perfect residue field
k
and the uniformizer
\pi
.
Unless otherwise noted, in this section we reserve the notation
(-)^{\wedge}
for completion with respect to
p
-adic topology.

To begin, recall that a topologically of finite type
K
-algebra
A
is an excellent ring.
Moreover, if
Z={\mathrm{Spa}}(A)
and
z\in Z
corresponds to a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq A
, then the analytic localization
\mathcal{O}_{Z,z}
is also excellent (
[
Con99
, Thm 1.1.3]
) and there is a natural local morphism
A_{(\mathfrak{m})}\to\mathcal{O}_{Z,z}
of Noetherian rings which becomes an isomorphism after completion.

We let
\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
be the category of locally Noetherian
p
-adic formal schemes
X
over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
such that the reduced special fiber
X_{s}
is a scheme locally of finite type over
k
.
Then
X\in\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
if and only if it is a
p
-adic formal scheme that is locally topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and an affine formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is an object of
\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
if and only if
R
is of a quotient of
\mathcal{O}_{K}\left<x_{1},\cdots,x_{m}\right>
for some
m,n\geq 0
(
[
dJ95a
, §7.1]
).
As a classical convention (cf.
[
Bos14
, §7.4]
), we call a formal scheme
admissible
if it is in
\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
and is flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
We also let
\mathsf{Rig}_{K}
be the category of rigid analytic spaces over
K
.
As usual, we denote the generic fiber functor
\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\to\mathsf{Rig}_{K}
by
X\mapsto X_{\eta}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
X\in\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
be an admissible formal scheme over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
. Then every classical point
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
extends to a morphism
x:{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})\to X
such that
x_{\eta}
is given by the rigid generic fiber of
x
.

Proof.

Assume that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(A)
is affine and let
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq A_{K}
be the maximal ideal which gives
x_{\eta}
, i.e.,
K(x_{\eta})=A_{K}/\mathfrak{m}
. Let
B
denote the image of
A
under the reduction map
A_{K}\to K(x_{\eta})=:K^{\prime}
. Then by
[
Bos14
, §8.3 Prop. 7]
and its proof,
B
is a local integral domain of dimension
1
such that
\mathcal{O}_{K}\subseteq B\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
. The point
x
is given by the composite
A\to B\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
.
∎

Definition 6.2
.

In the setting of
Proposition

6.1
, we call the closed point on
X_{s}
given by the image of
x_{s}
the
reduction
of
x_{\eta}
.

Let
X
be an object of
\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
or of
\mathsf{Rig}_{K}
.
Then
X
is said to have property
P
at a closed point
x\in X
for

P\in\{\text{regular, reduced, normal, Gorenstein, Cohen-MacCaulay}\}

if the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
has property P.
If
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(A)\in\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
is affine (resp.
X=\mathrm{Spa}(A)\in\mathsf{Rig}_{K}
is affinoid), then
X
has property
P
at every closed point if and only if the ring
A
does (
[
Con99
, Lem. 1.2.1]
).
Moreover, by the
normalization
of an affine (resp. affinoid) object
X
, we mean
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A^{\nu})
(resp.
\mathrm{Spa}(A^{\nu})
), where
A^{\nu}
is the integral closure of the ring
A/\mathrm{Nil}(A)
in its total ring of fractions, where
\mathrm{Nil}(A)
is the nilpotent radical of the ring
A
.

We will freely make use of the following fact:

Proposition 6.3
.

[
Con99
, Cor. 1.2.3, Thm 2.1.3]

If the map
{\mathrm{Spa}}(A^{\prime})\to{\mathrm{Spa}}(A)
(resp.
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A^{\prime})\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(A)
) is an open immersion, then the fiber product
{\mathrm{Spa}}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}A^{\nu})
(resp.
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}A^{\nu})
) is the normalization of
{\mathrm{Spa}}(A^{\prime})
(resp.
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A)
).
If
X\in\mathsf{FS}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
and
X^{\nu}\to X
is the normalization map of
X
, then
(X^{\nu})_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is the normalization of
X_{\eta}
.

Let
\mathsf{C}_{K}
denote the category of admissible
normal
affine formal schemes
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
such that the reduced special fiber
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
is irreducible and
X_{\eta}
is a smooth rigid space over
K
,
and we require the morphism to be a morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes whose mod
p
reduction is generically finite.
Let
\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}\subseteq\mathsf{C}_{K}
be the subcategory of those
X
that are smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
For each
X\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
, we define the following objects:

•

F\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\,
the fraction field of the reduced special fiber
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

•

\mathfrak{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\,
the prime ideal of
R
corresponding to the generic point of
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
, i.e.,
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
is the integral affine scheme
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R/\mathfrak{p})
.
Note that if
X\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}
, then
\mathfrak{p}(X)=\pi R
.

•

\mathcal{O}_{L}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
, which is a discrete valuation ring with residue field
F
.

•

L\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\,
the fraction field of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{O}_{L}[\pi^{-1}]
.

Note that as
R
is normal, Serre’s criterion
[
Sta23
, 031S]
implies that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a discrete valuation ring; moreover, if
\varpi
is its uniformizer of
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then we have
\pi=\varpi^{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, and
m=1
if and only if the mod
\pi
reduction
X_{k}
is generically reduced.
The notation
R^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{p}}
can be viewed as either
p
-adic or
\varpi
-adic completion.
When we need to emphasize the dependency on
X
, we shall write
F(X),\mathfrak{p}(X),\mathcal{O}_{L}(X),L(X)
for
F,\mathfrak{p},\mathcal{O}_{L},L
respectively.
It is then clear that
\mathcal{O}_{L}(-)
(resp.
L(-)
) defines a functor from
\mathsf{C}_{K}
to the category of complete discrete valuation rings (resp. complete discrete valuation fields).

Convention 6.4
.

Given
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
and
f\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
, we set
R_{f}
to be the localization
R[1/f]
and let
R_{\{f\}}
be the
p
-comopletion
(R_{f})^{\wedge}
.
By
shrinking
X
, we mean that we replace
X
by
X_{f}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{\{f\}})
for some
f\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
.
If there is any other formal scheme
X^{\prime}
that admits a map to
X
in the context, then we replace
X^{\prime}
by the open sub formal scheme
X^{\prime}\times_{X}X_{f}
accordingly.

Remark 6.5
.

We shall often implicitly use the fact that the functors
\mathcal{O}_{L}(-)
, and hence
L(-)
, are invariant under shrinking:
Given
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
and
X_{f}
for
f\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
, the open immersion
X_{f}\to X
induces an identification
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X_{f})=\mathcal{O}_{L}(X)
.
To see this, as
\mathfrak{p}R_{\{f\}}
is a prime ideal in
R_{\{f\}}
, it suffices to show that the natural morphism
R\to R_{\{f\}}
induces an isomorphism

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\pi^{n}\to(R_{\{f\}})_{\mathfrak{p}R_{\{f\}}}/\pi^{n}

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
The latter holds since the mod
\pi^{n}
reduction of
f
does not belong to the ideal
\mathfrak{p}R/\pi^{n}
.

6.2.
A Reduced fiber theorem

We give a variant of the reduced fiber theorem of
[
BLR95
]
for formal schemes in terms of normalizations, which is more similar to the classical version for schemes in
[
Sta23
,
Tag 09IL
]
.
The benefit of doing so is that normalizations have universal properties which allow us to construct morphisms to them.

We start with an elementary lemma:

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
R
be a normal integral domain over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and set
X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
.
Suppose that the mod
\pi
reduction
X_{k}
is irreducible and the set

\{x\in X_{k}\mid x=(\widetilde{x})_{k}\textit{ for some }\widetilde{x}\in X(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})\textit{ where $K^{\prime}/K$ is a finite unramified extension}\}

is Zariski dense in
X_{k}
.
Then
X_{k}
is generically reduced.

Proof.

It suffices to assume that
k
is algebraically closed.
Let
\eta_{k}\in X
be the generic point of the reduced special fiber
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
, which corresponds to a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subseteq A
.
By the normality assumption,
X
is regular in codimension
1
, so the local ring
A_{(\mathfrak{p})}
is a discrete valuation ring, and we denote its uniformizer by
\varpi
.
Let
i:\mathcal{O}_{K}\hookrightarrow A
be the structural morphism.
Then
i(\pi)=u\varpi^{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
u\in A_{(\mathfrak{p})}
.
Our goal is to show that
m=1
.

Suppose for the sake of contradiction that
m>1
.
Up to replacing
X
by an affine open neighborhood of
\eta_{k}
, we may assume that the elements
u,u^{-1}
and
\varpi
all lie in
A
.
By the density assumption, after shrinking there still exists a morphism
\widetilde{x}:A\to\mathcal{O}_{K}
such that
\widetilde{x}\circ i=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
.
Then by assumption, we have
\widetilde{x}(\varpi)=v\pi^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
and
v\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
, and

\pi=\widetilde{x}(i(\pi))=\widetilde{x}(u)\widetilde{x}(\varpi)^{m}=\widetilde{x}(u)v\pi^{mn}.

If
n=0
, then
\pi=\widetilde{x}(u)v
becomes a unit in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, which is a contradiction.
But if
n>1
, then
mn>1
and
1=\widetilde{x}(u)v\pi^{mn-1}
, so that
\pi
again becomes a unit in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, which is a contradiction.
Therefore, we must have
m=1
as desired.
∎

Now we state the version of the reduced fiber theorem that will be used later.

Theorem 6.7
.

(Reduced Fiber Theorem)

Let
X
be an admissible
p
-adic formal scheme over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, with
X_{\eta}
a smooth rigid space and
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
an irreducible scheme.
Then there exists a finite extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
with residue field
k^{\prime}
, such that the special fiber
((X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}})^{\nu})_{k^{\prime}}
of the normalization
(X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}})^{\nu}
is generically reduced along every irreducible component that dominates
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

Proof.

The main theorem of
[
BLR95
]
tells us that there exists some finite extension
K^{\prime}
and a flat topologically finite type
p
-adic
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
-formal scheme
Y
that has the reduced special fiber, together with a finite morphism
Y\to X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
inducing an isomorphism on the rigid generic fibers.
We fix this
K^{\prime}
for the moment.
For simplicity, below we use
\widetilde{X}
to abbreviate the normalization
(X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}})^{\nu}
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
K^{\prime}
is totally ramified over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
so that the residue field
k^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
is the same as
k
.

Note that the natural morphism
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta,K^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism as it is a normalization map (
[
Con99
, Thm 2.1.3]
) with the target being already normal.
In particular, the rigid generic fibers of
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}},Y
and
\widetilde{X}
are naturally identified.
Let
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}
be the maximal open formal subscheme of
Y
which is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
.
The reducedness of
Y_{k}
implies that it is generically smooth along every irreducible component, so that
(Y^{\mathrm{sm}})_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}Y^{\mathrm{sm}}\times_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})}{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
is Zariski dense in
Y_{k}
.
As
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}
is normal, its morphism to
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
factors through a morphism
\epsilon:Y^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\widetilde{X}
.
In summary, we get a commutative diagram of
p
-adic formal schemes as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{\mathrm{sm}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}

In the following, we call an irreducible component of
(\widetilde{X}_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}

good
if it dominates
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
.
We then claim that the special fiber
\epsilon_{k}:(Y^{\mathrm{sm}})_{k}\to\widetilde{X}_{k}
of the map
\epsilon
is dominant on each good irreducible component of
\widetilde{X}_{k}
.
First, we note that the map
Y_{k}\to X_{k}
is surjective on closed points.
Recall that for an admissible
p
-adic formal scheme, the specialization map from classical points on the rigid-analytic generic fiber to the closed points on the special fiber is surjective (
[
Bos14
, §8.3 Prop. 8]
).
Let
\widetilde{x}\in X_{\eta,K^{\prime}}
be a classical point which specializes to
x\in X_{k}
.
By identifying
\widetilde{x}
as a closed point in
Y_{\eta,K^{\prime}}\simeq X_{\eta,K^{\prime}}
, the point
\widetilde{x}
specializes to a point
y\in Y_{k}
.
Since specialization maps are compatible with morphisms between admissible
p
-adic formal schemes, by contemplating the generic and the special fibers of
Y\to X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
, we see the image of
y
under the map
Y_{k}\to X_{k}
is equal to
x
.
Moreover, as the map
Y_{k}\to X_{k}
is finite and
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}\subseteq Y_{k}
is Zariski dense, there exists an open dense subscheme
U\subseteq X_{k}
such that
U\times_{X_{k}}Y_{k}\subseteq Y^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}
.
Since
\widetilde{X}_{k}\to X_{k}
is also finite, on each good irreducible component
\widetilde{X}_{0}
of
(\widetilde{X}_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
, the image of a general closed point
x_{0}\in\widetilde{X}_{0}
along the map
\widetilde{X}_{k}\to X_{k}
lies in
U
.
So to show the dominance, it suffices to find a closed point
y\in Y^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}
such that
\epsilon_{k}(y)=x_{0}
, for which we apply the above argument again:
Choose a classical point
\widetilde{x}_{0}
on
\widetilde{X}_{\eta,K^{\prime}}\simeq Y_{\eta,K^{\prime}}
which specializes to
x_{0}
, and we let
y
be the specialization of
\widetilde{x}_{0}\in Y_{\eta,K^{\prime}}
to
Y_{k}
.
Then we claim that
y
is contained in
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}
with
\epsilon_{k}(y)=x_{0}
.
To see this, we notice that by the compatibility of specialization maps, as the image of
x_{0}
(denoted as
x
) along
\widetilde{X}_{k}\to X_{k}
is in
U
, the image of
y
along
Y_{k}\to X_{k}
, which is equal to
x
, is contained in
U
as well.
In particular, the point
y
is inside of
U\times_{X_{k}}Y_{k}
and thus in
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}
.
As a consequence, by the choice of
y_{0}
and the compatibility of specialization maps again, we see
\epsilon_{k}(y)=x_{0}
.
The relation of various points in the special fiber is summarized in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{0}\in\widetilde{X}_{0}=\text{reduction of $\widetilde{x}_{0}\in\widetilde{X}_{\eta,K^{\prime}}$}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{0}\in Y_{k}^{\mathrm{sm}}=\text{reduction of $\widetilde{x}_{0}\in\widetilde{Y}_{\eta,K^{\prime}}$}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\in U\subset X_{k}.}

By the smoothness, every closed point on
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}
lifts to an
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
-point on
Y^{\mathrm{sm}}
for some
K^{\prime\prime}/K^{\prime}
finite and
unramified
.
The preceding paragraph then implies that the same can be said about general closed points on a good irreducible component of
(\widetilde{X}_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
.
Hence we can conclude by
Lemma

6.6
.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Let
K^{\prime\prime}/K^{\prime}
be any finite extension of
p
-adic fields.
As in the first paragraph of the proof for
Theorem

6.7
, the conditions on
Y
relative to
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
remains unchanged if we replace
Y\to X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
by their base change along
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
.
In particular,
Theorem

6.7
still holds if we replace
K^{\prime}
by its any finite extension, including those
K^{\prime\prime}
that are Galois over
K
.

6.3.
Normal covers

Definition 6.9
.

Given
X\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
, we say that a finite morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
is a
normal cover
if
X^{\prime}\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
and the map of generic fibers
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is a finite étale cover.
Denote the category of normal covers of
X
by
\mathsf{N}(X)
, where the morphisms between objects are morphisms between
p
-adic formal
X
-schemes.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
and
X^{\prime}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime})\in\mathsf{N}(X)
.
Let
L\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X)
,
L^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X^{\prime})
,
\mathfrak{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{p}(X)
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{p}(X^{\prime})
, and let
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
be the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
in
L^{\prime}
.
Then we have:

(i)

There are canonical identifications

R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L}=(R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}=(R^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}})^{\wedge}=\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}.

(ii)

For every
f\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
, the map
X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}|_{X_{f}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X^{\prime}\times_{X}X_{f}
forms an object of
\mathsf{N}(X_{f})
. In particular, the restriction
(-)|_{X_{f}}
defines a natural functor
\mathsf{N}(X)\to\mathsf{N}(X_{f})
.

(iii)

R^{\prime}
is the integral closure of
R
in both
R^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}[\pi^{-1}]
and
R^{\prime}[\pi^{-1}]
.

(iv)

The natural morphism
\mathrm{Aut}(X^{\prime}/X)\to\mathrm{Aut}(X^{\prime}_{\eta}/X_{\eta})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We start with
(i)
.
Recall by definition that
\mathcal{O}_{L}=(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
.
Consider the natural map
\varepsilon:R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\to R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
obtained by base changing
R_{\mathfrak{p}}\to R_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\wedge}
along
R\to R^{\prime}
. Since
(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
is a Noetherian local ring and
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
is finite over
(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
, Krull’s intersection theorem
[
Sta23
, 00IP]
ensures that
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
is still
p
-adically separated.
Then we apply
[
Sta23
,
Tag 031B
]
to deduce that
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
is already
p
-complete.
Moreover, by taking completion, we obtain the map
\varepsilon^{\wedge}:(R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}\to R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}
.
It is easy to see that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varepsilon^{\wedge}/\pi^{n}
is an isomorphism, and hence so is
\varepsilon^{\wedge}
.
This together with the completeness above gives the first identification in
(i)
.
For the second, we note that as
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\subseteq R^{\prime}
is the unique prime above
\mathfrak{p}
, the multiplicative set
R^{\prime}\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
is the saturation of the multiplicative set
R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
inside
R^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}=R^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}
, whose
p
-completion is
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})
by definition.
Finally, to see that
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
, recall that the normality of
X^{\prime}
implies that
R^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}
(and hence
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})
) is a discrete valuation ring.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})
is integrally closed in its faction field
L^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})
is finite over
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, the ring
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime})
is in fact the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
in
L^{\prime}
, i.e., the subring
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
.

Part
(ii)
follows from
Proposition

6.3
.
For
(iii)
, simply note that by
[
Sta23
,
Tag 03GH
]
, the integral closure of
R
in either
R^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}[\pi^{-1}]
or
R^{\prime}[\pi^{-1}]
is finite over
R
, and hence finite over
R^{\prime}
.
But notice that
R^{\prime}
is already integral closed, hence it coincides with the integral closure of
R
. In fact, both
(ii)
and
(iii)
eventually reduce to the technical statement that
R
is an excellent and thus Nagata ring.
Finally,
(iv)
is a direct consequnece of
(iii)
.
∎

As an application of Gabber–Ramero’s approximation result, we have the following spreading-out equivalence on finite extensions of the generic point.
Below we use
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(*)
to denote the category of finite étale
*
-algebras.

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
X\in\mathsf{C}_{K}
, and let
L\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X)
.
Denote by
\mathsf{DVF}
the category of fraction fields of discrete valuation rings.
The functor
L(-):\mathsf{C}_{K}\to\mathsf{DVF}
then induces an equivalence of categories

L_{X}:\varinjlim\mathsf{N}(X_{f})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)^{\circ}

where the colimit is taken over
f\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)^{\circ}
denotes the category of the connected objects of
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)
, i.e., finite field extensions of
L
.

We view
R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
as a filtered direct system such that
f\leq g
if the open immersion
X_{\{g\}}\to X
factors through
X_{\{f\}}
(e.g., when
f\mid g
).
We refer the reader to
[
Art62
, III.3]
for the definition of a filtered colimit of categories that is used here.

Proof.

We first prove
essential surjectivity
.
Let
L\subseteq L^{\prime}
be a finite field extension.
We need to show that, up to shrinking
X
, there exists a normal cover
X^{\prime}
of
X
such that
L^{\prime}\cong L(X^{\prime})
.

First, one quickly checks that
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, which by definition is
(\varinjlim R_{f})^{\wedge}
, is also equal to
(\varinjlim R_{\{f\}})^{\wedge}
.
Indeed, the natural morphism
\varepsilon:\varinjlim R_{f}\to\varinjlim R_{\{f\}}
induces a morphism between their completions, and the latter is an isomorphism since it is true for each reduction
\varepsilon/\pi^{n}
and colimits commute with tensor products.
Below we write
T
for the filtered colimit
\varinjlim R_{\{f\}}
, so that
T^{\wedge}=\mathcal{O}_{L}
.
The reason to consider
T
instead of
\varinjlim R_{f}
is that
(T,(\pi))
is now a Henselian pair by
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0FWT
]
, so that we we may apply Gabber–Ramero’s approximation result (extending that of Elkik)
[
GR03
, Prop. 5.4.53]
, which tells us the following: Base change along the map
T[\pi^{-1}]\to T^{\wedge}[\pi^{-1}]=L
induces an equivalence of categories
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(T[\pi^{-1}])\simeq\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)
.
Since
L^{\prime}\in\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)
, we can find some
V\in\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(T[\pi^{-1}])
such that
L\otimes_{T[\pi^{-1}]}V=L^{\prime}
.
As colimits commute with localization,
T[\pi^{-1}]=\varinjlim(R_{\{f\}}[\pi^{-1}])
.
Now we have

(50)

\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)\simeq\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(T[\pi^{-1}])=\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(\varinjlim(R_{\{f\}}[\pi^{-1}]))\simeq\varinjlim\,\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(R_{\{f\}}[\pi^{-1}]).

For a reference of the second isomorphism, see
[
Art62
, III.3]
.
This implies that for some
f\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
, there exists an object
V_{f}\in\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(R_{\{f\}}[\pi^{-1}])
such that
V=V_{f}\otimes_{R_{\{f\}}[\pi^{-1}]}T[\pi^{-1}]
(so that
L^{\prime}=L\otimes_{R_{\{f\}}[\pi^{-1}]}V_{f}
).

We now shrink
X
to
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{\{f\}})
(i.e., replace
R
by
R_{\{f\}}
), after which we have a finite étale
R[\pi^{-1}]
-algebra
V
inside
L^{\prime}
such that
L^{\prime}=L\otimes_{R}V
.
Let
R^{\prime}
be the integral closure of
R
in
V
.
Then we have
R^{\prime}[\pi^{-1}]=V
and that
R^{\prime}
is finite over
R
.
Note also that by construction
R^{\prime}
is flat (as
R^{\prime}\subseteq L^{\prime}
) and topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, so
X^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime})
is admissible.
Moreover, we claim that
(X_{k}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{red}}
has at most a single irreducible component that dominates
X_{k}
.
Suppose not. Then there exists an
f_{0}\in R\smallsetminus\pi R
such that for all
f_{0}\mid f
, the fiber product
X^{\prime}\times_{X}X_{\{f\}}
is disconnected, which would imply
\varinjlim(R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\{f\}})=R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is not an integral domain.
On the other hand, as
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a discrete valuation ring, the morphism
R_{\mathfrak{p}}\to R_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\wedge}
is faithfully flat and in particular injective, and hence so is its base change along
R\to R^{\prime}
, i.e.,
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\to R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\wedge}
.
But by
Lemma

6.10
.
(i)

R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\wedge}=R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L}
is a subalgebra of
L^{\prime}
, which must be an integral domain.
This gives the desired contradiction to affirm the claim. Therefore, we may shrink
X
again to make
(X_{k}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{red}}
integral.
Now
X^{\prime}
becomes an object of
\mathsf{N}(X)
such that
L(X^{\prime})=L^{\prime}
, which is what we are looking for.

Finally we show that the functor
L_{X}
is
fully faithful
.
Faithfulness follows from
Lemma

6.10
.
(i)
.
The fullness amounts to the following statement: Suppose for
i=1,2
we are given the data
X^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathsf{N}(X_{f_{i}})
,
L_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X^{\prime}_{i})
and
\tau\in\mathrm{Hom}_{L}(L_{1},L_{2})
. Then there exists
g\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
divisible by both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
such that
\tau
is induced by a morphism
\widetilde{\tau}:X^{\prime}_{1}|_{X_{g}}\to X^{\prime}_{2}|_{X_{g}}
in
\mathsf{N}(X_{g})
.
To prove this statement, we are allowed to shrink
X
, so we may simply assume that
X=X_{f_{1}}=X_{f_{2}}
.
Suppose that
X_{i}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{i})
and
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{p}(X_{i})\subseteq R_{i}
.
By considering the equivalence of categories in (
50
) again, we find some
g\in R\smallsetminus\mathfrak{p}
such that
\tau
, which is a morphism in
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(L)
, comes from a morphism
\widetilde{\tau}:(R_{1}\otimes_{R}R_{\{g\}})[\pi^{-1}]\to(R_{2}\otimes_{R}R_{\{g\}})[\pi^{-1}]
.
As
X_{i}|_{X_{g}}
is nothing but
{\mathrm{Spf}}((R_{i}\otimes_{R}R_{\{g\}})^{\wedge})
, by
Lemma

6.10
.
(ii)

\widetilde{\tau}
restricts to a morphism
X_{1}|_{X_{g}}\to X_{2}|_{X_{g}}
as desired.
∎

6.4.
Primitive inseparable covers

Let
f:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be a morphism of schemes in characteristic
p
.
We say that
f
is
purely inseparable
if it is injective on the underlying topological spaces and for every point
z\in Z^{\prime}
, the residue field extension
k(z)\hookrightarrow k(f(z))
is purely inseparable.
If
Z
is normal, Noetherian, and integral and
Z^{\prime}
is integral, then a finite morphism
f:Z^{\prime}\to Z
is purely inseparable if and only if the induced extension between their fraction fields is purely inseparable (
[
Liu02
, p. 208]
together with Nagata’s compactification result).

Definition 6.12
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}
. We say that a finite flat morphism
f:Y\to X
is a
primitive inseparable cover
if the special fiber
f_{k}
is purely inseparable of degree
p
and the generic fiber
f_{\eta}
is a Galois cover with group
G\cong\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
.

We recall a simple consequence of the Cohen structure theorem.

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
(V,(\pi))
be a complete discrete valuation ring with residue field
k
.
Let
(R,\mathfrak{m})
be a complete local
V
-algebra with Krull dimension
n+1
for some
n\geq 0
.
Suppose that the natural morphism of residue fields
V/\pi V\to R/\mathfrak{m}
is an isomorphism, and the ring
R/\pi R
is regular of dimension
n
.
Then we have
R\cong V[\![t_{1},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
.

Proof.

By Cohen structure theorem
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0C0S
]
, we know
R/\pi R\cong k[\![x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}]\!]
.
Fix such an isomorphism and lift
x_{i}
’s to
\widetilde{x}_{i}\in\mathfrak{m}
.
Then there is a natural morphism
V[\![t_{1},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]\to R
sending
t_{i}
to
\widetilde{x}_{i}
, which is surjective by applying
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0315
]
onto the ideal
(\pi,t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})
.
On the other hand, as
V[\![t_{1},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
is an integral domain and has the same dimension as
R
, the morphism must also be injective—otherwise the quotient would have strictly smaller dimension.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
X,Y\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}
.
Suppose that
f:Y\to X
be a primitive inseparable cover.
Then up to shrinking
X
and replacing
Y
by its restriction, there exists for every closed point
x_{0}\in X_{s}
,

•

a finite unramified extension
K^{\prime}/K
,

•

a totally ramified extension
K^{\prime\prime}/K^{\prime}
of degree
p
,

•

a point
x\in X(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
lifting
x_{0}
,

such that the fiber product
Y\times_{X}x
is a single
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
-point of
Y
.

Proof.

Suppose that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(A)
and
Y={\mathrm{Spf}}(B)
.
Then we may view
f
as a map between
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras
A\to B
.
By abuse of notation, we shall also use
f
to denote other morphisms between algebras which are naturally induced by
A\to B
.

By assumption, the fraction field
F(Y)=\mathrm{Frac}(B_{k})
is generated over
F(X)=\mathrm{Frac}(A_{k})
via the embedding
f:F(X)\hookrightarrow F(Y)
by an element
g
such that
g^{p}=t
for some
t\in F(X)
.
Up to shrinking
X
and
Y
, below we shall assume that
t\in A_{k}
and
g\in B_{k}
.
Then we note that
dt\in\Omega^{1}_{A_{k}/k}
is necessarily nonzero.
Indeed, Cartier isomorphism tells us that

\ker(F_{*}(d):F_{*}(A_{k})\to F_{*}(\Omega_{X_{k}/k}))=\mathcal{O}_{X^{(p)}_{k}},

where
F:X_{k}\to X_{k}^{(p)}
is the relative Frobenius of
X_{k}
.
Hence if
dt=0
, then
t
has to be a
p
th power, which implies that
g\in f(F(X))
and contradicts our assumption.
Moreover, by the noetherian assumption on
A
, we know the
p
-completely flat morphism
A\to B
is actually flat (
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0912
]
).

Up to further shrinking
X
, we shall assume that
dt
is nowhere vanishing at closed points of
X
.
Let
x_{0}\in X(k^{\prime})
be any point for a finite extension
k^{\prime}/k
of residue fields, and let
K^{\prime}
be the unramified extension of
K
lifting
k^{\prime}/k
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}\subseteq A
be the maximal ideal defined by
x_{0}
.
Let
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
denote the completion of
A
at
\mathfrak{m}
.
Let
n
be the relative dimension of
X
and
Y
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Then by
Lemma

6.13
, we have an isomorphism of complete local rings
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
, which we fix for now.
Similarly, let
y_{0}\in Y(k^{\prime})
be the unique pre-image of
x_{0}
and
\mathfrak{n}\subseteq B
be the maximal ideal given by
y_{0}
.
We also fix an isomorphism
B^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{n}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![s_{0},\cdots,s_{n}]\!]
.
Below we shall view
A
and
B
as subalgebras of
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
B^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{n}}
respectively and the same applies to their mod
\pi
reductions.
Up to replacing
g
by
g-g(y_{0})
and
t
by
t-g(y_{0})^{p}
, we assume that
t(x_{0})=0
and
g(y_{0})=0
.
This implies that when viewed as elements in
k^{\prime}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
and
k^{\prime}[\![s_{0},\cdots,s_{n}]\!]
respectively,
t
and
g
have no constant terms.

We then claim that there exists an automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
which sends
t_{0}
to an element
\widetilde{t}
such that
\widetilde{t}\mod\pi=t
.
By the assumption that
dt
does not vanish at
x_{0}
, we know
\partial t/\partial t_{i}\neq 0
for some
i
.
Up to renaming variables we assume
i=0
.
Let
\widetilde{t}\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
be any lift of
t
with no constant term.
Then we consider the endomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
which sends
t_{0}
to
\widetilde{t}
and fixes
t_{i}
for each
i>0
.
As a consequence, since
\partial t/\partial t_{0}\neq 0
, the endomorphism has an invertible Jacobian (as a matrix with
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
-entries) and hence is an automorphism by the inverse function theorem on power series rings.

The above allows us to assume that the mod
\pi
reduction of
t_{0}
is
t
.
Similarly, let
\widetilde{g}\in B^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{n}}
be an element with no constant term such that
\widetilde{g}\mod\pi=g
.
Note that
f
induces a morphism
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![s_{0},\cdots,s_{n}]\!]
and we shall denote by
f_{i}
the image of
t_{i}
.
Then our assumption that
g^{p}=t
translates to
\widetilde{g}^{p}+\pi h=f_{0}
for some
h\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![s_{0},\cdots,s_{n}]\!]
.
Let
h_{0}
be the constant term of
h
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
be an element such that
u\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}^{\times}
and
u\neq h_{0}
if
h_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}^{\times}
.
We set
x
to be the
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
-point of
X
given by sending
t_{0}
to
u\pi
and
t_{i}
to
0
for
i>0
, and write
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}(K)
for its generic fiber.
Then by assumption, the point
x
specializes to
x_{0}
in the special fiber.
Let
y_{\eta}\in Y_{\eta}
be any closed point in the preimage of
x_{\eta}
and set
K^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K(y)
. Then the surjection
y_{\eta}:B[1/p]\to K^{\prime\prime}
restricts to a surjection
y:B\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
, i.e.,
y_{\eta}
extends to a point
y\in Y(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}})
(cf.
[
Bos14
, §8.3, Prop. 8]
).
As the point
y
specializes to
y_{0}
, the corresponding map of
y_{0}
lifts to a morphism
y\colon\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![s_{0},\cdots,s_{n}]\!]\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
.
Hence we get a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![s_{0},\cdots,s_{n}]\!]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{i}\mapsto f_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}

Suppose that
y
is given by
s_{i}\mapsto\beta_{i}
for some pesudo-uniformizer
\beta_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then by looking at the images of
t_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}[\![t_{0},\cdots,t_{n}]\!]
under the maps above, the diagram yields the following equation in
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
:

(51)

\widetilde{g}(\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n})^{p}+\pi h(\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n})=f_{0}(\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n})=u\pi.

As
\widetilde{g}
has no constant term,
u\neq h_{0}
, and
\mathrm{val}_{\pi}(\beta_{i})>0
for each
i
, the above equation tells us that for some
i
,
\mathrm{val}_{\pi}(\beta_{i})\leq p^{-1}
.
Indeed, otherwise
\mathrm{val}_{\pi}(\widetilde{g}(\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n})^{p})>1
, but by the choice of the element
u
, we know
\mathrm{val}_{\pi}(u\pi-\pi h(\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{n}))=1
.
As a consequence, we see
[K^{\prime\prime}:K^{\prime}]\geq p
.
On the other hand, as
f_{\eta}
is a finite Galois cover of degree
p
, we have
[K^{\prime\prime}:K^{\prime}]\leq p
.
So the above equality must be achieved, i.e.,
[K^{\prime\prime}:K^{\prime}]=p
and we have
\mathrm{val}_{\pi}(\beta_{i})=p^{-1}
.

Finally, to see that the point
y
is the fiber product
Y\times_{X}x
, we first notice that this is clear for the generic fiber: because
Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is a finite étale cover of degree
p
, and
K(y_{\eta})=K^{\prime}
is a degree
p
ramified cover of
K=K(x_{\eta})
.
The claim in the level of formal schemes then follows from the flatness assumption of
A\to B
: the base change
Y\times_{X}x
is flat of degree
p
over
x={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
and contains
y={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime\prime}})
as a closed subscheme, hence must equal to
y
itself.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

Here is a prototypical example for
Proposition

6.14
: Suppose that
\mathcal{O}_{K}
contains all
p
th roots of unity and consider the one-dimensional torus
X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\left<t^{\pm}\right>)
. Its endomorphism given by
t\mapsto t^{p}
satisfies the hypothesis of
Proposition

6.14
. Note that
X(K)
is identified with
\{x\in\mathcal{O}_{K}\mid\mathrm{val}_{\pi}(x)=0\}
. The proof suggests that, to find a point
x\in X(K)
such that the cover is ramified at
x
, we may take
x=1+\pi
. Indeed, the unique
y\in X_{\eta}
over
x
has the generic residue field
K^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K(\alpha)
for
\alpha^{p}=1+\pi
, and the minimal polynomial of
\alpha-1
is a degree
p
Eisenstein polynomial, so
[K^{\prime\prime}:K]=p
as desired.

6.5.
Towers of covers

In this subsection, we show that up to replacing the base field by a finite extension and shrinking the base
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, a finite extension of the field
L(X)
always comes from a tower of finite covers of
X
consisting of a finite Galois cover followed by a tower of primitive inseparable covers.

Proposition 6.16
.

Suppose that
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}
are of the same relative dimension over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
X^{\prime}\to X
is a finite morphism such that the induced morphism
L(X)\to L(X^{\prime})
is a Galois extension.
Then up to shrinking
X
, there exists a tower of finite covers

X^{\prime}=X_{m}\to X_{m-1}\to\cdots\to X_{0}\to X

for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
, such that
X_{0}\to X
is a Galois étale cover and
X_{i+1}\to X_{i}
are primitive inseparable covers for
i\geq 1
.

Proof.

Let
L\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X)
, let
L^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X^{\prime})
, and denote their residue fields by
F
and
F^{\prime}
.
By the smoothness assumptions on
X
and
X^{\prime}
, the element
\pi
is an uniformizer for both
\mathcal{O}_{L}
and
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
.
In particular, the extension
\mathcal{O}_{L}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
is weakly unramified in the sense of
[
Sta23
, 09E4]
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
be the maximal subextension unramified over
\mathcal{O}_{L}
and
F_{0}
be its residue field. Then
F_{0}
is the maximal separable extension of
F
in
F^{\prime}
, so that
F^{\prime}/F
is purely inseparable.
We briefly recall the argument for the reader’s convenience: Since
\mathcal{O}_{L}
is a complete discrete valuation ring, any finite separable extension
F^{\prime\prime}
of
F
gives an unramified extension
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime\prime}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime\prime}}
is necessarily of the form
\mathcal{O}_{L}[\alpha]/(f(\alpha))
where
f(x)\in\mathcal{O}_{L}[x]
is a polynomial whose reduction in
F^{\prime\prime}[x]
is separable.
By Hensel’s lemma, if
F^{\prime\prime}
embeds into
F^{\prime}
, then the equation
f(x)=0
is solvable in
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
and hence
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime\prime}}
embeds into
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
.

Let
G\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Gal}}(L^{\prime}/L_{0})
. Since
L^{\prime}/L_{0}
is weakly unramified and
F^{\prime}/F_{0}
is purely inseparable,
G
is a
p
-group.
By Jordan-Hölder theorem, we may take a composition series

1\unlhd G_{m-1}\unlhd\cdots\unlhd G=G_{0}

for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then being a simple
p
-group,
H_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}G_{i}/G_{i+1}
is necessarily isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
.
The compoistion series gives a filtration

L_{0}\subseteq L_{1}\subseteq L_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq L_{m}=L^{\prime}

such that
{\mathrm{Gal}}(L^{\prime}/L_{i})=G_{i}
.
Let
F_{i}
be the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{L_{i}}
.
Then since each
\mathcal{O}_{L_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{L_{i+1}}
is weakly unramified, the purely inseparable extension
F_{i+1}/F_{i}
has degree exactly
p
.

Finally, we note that for any
X^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{N}(X)
such that
\mathcal{O}_{L}(X^{\prime\prime})
is weakly unramified over
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, the special fiber
X^{\prime\prime}_{k}
is generically reduced, and hence generically smooth over
k
.
After shrinking
X
, the cover
X^{\prime\prime}
becomes smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Moreover, by the generic smoothness of the maps of special fibers, we can assume that the map
X^{\prime\prime}_{k}\to X_{k}
is flat.
This implies that the map
X^{\prime\prime}\to X
is
p
-completely flat, and by the noetherian assumption on
X
, we know the
p
-completely flat morphism
X^{\prime\prime}\to X
is actually flat (
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0912
]
).
Now the conclusion follows easily from
Theorem

6.11
and the preceding paragraph.
∎

In practice,
Proposition

6.16
will be used in conjunction with
Theorem

6.17
below.

Theorem 6.17
.

Suppose that
X\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}
has a geometrically connected special fiber and let
L^{\prime}
be any finite Galois extension of
L\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L(X)
.
Then up to shrinking
X
, there exists

•

a finite Galois extension
\mathcal{K}/K
,

•

a commutative diagram of finite Galois field extensions as below

(52)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L^{\prime}},}

•

and a normal cover
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{X}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{K}}
,

such that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.

It will be clear in the proof that
L\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}
is indeed a field and is nothing but
L(\mathcal{X})
.
Before proving
Theorem

6.17
, we recall an elementary fact on field extensions.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
E\subseteq F
be a finite Galois field extension and
E\subseteq E^{\prime}
be any other finite separable extension. Set
d\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[F:E]
. Then there is a decomposition
F\otimes_{E}E^{\prime}\simeq\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}F_{i}
for some
m\mid d
, such that each
F_{i}
is a Galois extension of
E^{\prime}
of degree
d/m
.

Proof.

Let
f(x)\in E[x]
be a monic irreducible polynomial such that
E^{\prime}\simeq E[x]/(f(x))
.
Let
f_{0}(x)
be a factor of
f(x)
in
F[x]
and let
H
be the stabilizer of
f_{0}(x)
under the natural action of
G\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Gal}}(F/E)
on
F[x]
.
Let
\sigma_{0}=1,\cdots,\sigma_{m-1}
be representatives of
G/H
. Then we must have

f(x)=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}f_{i}(x)\text{ where $f_{i}(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sigma_{i}(f(x))$.}

Indeed, the right hand side is defined over
E
and divides
f(x)
. But
f(x)
is monic and irreducible in
E[x]
, so they must be equal.
Since
E^{\prime}/E
is separable, roots of
f(x)
(in some algebraic closure of
E
) are distinct, so that
f_{i}(x)
’s are pairwise coprime in
F[x]
.
Therefore, the Chinese remainder theorem gives a canonical decomposition
F\otimes_{E}E^{\prime}\simeq\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}F_{i}
for
F_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}F[x]/(f_{i}(x))
.
The action of
H_{i}
on
F_{i}
fixes
E^{\prime}
, so that
H_{i}\leq\mathrm{Aut}(F_{i}/E^{\prime})
. However, one easily checks that
|H_{i}|=[F_{i}:E^{\prime}]=d/m
.
Hence
F_{i}/E^{\prime}
is Galois with
{\mathrm{Gal}}(F_{i}/E^{\prime})=H_{i}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

6.17
.

By
Theorem

6.11
, after shrinking
X
we can find a normal cover
X^{\prime}
over
X
such that
L^{\prime}
can be identified with
L(X^{\prime})
.
By
Theorem

6.7
, there exists a finite extension
\mathcal{K}
over
K
such that the special fiber
(X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}})^{\nu}_{\kappa}
of the normalization
(X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}})^{\nu}
of
X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
is generically reduced along every irreducible component which dominates
X_{k}
, where
\kappa
is the residue field of
\mathcal{K}
.
Note that by
Remark

6.8
, we are allowed to replace
\mathcal{K}
by its normal closure over
K
and assume that
\mathcal{K}
is Galois over
K
.
After shrinking
X
again, we may then assume that
(X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}})^{\nu}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
.
Let
(X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}})^{\nu}=\coprod_{i=0}^{m-1}\mathcal{X}_{i}
be its decomposition into connected components. Then each
\mathcal{X}_{i}
is an object of
\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{K}}
.
Below we shall suppose that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
, and
X^{\prime}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime})
, so that
\mathcal{X}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{R})
is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
for
\mathcal{R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
.
We also let
\varpi
be the uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
.

Recall that
X_{k}
is assumed to be geometrically connected, so that
\mathcal{X}
is also connected.
By the smoothness of
\mathcal{X}
over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
,
the ring
\mathcal{R}
is an integral domain and
\mathcal{R}_{(\varpi)}=\mathcal{R}\otimes_{R}R_{(\pi)}
is a discrete valuation ring.
Moreover, we have (cf.
Lemma

6.10
.(a))

\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}=(R_{(\pi)})^{\wedge}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}=(R_{(\pi)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}})^{\wedge}=(\mathcal{R}_{(\varpi)})^{\wedge}.

This implies that the tensor product
\mathcal{L}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}
is isomorphic to
L(\mathcal{X})
.
In particular,
\mathcal{L}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}L\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}
is a field.
Let
\mathcal{R}_{i}
be the finite
\mathcal{R}
-algebra such that
\mathcal{X}_{i}={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{R}_{i})
. Set
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}=\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathcal{X}_{i})=(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{i,(\varpi)})^{\wedge}
and
\mathcal{L}_{i}=L(\mathcal{X}_{i})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}[\varpi^{-1}]
.
As one would expect, we have:

Lemma 6.19
.

L^{\prime}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}=\prod_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{i}
.

Proof.

Let
T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes_{R}R_{(\pi)}
.
As the ring
R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}
is finite over
R
, we have
T^{\wedge}=R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes_{R}(R_{(\pi)})^{\wedge}=R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L}
.
Since
T
is a finite
R_{(\pi)}
-algebra and
T[\pi^{-1}]
is normal (in fact a product of fields), we may switch the order of normalization and
p
-completion thanks to
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0BG9
]

17
17

17

Here to apply the result, we implicitly use the fact that for an
R_{(\pi)}
-algebra, the completion with respect to the maximal ideal of
R_{(\pi)}
is just
p
-adic completion.
, and get

(T^{\wedge})^{\nu}\simeq T^{\nu}\otimes_{R_{(\pi)}}(R_{(\pi)})^{\wedge}=(T^{\nu})^{\wedge}.

On the other hand, as normalization commutes with localization
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0307
]
, we have

T^{\nu}=(R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}})^{\nu}\otimes_{R}R_{(\pi)}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{i,(\varpi)}.

Combined with the previous isomorphism that
(T^{\wedge})^{\nu}\simeq(T^{\nu})^{\wedge}
, we then get

(53)

(R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\nu}=(T^{\wedge})^{\nu}\simeq(T^{\nu})^{\wedge}=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}(\mathcal{R}_{i,(\varpi)})^{\wedge}=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}.

Inverting
\varpi
and using that normalization commutes with localization again, the left end term in
Equation

53
becomes

(54)

(R^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\nu}[\varpi^{-1}]=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}[\varpi^{-1}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\nu}=(\mathcal{K}\otimes_{K}L^{\prime})^{\nu}=\mathcal{K}\otimes_{K}L^{\prime},

where we used
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{L}=\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}
in the second equality.
Now combine (
53
) and (
54
), we get the result.
∎

Note that the extensions
L\subseteq L^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}\subseteq\mathcal{L}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}
are both finite and Galois.
Therefore, by
Lemma

6.18
,
\mathcal{L}_{0}
is a Galois extension of both
L^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{K}
. Hence we obtain the diagram (
52
) by setting
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{L}_{0}
.
Accordingly, we set
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{X}_{0}
. Then being a connected component of
(X^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}})^{\nu}
,
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{K}}
.
Moreover,
Equation

53
implies that
\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, so we are done.
∎

Remark 6.20
.

It is sometimes convenient to apply
Theorem

6.17
together with the following observation: Suppose that
X
is a connected
p
-adic formal scheme of finite type over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
. Then there exists a finite unramified extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
such that the structure morphism
X\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
factors through
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, where
X
has geometrically connected special fiber as a
p
-adic formal scheme over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
. To see this, since
X_{s}
is a connected
k
-variety,
\pi_{0}(X_{s})={\mathrm{Spec}}(k^{\prime})
for some finite extension
k^{\prime}
of
k
, so that
X_{s}
is geometrically connected over
k^{\prime}
(
[
Liu02
, §10.2 Prop. 2.18]
).
Let
K^{\prime}
be the corresponding finite unramified extension of
K
. Then the formal étaleness of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
implies that the structure morphism
X_{s}\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(k^{\prime})
lifts unqiuely to a morphism
X\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
.

6.6.
Effective sets and an
\ell
-adic Analogue

In this section, we introduce the notion of the
effective set
and prove the
\ell
-adic analogue of the pointwise criterion.

We start with the following two lemmas that check that the purity result of branch locus for schemes also hold true for suitable formal schemes.

Lemma 6.21
.

Let
X
be a connected and admissible
p
-adic formal scheme over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
. If the mod
\pi
special fiber
X_{k}
is reduced, then
X_{\eta}
is connected.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the case when
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine. Then
X_{\eta}={\mathrm{Spa}}(R_{K})
is connected if and only if
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{K})
is connected (
[
Con08
, Ex. 2.4.9]
). Therefore, we reduce to showing that the scheme
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
has connected generic fiber, which follows from
[
Sta23
, 055J]
.
∎

We remark that the reducedness assumption on
X_{k}
is important. Otherwise
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}[x]/(x^{2}-\pi^{2}))
would produce a counterexample.

Lemma 6.22

(Purity of branch locus)
.

Let
X
be a regular admissible formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with an irreducible and normal reduced special fiber
X_{s}
, and let
U\subseteq X
be a non-empty formal open subscheme. Then we have the following:

(i)

The restriction functor
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X)\to\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U)
is fully faithful.

(ii)

The restriction functor
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X)\to\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{\eta})
is faithful.

(iii)

The restriction functor
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{\eta})\to\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U_{\eta})
is faithful.

(iv)

The induced functor

(55)

\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X)\to\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{\eta})\times_{\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U_{\eta})}\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U)

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from the simple facts that
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X)=\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{s})
,
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U)=\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U_{s})
and
\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{s})\to\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(U_{s})
is fully faithful (
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0BQI
]
).
Then we consider
(ii)
and
(iii)
simultanenously. This can be checked Zariski-locally on
X
, so we may assume that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine. Take some
U
open in
R
and
X^{\prime}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime})\in\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X)
.
We may assume that
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{\{f\}})
for some
f\in R
which does not vanish on
X_{s}
. Now
(ii)
(resp.
(iii)
) follows from the fact that
R^{\prime}\to R^{\prime}_{\{f\}}
(resp.
R^{\prime}\to R^{\prime}[1/p]
) is injective.

Finally, we consider
(iv)
. The full-faithfulness of (
55
) follows from
(i)
,
(ii)
, and
(iii)
.
Then, by standard gluing arguments, to prove essential surjectivity we reduce to considering the case when
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine as before.
Let
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{\eta})
be any object, which we may assume to be connected.
Let
X^{\prime}
be the normalization of
X
in
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
, so that
X^{\prime}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R^{\prime})
for some finite
R
-algebra
R^{\prime}
.
By
Proposition

6.3
, the given hypotheses tells us that
R[1/p]\to R^{\prime}[1/p]
is étale and for some
f
which does not vanish on
X_{s}
, the restriction
R_{\{f\}}\to R^{\prime}_{\{f\}}
is étale. We shall view
X^{\prime}
and
X
as formal completions of the schemes
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}:={\mathrm{Spec}}(R^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{X}:={\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
along their corresponding special fibers.
Then it suffices to show that the morphism between the schemes
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}\to\widetilde{X}
is étale.
As both schemes are locally noetherian, we are allowed to apply
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0BMB
]
, for which the assumptions (1)-(4) in
loc. cit.
are clear, and we check item (5) below.

Let
x^{\prime}\in\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{k}=X^{\prime}_{k}
be a closed point such that
f(x^{\prime})=0
and let
y\in\widetilde{X}^{\prime}
be any point which specializes to
x^{\prime}
and is of codimension
1
.
If the characteristic of the residue field of
y
is zero, then we are done because
R[1/p]\to R^{\prime}[1/p]
is étale. Otherwise,
y
must be the generic point of an irreducible component of
\widetilde{X}^{\prime}_{k}=X^{\prime}_{k}
, and since the function
f
does not vanish on
X_{s}
, we know
f(y)\neq 0
.
Since the map
R\to R^{\prime}
is finite, the ring
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\{f\}}
is thus
p
-complete and the natural morphism
R^{\prime}\otimes_{R}R_{\{f\}}\to R^{\prime}_{\{f\}}
is an isomorphism.
Finally, as the unramified locus of a morphism locally of finite type is invariant under arbitrary base change
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0475
]
, the fact that
R_{\{f\}}\to R^{\prime}_{\{f\}}
is unramifed at
y
implies that
R\to R^{\prime}
is also unramified at
y
.
∎

Construction 6.23
.

Let
X
be a connected smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Let
b\in X_{\eta}
be a classical point. Let
b_{0}\in X_{k}
be the closed point given by the reduction of
b
and choose geometric points
\bar{b},\bar{b}_{0}
over
b,b_{0}
respectively. We write
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})
for the
algebraic fundamental group
of the rigid analytic space (cf.
[
dJ95b
]
). Namely,
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})
is the automorphism group of the fiber functor from the category of finite étale covers of
X_{\eta}
to that of sets. Then there is a natural specialization map
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})\to\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X_{k},\bar{b}_{0})
, and we define the kernel of which to be the
global inertia group
and denote it by
I_{X}
as before. Now for each classical point
x\in X_{\eta}
, we choose a geometric point
\bar{x}
over
x
. Moreover, we choose an étale path connecting
\bar{x}
and
\bar{b}
, thereby obtaining an isomorphism
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{x})\simeq\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})
. Then we define
I_{x}
to be the inertia subgroup of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x)}=\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(x,\bar{x})
, which up to change of base points admits a map to
I_{X}
.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{x}
denote the union of conjugates of
\mathrm{im}(I_{x}\to I_{X})
. It is clear that
\mathcal{I}_{x}
depends only on
x
and not on the choice of
\bar{x}
or the étale path connecting
\bar{x}
and
\bar{b}
.

Lemma 6.24
.

In
Construction

6.23
, the specialization map
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})\to\pi_{1}(X_{k},\bar{b}_{0})
is surjective. As a consequence, a finite étale cover
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
over
X_{\eta}
extends to a finite étale cover of
X
if and only if the monodromy representation
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})\to\mathrm{Aut}(X^{\prime}_{\eta,\bar{b}})
vanishes on
I_{X}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the first statement, which easily implies the second. Let
U\subseteq X
be any non-empty open formal subscheme which contains the geometric point
\bar{b}
(and hence
\bar{b}_{0}
). Then we have a commutative diagram
