As the bottom arrow is surjective by
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0BQI
]
, it suffices to show that the left vertical arrow is surjective. Therefore, we may shrink
X
and assume that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(A)
is affine. In this case,
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})=\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A),\bar{b})
, so that the conclusion follows from
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0BQI
]
again.
∎

Convention 6.25
.

We use
\mathcal{C}(X)
to the denote the set of classical points on the rigid generic fiber of a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ur}}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
denote the subset of points defined over unramified extensions of
K
.

Definition 6.26
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. We say that a morphism
Y\to X
is
semi-étale
if the induced map on generic map
Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is étale, and the map
Y\to X
can be factored as
Y\to U_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\to X
, such that

•

U
is an open sub formal scheme of
X
;

•

K^{\prime}
is a finite extension of
K
;

•

the map
Y\to U_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
is finite;

•

the composition
Y\to U_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
makes
Y
a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
.

The various properties are summarized in the diagram below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\eta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\eta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{finite}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{smooth}}
étale

We shall call the intermediate field extension
K^{\prime}
the
field of definition
for
Y
. If
Y
is connected, then
K^{\prime}
is the compositum of all subalgebras of
\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{Y})
which are finite étale over
K
.

Definition 6.27
.

We say that a subset
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}(X)
is
dense in inertia
if the subgroup generated by
\cup_{x\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{I}_{x}
is dense in the topological group
I_{X}
(i.e.,
\cup_{x\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{I}_{x}
topologically generates
I_{X}
).
We say that
\mathcal{C}
is
effective
if it is semi-étale-locally dense in inertia, i.e., for every semi-étale morphism
Y\to X
, the pullback
\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}}
of
\mathcal{C}
to
Y_{\eta}
is dense in inertia as a subset of
\mathcal{C}(Y)
.

Recall that when
G
is a profinite group, a (normal) subgroup
H\leq G
is dense in
G
if and only if the only open (normal) subgroup of
G
which contains
H
is
G
itself. This immediately implies the following lemma.

Lemma 6.28
.

Let
X
be a connected smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. A subset
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}(X)
is dense in inertia if and only if it has the following property: For every connected finite Galois cover
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, the normalization
X^{\prime}
of
X
in
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
is étale over
X
if and only if
X^{\prime}_{\eta}|_{x}
is unramified over
K(x)
(i.e., extends to a finite étale
\mathcal{O}_{K(x)}
-scheme) for every
x\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Assume that
X
is connected and choose base points
\bar{b},\bar{b}_{0}
as above. Then
X_{\eta}^{\prime}
is defined by an open subgroup
H\leq\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})
. It extends to a connected finite étale cover
X^{\prime}\to X
if and only if there exists an open subgroup
H_{0}\leq\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{k},\bar{b}_{0})
whose pre-image in
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{b})
is
H
. Clearly, this is satisfied if and only if
\mathcal{I}\leq H
, thanks to
Lemma

6.24
.
∎

Theorem 6.29
.

Let
X
be a connected smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
If
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}(X)
is effective, then for every semi-étale morphism
Y\to X
with a primitive inseparable cover
f:Y^{\prime}\to Y
, there exists some
y\in\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}}
such that
f^{-1}(y)
is a single point defined over a totally ramified extension of
K(y)
.
If the reduction of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ur}}
is Zariski dense, the converse is also true.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
is effective and let
Y^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y\to X
be as in the first statement. By assumption
\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}}
is dense in inertia.
By replacing the base point of the fundamental group if necessary, we may choose a lift of
\bar{b}
to
Y
, which we still denote by
\bar{b}
. Now
f_{\eta}
is defined by an index
p
open normal subgroup
H\leq\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(Y_{\eta},\bar{b})
.
We first claim that
I_{Y}
is not contained in
H
.
Otherwise
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}
would extend to a finite étale cover
\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}
of
Y
, and we must have
\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}=Y^{\prime}
, which is not possible.
Note that the fiber
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}|_{y}
, being a Galois étale cover of
y
of degree
p
, is either a single point defined over a degree
p
totally ramified extension of
K(y)
, or unramified over
K(y)
.
In the latter case,
\mathcal{I}_{y}
is contained in
H
. Therefore, if there is no closed point
y
in
\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}}
such that
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}|_{y}
is a single ramified point, then
\cup_{y\in\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}}}\mathcal{I}_{y}
is contained in
H\cap I_{Y}
, which is properly contained in
I_{Y}
—this contradicts our assumption.

Now we prove the converse assuming that the reduction of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ur}}
is Zariski dense in the special fiber.
Note that by
Remark

6.20
we may assume that the special fiber
X_{k}
is geometrically connected.
Let
Y\to X
be a semi-étale morphism and
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}\to Y_{\eta}
be an arbitrary Galois étale cover. Define
Y^{\prime}
to be the normalization of
Y
in
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}
. By
Lemma

6.28
, it suffices to show that if
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}|_{y}
is unramified for every
y\in\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}}
, then
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is étale. Using the assumption that the reduction of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ur}}
is Zariski dense, one quickly infers that
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ur}}|_{Y^{\prime}_{\eta}}
(as a subset of
\mathcal{C}(Y^{\prime})
) has also Zariski dense reduction.
If
K^{\prime}
is the field of definition of
Y
, then points in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ur}}|_{Y^{\prime}_{\eta}}
are by assumption defined over unramified extensions of
K^{\prime}
. Therefore, by
Lemma

6.6
, the special fiber of the
p
-adic formal scheme
Y^{\prime}
over
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
is generically reduced.
After we shrink
X
(or
Y
),
Y^{\prime}
becomes smooth but possibly disconnected, in which case we shall replace
Y^{\prime}
be a connected component and assume that
Y^{\prime}\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{\prime}}
; moreover, applying
Proposition

6.16
, we may further assume that
Y^{\prime}\to Y
admits a factorization
Y^{\prime}=Y_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{m}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\to}}Y_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{0}}}{{\to}}Y
such that
f_{0}
is étale and
f_{j}
is a primitive inseparable cover for every
j>0
. However, here our hypothesis forces
m=0
, i.e.,
Y^{\prime}=Y_{0}
; otherwise by assumption, we can find some
y\in f_{0}^{-1}(\mathcal{C}|_{Y_{\eta}})
such that
f_{1}^{-1}(y)
is ramified over
K(y)
, but then
Y^{\prime}_{\eta}|_{y}
cannot be unramified over
K(y)
.
∎

Corollary 6.30
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. If there exists a Zariski dense subset
\mathcal{C}_{s}
of closed points of
X_{k}
such that
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}(X)
contains all the classical points which specialize to a point in
\mathcal{C}_{s}
, then
\mathcal{C}
is effective. In particular,
\mathcal{C}(X)
itself is effective.

Proof.

This follows directly from
Proposition

6.14
and
Theorem

6.29
.
∎

We immediately infer the
\ell
-adic analogue of
Theorem

1.1
.

Theorem 6.31

(
\ell
-adic pointwise criterion)
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
\ell
be a prime (
\ell=p
is allowed). If an
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-local system
T
on
X_{\eta}
is unramified at all classical points, then
T
is unramified with respect to
X
.

Proof.

We may assume that
X
is connected and use notations in
Construction

6.23
. From
Corollary

6.30
, we know in particular that
\mathcal{C}(X)
itself is dense in inertia. Then we conclude by
Lemma

6.24
.
∎

Remark 6.32
.

In the previous sections, we had taken a more algebraic point of view on
\pi_{1}
.
Suppose that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\in\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K}
.
As mentioned in
Construction

4.18
, the algebraic fundamental group
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\overline{L})
is isomorphic to the Galois group
G_{X_{\eta}}
of the maximal connected pro-finite-étale cover
{\mathrm{Spf}}((\overline{R})^{\wedge}_{p})_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
.
This identification amounts to choosing an embedding
\overline{R}\hookrightarrow\overline{L}
as the base point
and in particular induces a choice of an object
\mathfrak{p}\in T_{X}
, where
T_{X}
is the set of prime ideals of
\overline{R}
above
(\pi)
.
More concretely, the choice of the prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
is equivalent to choosing, for each connected finite étale cover
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, an irreducible component of the reduced special fiber
X^{\prime}_{s}
in a compatible way (where
X^{\prime}
is the normalization of
X
in
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
).
Likewise, one checks that the choice of
\overline{R}\hookrightarrow\overline{L}
also induces an identification
G_{X_{k}}\simeq\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\overline{L})
.

Suppose now
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(V)
is an affine open formal subscheme of
X
.
Then we may choose compatible embeddings
\overline{R}\hookrightarrow\overline{V}
and
\overline{V}\hookrightarrow\overline{L}
whose composition is the chosen
\overline{R}\hookrightarrow\overline{L}
.
Note that
\overline{R}\hookrightarrow\overline{V}
in particular induces a map
T_{U}\to T_{X}
.
It is known that
G_{X_{\eta}}
acts transitively on
T_{X}
, and the natural map
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L}\to G_{X_{\eta}}
surjects onto the stabilizer
G_{X_{\eta}}(\mathfrak{p})
of
\mathfrak{p}
in
G_{X_{\eta}}
(
[
Bri08
, Lem. 3.3.1]
, cf.
[
Shi22
, §6.1]
).
As the same is true for
U
, one quickly checks that
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\overline{L})
is the
normal closure
of the image of
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(U_{\eta},\overline{L})
.
Alternatively, one can see this using the fact that
\mathcal{C}(U)
is an effective subset of
\mathcal{C}(X)
.

We caution the reader that the image of
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(U_{\eta},\overline{L})
is in general not the full
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\overline{L})
. The reason is that the natural map
T_{U}\to T_{X}
is in general not surjective, which happens whenever there exists some connected
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathsf{F\acute{E}t}(X_{\eta})
such that the restriction
U^{\prime}_{\eta}:=U_{\eta}\times_{X_{\eta}}X^{\prime}_{\eta}
is not connected.
To illustrate this, we present below an example when
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is replaced by
\mathbb{C}[\![t]\!]
, but the reader should find no difficulty in finding a similar example over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

Example 6.33
.

Let
E
be a union of three lines on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
over
\mathbb{C}
which intersect transversely at nodes
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3}
, and let
E^{\prime}
be a general (smooth) plane cubic. Suppose that
E
and
E^{\prime}
are defined by
F
and
F^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(3))
respectively.
Set
B:=\mathrm{Proj}(\mathbb{C}[t,s])
and consider the pencil
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{2}\times B
defined by
tF+sF^{\prime}
, so that
E=\mathcal{E}_{b}
for
b=[1:0]
.
Let
\widehat{B}
be the formal complection of
B
at
b
, with (rigid) generic point
\eta
. Set
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}:=\mathcal{E}\times_{B}\widehat{B}
, i.e., the formal completion of
\mathcal{E}
along
E=\mathcal{E}_{b}
. One checks that
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}\to\widehat{B}
has semi-stable reduction. In particular,
\mathcal{E}_{\eta}
is smooth over
\eta
.

Pick a point
\infty\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
and a line
L\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{2}
in general positions. Then projection from
\infty
to
L
defines a finite morphism
\psi:\widehat{\mathcal{E}}\to\widehat{L}:=L\times\widehat{B}
of degree
3
.
There exist formal open subschemes
X\subseteq\widehat{L}
and
X^{\prime}\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{E}}
such that
\psi
restricts to an étale cover
X^{\prime}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, and
X_{b}
contains
Q_{i}
’s. By standard Bertini-type arguments, one finds an open formal subscheme
U\subseteq X
an open formal subscheme such that
U_{b}
does not contain the images of
Q_{i}
’s.
Then
X^{\prime}_{\eta}
is connected, whereas
U_{\eta}^{\prime}:=U\times_{X}X^{\prime}_{\eta}
is not. In fact,
U_{\eta}
has
3
connected components.

7.
Pointwise criteria

In this section, we prove the pointwise criteria for the crystallinity and the semi-stability.
Namely, for a smooth rigid space
X_{\eta}
that has good (resp. semi-stable) reduction, and a
p
-adic local system over
X_{\eta}
, the property of being crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to a given semi-stable model can be checked by its restrictions on an effective subset
\mathcal{C}
of classical points in
X_{\eta}
.

Throughout the section, we use
K
to denote a discretely valued
p
-adic field with perfect residue field
k
as before.
We also recall that for a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we use
X_{s}
to denote the reduced special fiber
(X_{k})_{\mathrm{red}}
, and use
X_{k}
to denote the mod
\pi
fiber.

7.1.
Statement and first reductions

In the following, we recall that by a
classical point
of a rigid space
X_{\eta}
, we mean a point
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
that locally defined by a maximal ideal of
A
, where
{\mathrm{Spa}}(A)\subset X_{\eta}
is an affinoid neighborhood of
x_{\eta}
.
Moreover, given such a classical point, we use
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{x_{\eta}}
to denote the absolute Galois group of the residue field
K(x_{\eta})
of
X_{\eta}
at
x_{\eta}
, where
K(x_{\eta})
is a finite extension of
K
.
As explained in
Proposition

6.1
, if
X_{\eta}
admits an integral model
X
which is flat and topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, then
x_{\eta}
also admits an integral model
x\in X(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})
.

To begin, we extend the notion of the effective set to the semi-stable reduction.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
X
be a semi-stable formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. We say that a subset
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}(X)
is effective if the restriction of
\mathcal{C}
to the smooth locus of
X
is effective.

Now we can state the exact form of the statement we want to prove

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
X
be a smooth (resp. semi-stable)
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
\mathcal{C}\subset X_{\eta}
be an effective subset of classical points.
Then a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
X
if and only if the restriction
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) for each
x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

By
Corollary

5.4
, it suffices to prove the “if” statement.
First we notice that thanks to the recent purity result for semi-stable local systems in
[
DLMS24
, Thm. 1.1]
, since the set of Shilov points is contained in the good reduction locus, it suffices to assume
X
is smooth, affine, and connected, with an unramified model
R_{0}
as in
Convention

3.11
.
By
Theorem

5.20
.
(v)
, we only need to show that the associated weak
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
) is of rank
d\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{rank\,}T
everywhere.
Here we note that by the purity result again, we are allowed to shrink
X
further by an open formal subscheme whenever needed.
In particular, we may assume the weak
F
-isocrystals above are actual
F
-isocrystals.
Moreover, since for any finite unramified extension of the base field
K^{\prime}/K
the canonical morphism
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\to X
is finite étale,
Theorem

5.5
.
(iii)
allows us to replace
X
by a connected component of
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
.
Therefore, we may assume that
X_{s}
is geometrically connected.

Following the convention and notation as in
Section

6.1
, we let
\mathcal{O}_{L}
be the complete discrete valuation ring with imperfect residue field, defined by the
p
-complete localization of
R
at the ideal
\pi R
, with
L=L(X)
being the fraction field of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
.
There is then a natural map of
p
-adic formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\to X
that induces a map of adic spaces
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)\to X_{\eta}
, identifying
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
as the Shilov point of
X_{\eta}
(
[
BH22
, Prop. 2.2]
).

Next we note that since
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is a crystalline and hence de Rham representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{x_{\eta}}
for some
x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}
, by Liu–Zhu’s rigidity theorem of de Rham local systems (
[
LZ17
]
; cf.
Corollary

4.11
), we know that
T
is a de Rham local system over
X_{\eta}
.
In particular, the restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
corresponds to a de Rham representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L}
, which by the work of Morita (
[
Mor14
]
) and Ohkubo (
[
Ohk13
]
) is potentially semi-stable.
Namely, there is a finite Galois extension
L^{\prime\prime}/L
such that the restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L^{\prime\prime})}
corresponds to a semi-stable representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{L^{\prime\prime}}
.

Now thanks to
Proposition

6.16
and
Theorem

6.17
, by shrinking
X
and replacing
L^{\prime\prime}
by a further finite extension if necessary, there exists a map of
p
-adic formal schemes
X^{\prime\prime}\to X
which factors as a composite

X^{\prime\prime}=X_{m}\xrightarrow{g_{m}}\cdots\xrightarrow{g_{2}}X_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}X_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{0}}X^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\to X,

such that

•

the extension
L\hookrightarrow L^{\prime\prime}
is identified with
L(X)\hookrightarrow L(X^{\prime\prime})
;

•

K^{\prime}
is a finite Galois extension of
K
and
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\to X
is the canonical map given by extension of scalars;

•

g_{0}
is finite étale, and for each
i>0
, the map
g_{i}:X_{i}\to X_{i-1}
is a primitive inseparable cover between objects in
\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{\prime}}
(cf.
Definition

6.12
).

Let us finish the proof of the pointwise criterion for crystalline (resp. semi-stable) local systems.
By the purity of semi-stable local systems in
[
DLMS24
, Thm. 1.1]
, we know the preimage
T|_{X^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}}
is semi-stable with respect to
X^{\prime\prime}
.
Then by iteratively applying
Theorem

6.29
and
Theorem

7.6
below to each map
g_{i}
for
i\geq 1
, we deduce that
T|_{X_{0,\eta}}
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable).
In addition, by
Theorem

5.20
.
(iii)
, we know
T|_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable).
So it is left to descend the crystallinity (resp. the semi-stability) from
T|_{X^{\prime}_{\eta}}
to
T
.
For the latter, we notice that
\cap_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}K(x_{\eta})
is a finite unramified extension of
K
: if not, then the image
\cup_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{I}_{x_{\eta}}
of the inertial subgroup of points along the surjection
I_{X}\to I_{K}
would be contained in a proper open subgroup, contradicting our assumption that
\cup_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{I}_{x_{\eta}}
topologically generate
I_{X}
.
Therefore, we may conclude by
Theorem

7.7
.∎

7.2.
Descending crystallinity and semi-stability

We prove the two descent results
Theorem

7.6
and
Theorem

7.7
on the crystallinity and the semi-stability used in the previous subsection.

We start with a preparation about the Galois action on the associated
F
-isocrystal.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
K
be a
p
-adic field, and let
Z
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Assume
g:Z^{\prime}\to Z
is a map satisfying either of the following two assumptions:

(a)

It is a primitive inseparable cover between two smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

(b)

There is a finite Galois and totally ramified extension
K^{\prime}/K
such that
Z^{\prime}=Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}
and
g
is the extension map.

In either case, the generic fiber
g_{\eta}:Z^{\prime}_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}
is a finite Galois cover and we denote by
H
the Galois group.
Let
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Z_{\eta})
, and let
T^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{\eta}^{-1}T
be the preimage.

(i)

There is a natural
H
-action on the
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
(resp.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z^{\prime}_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
) that is functorial in
T
.

(ii)

In case
(b)
, assume that
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
is an unramified model of
Z
(and hence
Z^{\prime}
) as in
Convention

3.11
.
Then the
H
-action in
(i)
is compatible with the
H
-action on
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T^{\prime})
and
D_{{\mathrm{st}},R_{0}}(T^{\prime})
via
Theorem

5.20
.
(ii)
.
Moreover,
D_{*,R_{0}}(T)=D_{*,R_{0}}(T^{\prime})^{H}
for
*\in\{{\mathrm{st}},{\mathrm{crys}}\}
.

We remark that the common feature of assumptions
(a)
and
(b)
is that
g_{s}
is a homeomorphism, so that it induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{s})\simeq\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{s})
by
[
Ogu84
, Thm. 4.6]
.
The natural
H
-action on
D_{*,R_{0}}(T^{\prime})
will be explained in the proof.

Proof.

We first notice that for any element
h\in H={\mathrm{Gal}}(Z^{\prime}_{\eta}/Z_{\eta})
, we have a natural identity of maps
h_{\eta}\circ g_{\eta}=g_{\eta}
.
Since the local system
T^{\prime}
is defined as the preimage, we have the following isomorphisms as a part of the descent data:

\sigma_{h,\eta}:h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}T^{\prime}.

So by applying the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor, we get an isomorphism of
F
-isocrystals in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(X^{\prime}_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
that is compatible with the group structure on
H

\sigma_{h,s}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},\sigma_{h,\eta}}\colon\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}.

On the other hand, by
Lemma

6.10
(iv)
, the Galois group
H={\mathrm{Gal}}(Z^{\prime}_{\eta}/Z_{\eta})
naturally acts on
Z^{\prime}
in the category of
p
-adic formal schemes over
Z
.
So by identifying the elements
h\in H
with the automorphisms
h:Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}
and by the base change thoerem of the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor in
Theorem

5.20
.
(iii)
, we get an isomorphism of
F
-isocrystals in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
:

h_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}}.

Notice that by assumption the map of the reduced special fiber
g_{s}:Z^{\prime}_{s}\to Z_{s}
is purely inseparable or equal to the identity.
Hence the induced action of
H
on
Z^{\prime}_{s}
is trivial, and we have identifications
h_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}=\mathrm{id}_{Z^{\prime}_{s}}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}
.
As a consequence,
\sigma_{h,s}
a natural action of
H
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
:

(56)

\sigma_{h,s}:\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}.

Now we focus on the setup of
(ii)
. We note that by taking the evaluation at the pro-PD-thickening
(R_{0},R_{k})
, the above induces an action of
H
on
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T^{\prime})
.
To see the
H
-action above is compatible with the
H
-action on
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T^{\prime})
via
Theorem

5.20
.
(ii)
, we recall the
H
-action on the latter.
For simplicity, we assume
Z={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is the base change of of
Z_{0}={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
.
As in
Construction

4.18
, we let
\tilde{Z}={\mathrm{Spf}}(S)
be the integral perfectoid formal scheme over
R_{0}
defined by the maximal connected pro-finite-étale cover of
Z^{\prime}_{\eta}
(and hence of
Z_{\eta}
).
Then we have

D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(T(\tilde{Z}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{Z}_{\eta})\bigr)^{G_{Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}},

where the
H
-action is induced by the action of
G_{Z_{\eta}}
on
T(\tilde{Z}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(\tilde{Z}_{\eta})
.
So the compatibility of the two
H
-action follows from the translation between the Galois action and the descent data of pro-étale sheaves.
The claim that
D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T)=D_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(T^{\prime})^{H}
is clear.

Finally, for
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
, the proof works the same and we do not repeat it here.
∎

Another preparation is about the descent of
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-vector bundles.
In the following, for a given smooth rigid space
Z_{\eta}
, we let
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}}
be the horizontal period sheaf
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
over the proétale site
Z_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
introduced in
Section

3
.

Definition 7.4
.

Let
g:Z^{\prime}_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}
be a finite étale cover of smooth affinoid rigid spaces over
K
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
be a
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}
-vector bundle.
Then a
descent datum
for
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with respect to
Z_{\eta}
is an isomorphism
\epsilon:p_{1,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}p_{2,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, where
p_{i}:Z^{\prime}_{\eta}\times_{Z_{\eta}}Z^{\prime}_{\eta}\to Z^{\prime}_{\eta}
are the two projections, such that
\epsilon
satisfies the cocycle condition.

(2)

Define
\mathrm{Des}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}})_{Z_{\eta}}
to be the category whose objects are the pairs
(\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\epsilon)
where
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Vect}}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}})
and
\epsilon
is a descent datum on
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with respect to
Z_{\eta}
, and whose morphisms are defined in the natural way.

Note that in the special case when
Z^{\prime}_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}
is a finite Galois cover with the Galois group
H
, a descent datum can be translated as isomorphisms of
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}
-vector bundles

\sigma_{h}\colon h_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\simeq\mathcal{M}^{\prime},~\forall h\in H,

that are compatible with the group structure on
H
(cf.
[
Sta23
,
Tag 0CDQ
]
).

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
g:Z^{\prime}_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}
be a finite étale cover of smooth rigid spaces over
K
.
The pullback functor

g_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}:{\mathrm{Vect}}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}})\to\mathrm{Des}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}})_{Z_{\eta}}

is fully faithful.

Proof.

By considering the internal Homs, we reduce the problem to showing that if
\mathcal{M}\in\mathrm{Vect}(\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}})
,
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=g_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}\mathcal{M}
and
\epsilon
is the canonical descent datum, then an element
m^{\prime}\in\Gamma(Z^{\prime}_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
comes from
\Gamma(Z_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},\mathcal{M})
if and only if
\epsilon(p_{1,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(m^{\prime}))=p_{2,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(m^{\prime})
.

Let
\{U_{\alpha}={\mathrm{Spa}}(R_{\alpha},R^{+}_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha}
be an affinoid perfectoid cover of
Z_{\eta}
in
Z_{\eta,\text{pro\'{e}t}}
which trivializes
\mathcal{M}
.
Let
U^{\prime}_{\alpha}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}U_{\alpha}
and let
m^{\prime}_{\alpha}
be the restriction of
m^{\prime}
to
U^{\prime}_{\alpha}
.
Now using the assumption that the map
g
itself is finite étale, we see
\{U^{\prime}_{\alpha}\}
is also a set of affinoid perfectoid objects in
Z_{\eta,\text{pro\'{e}t}}
that covers
\{U_{\alpha}\}
(cf.
[
Sch13a
, Lem. 4.5(i)]
).
So by the sheaf property of
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}}
on
Z_{\eta,\text{pro\'{e}t}}
, for each index
\alpha
, the sequence

(57)

0\to{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R_{\alpha},R_{\alpha}^{+})}\to{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R^{\prime}_{\alpha},R^{\prime+}_{\alpha})}\rightrightarrows{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R^{\prime}_{\alpha}\widehat{\otimes}_{R_{\alpha}}R^{\prime}_{\alpha},(R^{\prime}_{\alpha}\widehat{\otimes}_{R_{\alpha}}R^{\prime}_{\alpha})^{+})}

is an equalizer diagram.
By the local freeness of
\mathcal{M}
over
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
, the sequence remains exact after we tensor with
\mathcal{M}
.
This implies that

(58)

0\to{\mathcal{M}(U_{\alpha})}\to{\mathcal{M}(U^{\prime}_{\alpha})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{M}(U^{\prime}_{\alpha}\times_{U_{\alpha}}U^{\prime}_{\alpha})}

is also an equalizer diagram.

Now, assume the element
m^{\prime}
satisfies the condition
\epsilon(p_{1,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(m^{\prime}))=p_{2,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(m^{\prime})
.
Then the element
m^{\prime}_{\alpha}
lies in the equalizer of the double arrows, and in particular is the image of some
m_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}(U_{\alpha})
.
Conversely, if we start with an element
m_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{M}(U_{\alpha})
, then by the short exact sequence again, we see its image
m^{\prime}_{\alpha}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}m_{\alpha}
satisfies the equation
\epsilon(p_{1,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(m^{\prime}_{\alpha}))=p_{2,\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}}^{*}(m^{\prime}_{\alpha})
.
Combining the observations above, we see the functor from
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}}
-locally free sheaves to the category of descent data over
Z^{\prime}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is fully faithful when restricted to each
U_{\alpha}
.
Finally, since the gluing of
m^{\prime}_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha
can be checked on
\{U^{\prime}_{\alpha}\}
, the claim for
m
follows from the sheaf property of
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and the above naturally globalizes to entire
Z_{\eta}
.
∎

Assembling the above preparations, we now prove the first descent result.

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
K
be a
p
-adic field, and let
g:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be a primitive inseparable cover between connected smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Z_{\eta})
, and let
T^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{\eta}^{-1}T
be the preimage.
Assume we have

•

the local system
T^{\prime}
is semi-stable with respect to
Z^{\prime}
;

•

there is a classical point
x_{\eta}\in Z_{\eta}
such that
g_{\eta}^{-1}(x_{\eta})
is one point that is ramified over
x_{\eta}
, and the restriction
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is a crystalline (resp. semi-stable) representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x_{\eta})}
.

Then the local system
T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
Z
.

Proof.

As the statement is local with respect to the Zariski topology of
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
, we assume that both
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
are affine and there is an unramified model
Z_{0}^{\prime}
of
Z^{\prime}
with a lift of Frobenius
\varphi_{Z_{0}^{\prime}}
as in
Convention

3.11
.
We separate the proof into two steps. In step 1, we show that the natural Galois action on the
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is trivial. In step 2, we show that
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
descends to an object in
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
and the descent is associated with the original local system
T
.

Step 1:

Assume the local system
T
is of rank
d
.
By
Lemma

7.3
, there is a natural action of the Galois group
H={\mathrm{Gal}}(Z^{\prime}_{\eta}/Z_{\eta})
on the log
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z^{\prime}_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
.
We first claim that the
H
action is trivial.

To see this, we let
x:{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})\to Z
be the point of the
p
-adic formal scheme
Z
whose generic fiber is
x_{\eta}
.
We let
y_{\eta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{\eta}^{-1}(x_{\eta})
be the preimage of
x_{\eta}
, which by assumption is a single point with
K(y_{\eta})
a degree
p
ramified Galois extension of
K(x_{\eta})
.
In particular the Galois group of the extension
K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta})
is the same as
H
.
We let
y\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(y_{\eta})})\to Z^{\prime}
be the induced map of
p
-adic formal schemes.
Then by construction, the
H
-action on the generic fiber naturally induces an action on
y
, and we get an
H
-equivariant map of
p
-adic formal schemes
y\rightarrow Z^{\prime}
that is compatible with
x\rightarrow Z
and the generic fibers, namely the commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(y_{\eta})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\text{-equivariant}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}

Now we apply
Theorem

5.20
.
(v)
, to get an
H
-equivariant isomorphism

(59)

y_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},y_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}},

where the latter is an
F
-isocrystal over
(k,M_{k})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
.
Moreover by
Theorem

5.20
.
(ii)
, the right hand side of

59
can be identified with the usual
K_{0}
-vector space
D_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{y_{\eta}})
with Frobenius and nilpotent operators, where
V_{y_{\eta}}=y_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}({\mathrm{Spa}}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C}))
is the
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(y_{\eta})}
-representation over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
that corresponds to the local system
y_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}
.
Here by the commutative diagram above, the local system
y_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}
is equal to the restriction of
T|_{x_{\eta}}
along the finite Galois cover
y_{\eta}\to x_{\eta}
.
On the other hand, by the assumption that the restriction
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is semi-stable, for each
h\in H
, the action of
h
on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{y_{\eta}})
is trivial.
By
Equation

59
, the isomorphism
\sigma_{h,s}:\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is an endomorphism of an the log
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
whose pullback along
y_{s}:{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)\to Z^{\prime}_{s}
is the identity map.
This is only possible when the map
\sigma_{h,s}
itself is the identity. To see this, we can translate
\sigma_{h,s}
to a Frobenius and monodromy equivariant isomorphism of flat connections over
Z^{\prime}_{0,\eta}
such that its pullback to a point is the identity (
Lemma

2.27
).
Since the category of flat connections over
Z^{\prime}_{0,\eta}
is abelian, both flat connections
\ker(\mathrm{id}-\sigma_{h,s})
and
\mathrm{coker}(\mathrm{id}-\sigma_{h,s})
vanish when restricted to a point, and thus vanish themselves.
Hence the automorphism
\sigma_{h,s}
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
(and hence on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}
) is equal to the identity, for every
h\in H
.
18
18

18

Here the triviality of the action
\sigma_{h,s}
can also be deduced from
[
Ogu84
, Thm 4.1]
.

Suppose now that the restriction
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is crystalline. Then the monodromy operator on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{y_{\eta}})
acts as zero.
Notice that the monodromy operator commutes with the flat connection under the identification in
Corollary

2.26
, and is compatible with the pullback isomorphism in
Equation

59
.
So by the fact that the category of flat connections over
Z^{\prime}_{0,\eta}
is abelian and using the same idea last paragraph, we see the monodromy operator on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime}_{0},Z^{\prime}_{k},(0{N})^{a})
is trivial as well.
Therefore,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is the image of
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}
under the morphism
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}})\to\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
induced by the natural forgetful functor
(Z_{k},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}\to Z_{k,{\mathrm{crys}}}
, so that
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})\simeq\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}})
(cf.
Remark

4.2
).

Step 2:

We then claim that both
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
descend to
Z^{\prime}_{s}
: since
Z^{\prime}_{s}\to Z_{s}
is a purely inseparable map of varieties in characteristic
p
, we know from
[
Ogu84
, Thm. 4.6]

19
19

19

More precisely, the self fiber products
(Z^{\prime}_{s})^{n}
of
Z^{\prime}_{s}
over
Z_{s}
are infinitesimal extensions of
Z^{\prime}_{s}
, and in particular the pullback functor along
(Z^{\prime}_{s})^{n}\to Z^{\prime}_{s}
induces an equivalence on
F
-isocrystals (
[
Ogu84
, Rmk. 2.7.2]
).
Hence the category of
F
-isocrystals of the Čech nerve of the proper surjective map
Z^{\prime}_{s}\to Z_{s}
is equivalent to the constant category
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
.

that the pullback functor induces an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z^{\prime}_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}}).

Moreover, thanks to
Corollary

2.26
, we have an analogous equivalence

\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z^{\prime}_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}).

As a consequence, we obtain
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(Z_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}})
and
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\in\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}((Z_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}})
together with isomorphisms
f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
and
f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
, which are unique up to unique isomorphism.
Here we equip
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
and
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
with trivial
H
-actions.

We then show that the
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
in
(Z_{s},(0{N})^{a})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is in fact naturally associated to the local system
T
, which by
Definition

4.4
will imply that the local system
T
is semi-stable with respect to
Z
.
We do this by descending the isomorphism of vector bundles over
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}

\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}^{\nabla=0,N=0}:\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}

to an isomorphism
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}}(\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T})\simeq T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}}
of vector bundles over
\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z_{\eta}}
.
By
Lemma

7.5
, this amounts to comparing the following two descent data:

•

The descent data
\delta_{h,\eta}:h_{\eta}^{*}\bigr(T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}
induced by
f_{\eta}^{-1}T\simeq T^{\prime}
.

•

The descent data

\delta_{h,s}:h_{\eta}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})
induced by
f_{s}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
.

More precisely, what we need to show is the commutativity of the following diagram:

(60)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h_{\eta}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h_{\eta}^{*}\bigr(T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}\bigr)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\eta}^{*}\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{h,s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{h,\eta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}

To begin, we remind the reader that

and

provide us with the following identifications

\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(h_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})\simeq h_{\eta}^{*}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})\textrm{ and }h_{\eta}^{-1}(T^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}\simeq h_{\eta}^{*}(T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}),

which we shall use implicitly for the content below.

Now, let us describe the two sets of descent data. First we shall temporarily suppress the assumption that
f_{s}
is purely inseparable and clarify some tautology. Let
\sigma_{h,\eta}:h_{\eta}^{*}T^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}T^{\prime}
be the descent data given by
f_{\eta}^{-1}(T)\simeq T^{\prime}
.
Then
\delta_{h,\eta}
is nothing but
\sigma_{h,\eta}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}
.
Similarly, let
\nu_{h,s}:h_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
be the descent data given by

.
Then

is characterized as the unique isomorphism such that the following diagram commutes (cf.

):

(61)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(h_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h_{\eta}^{-1}(T^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\eta}^{*}\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\nu_{h,s})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{h,s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}

Recall that
\sigma_{h,s}=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},\sigma_{h,\eta}}:\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is the map induced by the functoriality of the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor. The functoriality of the injection
\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
defined in
Theorem

4.12
.
(ii)
gives us a commutative diagram (cf.

4.21
)

(62)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{{\mathrm{st}},h_{\eta}^{-1}T^{\prime}}^{\nabla=0,N=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\sigma_{h,s})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{h,\eta}\otimes{\mathbb{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}},Z^{\prime}_{\eta}}}}

By comparing diagrams (

) and (

), as well as the diagram in

) is equivalent to the equality
\sigma_{h,s}=\nu_{h,s}
.

Finally, we make use of the assumption that

is purely inseparable, which forces

and
\nu_{h,s}
to be the identity maps, and turns the collection

into an

-action on

.
Therefore, we reduce to showing that the

-action on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is trivial, which is precisely what we showed in Step 1. Now we have shown that
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}
is associated to

, so that

is semi-stable. If we additionally assume that

is crystalline for some

, then by the last paragraph of Step 1,

is associated to

and hence

is crystalline.
∎

We then consider the another descent result along an extension of the base ring.

Theorem 7.7
.

Let
K^{\prime}/K
be a finite Galois and totally ramified extension of
p
-adic field, let
Z
be a connected smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
g:Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}}\to Z
be the extension map.
Let
T\in{\mathrm{Loc}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Z_{\eta})
, and let
T^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}g_{\eta}^{-1}T
be the preimage.
Assume we have

(a)

the local system
T^{\prime}
is semi-stable with respect to
Z^{\prime}
;

(b)

there exists a subset
\mathcal{C}
of classical points in
Z_{\eta}
such that the intersection
\cap_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}K(x_{\eta})
is an unramified extension over
K
and the restriction
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is a crystalline (resp. semi-stable) representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x_{\eta})}
.

Then the local system
T
is crystalline (resp. semi-stable) with respect to
Z
.

Proof.

We let
H
be the Galois group
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)
, and assume
T
is of rank
d
.
Apply
Lemma

7.3
under the assumption
(b)
in loc. cit..
Then we know that for both
*\in\{{\mathrm{crys}},{\mathrm{st}}\}
, there is a natural
H
-action on
\mathcal{E}_{*,T^{\prime}}
, and
\mathcal{E}_{*,T}
is the
H
-invariant part
\mathcal{E}_{*,T^{\prime}}^{H}
of
\mathcal{E}_{*,T^{\prime}}
. The latter statement can be checked Zariski-locally on
Z
, and follows from
Lemma

7.3
.
(ii)
.

We first show that
T
is semi-stable, for which we may assume that
Z
is affine. Using
Theorem

4.17
and
Lemma

7.3
.
(ii)
, we reduce to showing that the action of
H
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is trivial.
As before, we think of
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
as an F-isocrystal on
Z_{s}=Z^{\prime}_{s}
with a nil endomorphism
N
.
Recall that we are allowed to replace
K
by a finite unramified extension, so that we may assume that
\cap_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}K(x_{\eta})=K
, or equivalently,
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
is generated by
\cup_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x_{\eta})}
.
By
Lemma

6.18
, for each closed point
y_{\eta}\in g_{\eta}^{-1}(x_{\eta})
,
K(y_{\eta})
is a finite Galois extension of
K(x_{\eta}
) and
K^{\prime}\otimes_{K}K(x_{\eta})
is the product of such
K(y_{\eta})
’s.
Note that an element in
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta}))
naturally restricts to one in
H={\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)
. One quickly deduces that

(63)

\textrm{$H$ is generated by }\bigcup_{x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}}\bigcup_{y_{\eta}\in g^{-1}_{\eta}(x_{\eta})}\mathrm{im}({\mathrm{Gal}}(K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta}))\to H).

Now, for each
x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}
and
y_{\eta}\in g^{-1}_{\eta}(x_{\eta})
, the assumption that
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is a semi-stable representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x_{\eta})}
implies that the residual
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta}))
-action on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(T|_{y_{\eta}})
is trivial. As
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
is already semi-stale, the base change theorem
Theorem

5.20
.
(iii)
implies that the image of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K(y_{\eta})/K(x_{\eta}))
in
H
acts trivially on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T^{\prime}}
. Hence the conclusion follows from (
63
).

Finally, we need to argue that if in assumption
(b)
we additionally assume that
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is crystalline for all
x_{\eta}\in\mathcal{C}
, then
T
is moreover crystalline. Just as in the proof
Theorem

7.6
, this amounts to showing that
N=0
, which one may check by restricting to any point in
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

Example 7.8
.

The example in
Remark

6.15
gives a prototypical counterexample for the naive analogue of Liu-Zhu’s rigidity theorem, when the base is a smooth scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Namely, consider
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\left<t^{\pm}\right>)
and the endomorphism
f
on
X
defined by
t\mapsto t^{p}
. Then we consider the local system
T
given by
f_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Note that for each classical point
x_{\eta}
on
X_{\eta}
,
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is crystalline, or equivalently, semi-stable
20
20

20

It is a coincidence when the inertia group acts through a finite quotient. See Prop. 7.17 of Fontaine and Ouyang’s notes available at
www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~fontaine/galoisrep.pdf
, if and only if
f_{\eta}^{-1}(x_{\eta})
is a union of
p
distinct point each defined over
K(x_{\eta})
.
One can check by hand that (a) for every closed point
x_{s}\in X_{s}
,
T
is crystalline or semi-stale at some classical point
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
which specializes to
x_{s}
; and (b) for every
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
where
T
is crystalline or semi-stable,
T
remains crystalline or semi-stable at any
x^{\prime}_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
which is sufficiently
p
-adically close to
x_{\eta}
. Nonetheless,
T
is neither crystalline nor semi-stable.

7.3.
Relative semi-stable comparison

Finally, we prove the relative semi-stable comparison theorems in
Section

1.2
.
For the reader’s convenience, we recall the statement below.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
X
be a semi-stable
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with the standard log structure
M_{X}
, let
(Y,M_{Y})
be a fine and saturated
p
-adic formal log scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
f:(Y,M_{Y})\to(X,M_{X})
be a proper and log smooth morphism with Cartier type mod
\pi
reduction, and let
f_{\eta}
be the induced morphism between generic fibers.

Assume one of the following conditions is true.

(a)

The map
f
is algebrizable.

(b)

The underlying map between formal schemes of
f
is pointwise weakly semi-stable.

Then the higher direct image
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a semi-stable local system over
X_{\eta}
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

1.1
, it suffices to show that
T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a
p
-adic local system and for each classical point
x_{\eta}\in X_{\eta}
,
T|_{x_{\eta}}
is semi-stable. Let
x\in X(\mathcal{O}_{K(x_{\eta})})
be its integral extension and let
\overline{x}_{\eta}
be a geometric point over
x_{\eta}
.

First, we note that under assumption
(b)
, since
M_{Y}|_{Y_{\eta}}
is trivial,
Y_{\eta}
is proper and smooth over
X_{\eta}
and
T
is the usual etale cohomology
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. In particular, by
[
SW20
, Thm. 10.5.1]
and the proper base change theorem
[
Hub96
, Thm. 4.1.1]
,
T
is a local system and
T|_{x_{\eta}}
can be identified with the
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x_{\eta})}
-representation
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(Y_{\overline{x}_{\eta}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Then by
[
ČK19
, Thm. 9.5]
,
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(Y_{\overline{x}_{\eta}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is semi-stable.

The above argument applies in situation
(a)
as well with a little modification.
As the map
f
is assumed to be algebraic, we may apply
[
Nak17
, Thm. 13.1]
to deduce that
T
is indeed a local system.
Then, the proper base change theorem in the logarithmic setting (
[
Nak97
, Thm. 5.1]
) allows us to identify
T|_{x_{\eta}}
with the
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K(x_{\eta})}
-representation
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}((Y,M_{Y})_{\overline{x}_{\eta}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
As the log structure on
x_{\eta}
is trivial, the assumption that
(Y_{x_{\eta}},M_{Y}|_{Y_{x_{\eta}}})
being log smooth implies that
Y_{x_{\eta}}
is smooth over
x_{\eta}
and the log structure
M_{Y}|_{Y_{x_{\eta}}}
is given by a normal crossing divisor (cf.
[
DLLZ23
, Cor. 3.3.11]
).
Then, by Cor. 6.3.4 in
loc. cit.
, there is a canonial isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}((Y_{\overline{x}_{\eta}},M_{Y}|_{Y_{\overline{x}_{\eta}}}),\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(U_{\overline{x}_{\eta}},\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

where
U_{\overline{x}_{\eta}}
is the open subvariety of
Y_{\overline{x}_{\eta}}
on which the log structure is trivial (or equivalently, the open complement of the normal crossing divisor).
Finally,
[
CN17
, Cor. 5.15]
tells us that the right hand side is semi-stable.
∎

Remark 7.10
.

We clarify how condition
(b)
above is related to log structures of the fiber at a point.
Denote
K(x_{\eta})
by
K^{\prime}
and let
\pi^{\prime}
be its uniformizer.
Then the morphism
x:{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})\to X
necessarily extends to a (not necessarily exact) closed immersion
({\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}),(\pi^{\prime}){N})\to(X,M_{X})
.
By
[
Tsu19
, Prop. II.2.14]
, the special fiber
f_{k}
is saturated. In particular, the fibers of morphism between the underlying shcemes of
f_{k}
are reduced by
[
Tsu19
, Thm. II.4.2]
.
As the latter statement is also true for the generic fiber
f_{\eta}
, by
[
Tsu19
, Thm. II.4.2]
again the map
f
is saturated.
So thanks to
[
Tsu19
, Prop. II.2.13]
, the base change
(Y_{x},M_{Y_{x}}):=x^{*}(Y,M_{Y})
is saturated.
Now, note that
(Y_{x},M_{Y_{x}})
is regular in the sense of Kato. Indeed, by
[
Kat94
, Thm. 8.2]
, the fact that
(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}},(\pi^{\prime}){N})
is regular implies that
(Y_{x},M_{Y_{x}})
is also regular.
Then we know from
[
Kat94
, Thm. 11.6]
that
M_{Y_{x}}
is necessarily the standard log structure.

Before proving the association statement, we give a little lemma.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
S
be a smooth and connected rigid space over
K
. Let
M,N
be two vector bundles with flat connections on
S
. Let
K^{\prime}
be a finite totally ramified extension of
K
and set
S^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}S\otimes_{K}K^{\prime}
. Let
\alpha:M|_{S^{\prime}}\to N|_{S^{\prime}}
be a morphism between flat vector bundles. Let
F
be a finite unramified extension and set
F^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}F\otimes_{K}K^{\prime}
.

Suppose that for
s\in S(F)
and
s^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}s_{F^{\prime}}
, the fiber
\alpha_{s^{\prime}}:M_{s^{\prime}}\to N_{s^{\prime}}
descends to a morphism
M_{s}\to N_{s}
. Then
\alpha
descends to a morphism
M\to N
.

Proof.

To prove the statement we may replace
K^{\prime}
by its normal closure over
K
. By Galois descent, we reduce to showing that
\sigma\alpha\sigma^{-1}=\alpha
for all
\sigma\in{\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)
. Note that
\sigma\alpha\sigma^{-1}
is also a morphism between vector bundles with flat connections. Since a totally ramified extension and an unramified extension of
K
are linearly disjoint,
F^{\prime}
is a field and
{\mathrm{Gal}}(F^{\prime}/F)={\mathrm{Gal}}(K^{\prime}/K)
. The hypothesis implies that
\sigma\alpha\sigma^{-1}-\alpha
vanishes at
s^{\prime}
, and hence everywhere, thanks to
[
Ogu84
, Thm. 4.1]
.
∎

Now we prove the association statement
Theorem

1.8
in a more precise form.

Theorem 7.12
.

In the setting of
Theorem

7.9
.
(b)
,
assume
X
is in addition smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
f_{s}:(Y_{s},M_{Y_{s}})\to(X_{s},M_{X_{s}})
be the reduced special fiber of
f
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism of
F
-isocrystals on
(X_{s},M_{X_{s}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}

(64)

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{st}},T}\simeq R^{i}f_{s,{\mathrm{crys}}*}\mathcal{O}_{(Y_{s},M_{Y_{s}})/W}[1/p]

such that, when evaluated on the divided power thickeing
(X,X_{p=0})
, there is a commutative diagram of flat connections over
X_{\eta}

(65)

Below we give some remarks on the technical conditions in
Theorem

7.9
and
Theorem

7.12
(i.e.,
Theorem

1.6
and
Theorem

1.8
respectively).

Remark 7.13
.

(a)

The strategy of proving
Theorem

7.9
is to combine our
Theorem

1.1
with the semi-stable comparison theorems in the absolute setting, where we use
[
CN17
]
for
Theorem

7.9
.
(a)
and
[
ČK19
]
for
Theorem

7.9
.
(b)
.
In particular, the technical constraints in
Theorem

7.9
originate from the requirement for algebraicity in
[
CN17
, Cor. 5.15]
, and that for weak semi-stability in
[
ČK19
, Thm. 9.5]
respectively.

We also remark that, using the prismatic methods, one can extend the strategy of the first named author and Reinecke in
[
GR24
]
to remove the algebraicity assumption in
Theorem

7.9
.
(a)
.
This can achieved for example by combining the main results of
[
KY23
]

21
21

21

We thank Koshikawa for kindly drawing our attention to their work.

with
[
DLMS24
]
. On the other hand, using the pointwise criteria, one can show that
R^{i}f_{\eta,{\mathrm{k}{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is semi-stable as long as the generic fiber
f_{\eta}
is proper and log smooth, with
each fiber
satisfying either of the assumptions in
Theorem

7.9
.

(b)

We assume the setup of
Theorem

7.9
.
(b)
for
Theorem

7.12
, only because our proof of the latter requires certain compatibility with Scholze’s de Rham comparison isomorphism
[
Sch13a
]
, which in the absolute setting was explicitly checked in
[
ČK19
, Thm. 9.5]
.

(c)

Inspired by work of Kedlaya
[
Ked07
, Thm. 6.4.5]
, we find it plausible that when
X
is semi-stable, the restriction functor from the category of F-isocrystals on
(X_{k},M_{X}|_{X_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
(with suitable convergence properties) to those on
(X^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k},M_{X}|_{X^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}})_{{\mathrm{log}\textrm{-}\mathrm{crys}}}
is fully faithful, where
X^{\mathrm{sm}}_{k}
is the maximal smooth locus of
X_{k}
. If we assume this claim, then for
Theorem

7.12
the case of a semi-stable base follows rapidly from that of a smooth base.

Finally, we explain an example about the family of stable curves.
Recall that
\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
) is the moduli stack over
\mathbb{Z}
of
n
-marked smooth (resp. stable) curves of genus
g
, i.e.,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
is the Deligne-Mumford compactification
\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}\smallsetminus\mathcal{M}_{g,n}
be the boundary.

Proposition 7.14
.

Let
S
be a semi-stable
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Suppose that
f:C\to S
is a stable curve given by a morphism
\gamma:S\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
such that

•

the special fiber
\gamma_{k}
factors through the boundary
\mathcal{D}_{k}
;

•

the generic fiber
\gamma_{\eta}
factors through the analytification of the interior
\mathcal{M}_{g,n,K}
.

Let
\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}:S\to C
be the marked points, let
C^{\circ}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}C\smallsetminus\cup_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}(S)
be the open complement, and let
f^{\circ}:C^{\circ}\to S
be restriction of
f
on
C^{\circ}
.

Then
R^{1}f^{\circ}_{\eta,{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a semi-stable local system on
S_{\eta}
.

Proof.

We equip
C\to S
with the basic log structure
(C,M^{\mathrm{bas}}_{C})\to(S,M^{\mathrm{bas}}_{S})
, as is characterized in
[
Kat00
, Prop. 2.3]
.
It can be checked from the constructions in
loc. cit.
that this family is proper, log smooth, and its mod
\pi
reduction is of Carteier type.

Let
(X,D)
be the
p
-completion of
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n},\mathcal{D})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Let
M_{X}
be the log structure on
X
given by
D
.
By
[
Kat00
, Thm. 4.5]
, the universal curve over
X
can be equipped with the structure of a log stable curve, which is in particular proper and log smooth; moreover,
\gamma^{*}M_{X}
is nothing but
M_{S}^{\mathrm{bas}}
.

Recall that locally
M_{X}
is given by sections of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
which are units on the complement
X\smallsetminus D
. Similarly,
M_{S}
on
S
is given by sections of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
which are units on
S_{\eta}
. Since
\gamma_{\eta}^{-1}D
is supported on
S_{k}
by assumption, we know the image of
\gamma^{*}M_{X}
in
\mathcal{O}_{S}
is contained in that of
M_{S}
.
Hence we obtain a map of log pairs
(S,M_{S})\to(S,\gamma^{*}M_{X})
.

Finally, by pulling back
(C,M^{\mathrm{bas}}_{C})
along
(S,M_{S})\to(S,\gamma^{*}M_{X}=M^{\mathrm{bas}}_{S})
, we obtain a proper, log smooth, and algebraic morphism
(C,M_{C})\to(S,M_{S})
which has Cartier type mod
\pi
reduction
22
22

22

This can also be seen by applying
[
Tsu19
, Thm. 4.2]
—it is part of the definition of log stable curves that the geometric fibers of the underlying morphism between schemes are reduced.
. So the claim follows by applying
Theorem

7.9
.
(a)
.
∎

Below we also mention that one can easily construct a family of stable curves
\gamma
as above that satisfies the assumptions in
Proposition

7.14
.

Example 7.15
.

Let
X
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
D\subseteq X
be a relative normal crossing divisor that is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
M_{X}
be the log structure on
X
induced by
\mathcal{D}
.
We claim that for every point
z\in D_{s}
, there is an open neighborhood
U_{s}
of
z
in
D_{s}
such that
U_{s}
lifts to a semi-stable
p
-adic formal scheme
U\subseteq X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
that satisfies the property
U_{\eta}\subseteq X_{\eta}\smallsetminus D_{\eta}
.

The question is étale local, so without loss of generality we may assume that
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is an open formal subscheme of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{1},\cdots,x_{r},x_{r+1}^{\pm},\cdots,x_{n}^{\pm}\rangle)
,
D=V(x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{r})\subseteq X
, and
z=(0,\cdots,0,1,\cdots,1)
.
Then we can simply take
U_{s}=D_{s}
and
U=V(x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{r}-\pi)
.
